But against royal subjects who demolish boundaries: fifty marks, with the restoration thereof at the expense of the leaseholder of the royal estates, according to the intent of the constitution of the year 1578.

The penalty against a commissioner absent from the act of the commission, ^[*A penalty of 100 marks for a commissioner who is absent.*] after having signed the summons letters, is one hundred marks — saving the lawful impediment of illness.

* * *

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-24">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-24">p. 24</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

##### *Granicies ex Moderamine Magnifici Succamerarij, reprobatis Ductibus Partium, præcluso Juramento terminantur*

Quando contigerit Magnifico Succamerario, reprobare Ductum Sententia sua, Parti utrique; tam Actori, quàm Citato, uti nullis realibus Documentis ac Scopulis & Signis granicialibus ac Inquisitione probanti, non nisi ex re oculis subjecta, situatione Loci, considerato rectilinio, vel Limite naturali ex moderamine Judicij sui, dictante conscientia, Ductum Partibus præcluso Juramento, ne Perjuria fiant, assignabit, & Granicies determinabit.

Quando etiam in Termino Condescensionis ex ^[*Remissa ad Tribunal in Casu Discordantia.*] Decreto Tribunalitio, bini Magnifici Succamerarij, vel eorum Officiales, ratione Adjudicationis Ductûs, in Sententijs discordes fuerint, tunc ante Sipationem Scopulorum, remittent Causam rursus ad Judicium Tribunalis Regni, pro cognitione, & resolutione Sententiarum suarum.

#### PRÆSCRIPTIO
##### *Possesionis Præscriptio animadvertenda est, & fines ostendendi.*

^[*Herburt Fol. 333.*] Ex consuetudine, & Jure antiquo servabatur: quod vendens Hereditatem, Emptorem per tres ^[*Emptor Villæ trigennio & trimestri expleto præscribit agere*]

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

##### *Boundaries Determined by the Moderation of the Right Honourable Sub-Chamberlain, the Parties' Boundary Paths Having Been Rejected, with the Oath Barred.*

When it shall happen that the Right Honourable Sub-Chamberlain rejects by his sentence the boundary path of both parties — both plaintiff and respondent — as proved by no real documents, mounds, boundary markers, or sworn inquiry, but only from the thing presented to the eyes, the position of the place, the straight line considered, and the natural boundary — by the moderation of his court and in accordance with his conscience, he shall assign the boundary path to the parties with the oath barred, so that perjuries may not occur, and shall determine the boundaries.

When also at the session of the field hearing pursuant ^[*Referral to the Tribunal in case of disagreement.*] to a Tribunal decree, two Right Honourable Sub-Chamberlains, or their officials, shall be in disagreement in their sentences as to the adjudication of the boundary path, then before the erection of the mounds they shall refer the case back to the Crown Tribunal court for the cognisance and resolution of their sentences.

#### PRESCRIPTION
##### *The prescription of possession is to be noted, and boundaries are to be shown.*

^[*Herburt, fol. 333.*] By custom and ancient law it was observed: that the seller of an estate was bound to protect the buyer for three ^[*The buyer of a village prescribes the right to bring an action after the lapse of three years and three months.*]

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-25">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-25">p. 25</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

annos, & tres menses, ab impetitione quacunque; ad intervenientem & liberandum eundem sit adstrictus. Sed quia multoties contingit: quod vicini illius venditæ hereditatis, quorum Limites etiam ipsam contingunt, infra præfatos tres annos & tres menses, Emptorem vexare postponunt: donec Venditor & Intercessor Præscriptione emptionis evadat: qua elapsa; primò Emptorem pro Limitibus inquietare non formidant: licet venditioni præsentes exstiterint: ea propter volumus & statuimus: quod cum aliqua Hereditas ^[*Præscriptio ad Triennii Granicierum cum Novo Possessore Bonorum.*] Emptori, per Venditorem ostenditur, vicini illius Hereditatis, per Emptorem cum Ministeriali evocentur: quod illi ostensioni intersint. Qui si venire noluerint, sic per Ministerialem vocati; & infra tres annos & tres menses Possesori quæstionem pro Limitibus non moverint tunc Possessor, ad probationem suæ Emptionis cum testibus admittatur. Præscriptioneque; tanti Temporis juvabitur.

#### DELIBERATIO.
##### *Semel Judex deliberet, & semel Partes controvertant.*

^[*Herburt Folio 139.*] Statuimus: ut non nisi semel Judex Terrestris, vel Castrensis, vel Succamerarius deliberet: similiter & Capitaneus & Vice Capitaneus, semel etiam, ad duas septimanas, in Causa Officij Castrensis, ad alios Terminos Castrenses deliberet: sub pæna trium

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

years and three months from any claim by intervention and discharge. But because it often happens that the neighbours of the sold estate, whose boundaries likewise touch upon it, postpone troubling the buyer within the said three years and three months — until the seller and intercessor escapes through the prescription of the sale; after which has elapsed, they do not scruple first to disturb the buyer over the boundaries, even though they were present at the sale — for this reason we wish and enact: that when any estate ^[*Prescription of three years in respect of boundaries with the new possessor of the estates.*] is shown to the buyer by the seller, the neighbours of that estate shall be summoned by the buyer with the court messenger, to be present at that showing. If they shall have been unwilling to come, summoned thus by the court messenger, and within three years and three months shall not have raised a question of boundaries against the possessor, then the possessor shall be admitted to proof of his purchase with witnesses, and shall be assisted by the prescription of so long a time.

#### DELIBERATION.
##### *The judge deliberates once, and the parties contest once.*

^[*Herburt, fol. 139.*] We enact: that not more than once shall a land judge, a municipal judge, or a Sub-Chamberlain deliberate; and likewise a Captain and Vice-Captain, likewise once, for two weeks in a case of the municipal court office, shall deliberate until the next municipal sessions: under a penalty of three

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-26">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-26">p. 26</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

Marcarum pecuniæ, parti succumbenda: pro qua Capitaneus tenebitur justitiam Parti, vel coram Nobis, vel coram Judicio Terrestri: alias, ubi Parti libuerit, pænam ipsam persequi. Post quam deliberationem Judicis, Capitanei vel ejus Officij, jam nulla Pars poterit suam Controversiam meliorare, vel locupletare: tantùm Decretum audiet, ex illa Controversia, super quam deliberatum est. Sed quia Statuto novo cautum exstat: quia ante probationem Sententiæ, hoc est, ante Decretum, potest Pars suam Controversiam, vel Causam meliorare: hoc ita debet intelligi: quia hæc ipsa melioratio Controversiæ debet fieri semel, in Termino peremptorio: hoc est, tunc quando jam Judex recipit sibi ad deliberandum, seu ad interrogandum, namsi Pars deberet Causam suam aliquoties meliorare: Judex vero itidem super eadem aliquoties deliberare: tunc magna Justitiæ Comperendinatio fieret, & admitteretur.

##### *Decretum Deliberationis Judicij, seu Officij Succamerarialis.*

Actum in Loco Campestri Differentiarum, inter Hereditates Villarum N. Generosi N. N. Actoris ab una, & Generosi N. N. principaliter Citati, parte ab altera, circa tres Scopulos angulares notabiles, (seu non admodum notabiles) unum Villæ N. prædicti Actoris, alterum Villæ N. præfati principaliter

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

marks of money to be paid to the prevailing party; for which penalty the Captain shall be obliged to do justice to the party, either before Us or before the land court, or otherwise wherever it shall please the party to pursue that penalty. After which deliberation of the judge, captain, or his office, no party shall be able to improve or supplement its contested claim; it shall only hear the decree arising from that contested matter upon which deliberation was had. But because provision has been made in the new statute that, before the proof of the sentence — that is, before the decree — a party may improve its contested claim or case: this is to be understood as follows: that this same improvement of the contested matter must be made once, at the peremptory session, that is, at the time when the judge is already receiving the matter for deliberation, or for examination; for if a party were required to improve its case several times, and the judge were likewise required to deliberate upon the same several times, then a great delay of justice would arise and be permitted.

---

**Translator's Note**

[T.N. 1] *Odsypisko*: a Polish legal term for land deposited by the natural action of a river on one bank at the expense of the other. It denotes the accreted land itself, and the legal rule that such natural accretion belongs to the heir of the bank where it is deposited. The term has no established single-word English equivalent and is retained in italics.

the third of the village of [name] of the Honourable [names], collaterally summoned, standing by a paved public road running from the city of [name] to the town of [name], on the Monday next after the Sunday of [name], in the year of the Lord [names].

In Prosecutione Causæ, quoniam in Causa præsenti, variæ dubietates, & perplexitates, intercedunt, id circò insistendo Legi positivæ, Judicium Succamerariale ne per præcipitantiam sui aliqua Pars in fortuna patiatur detrimentum, (quoniam Causa præsens Granicierum vim perpetuitatis sapit) deliberat ad octo septimanas, conservando Partibus eo in loco Terminum comparendi Peremptorium, insimul cum Collaterali adcitato. Actus præsentis vigore.

year by the court messenger of [name] and drawn from those same records — he therefore considers that the decree both of the land court and of his own office has been satisfied, and enjoins the parties to prosecute the case. Bonorum Villæ N. Herede per Generosum NN. Actis Castrensibus N. Feria N. ##### Decretum ex Conservatione Termini concessæ Dilationis Majoris.

A Cum in Loco campestri Differentiarum, inter Hereditates Villarum N. Generosi NN. Actoris ab una, & Generosi NN. principaliter citati Parte ab altera, circa tres Scopulos angulares notabiles (seu non admodum notabiles) unum Villæ N. prædicti Actoris, alterum Villæ N. præfati principaliter citati,

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

heir of the estates of the village of [name], through the Honourable [names], his attorney registered in the municipal records of [name] on the [day] of [name] in the present year, appearing before his court and judicially contesting the matter between themselves; the contested submissions having been heard, at the application of the collaterally summoned party, the Honourable [names], or his attorney — since a boundary case cannot be decided without the judicial acknowledgement of the collateral heir, nor can a corner point be established — therefore adhering both to the statute of the Kingdom and to the constitution of the year 1611 enacted on postponements, grants and concedes the principal postponement of eight weeks, in which session the party shall be required to prove, by a document or attestation of the Illustrious Right Honourable Marshal of the Crown Tribunal, under penalty of loss of the case. With the Honourable [names], originally cited, he suspends the present case for judgment until that same session of eight weeks, preserving for the parties the peremptory session of appearance by virtue of the present decree.

##### Decree on Preservation of the Session of the Granted Principal Postponement.

Actum in loco Campestri Differentiarum, inter Hereditates Villarum N. Generosi NN. Actoris ab una, & Generosi NN principaliter citati parte ab altera, circa tres Scopulos angulares notabiles (seu non admodum notabiles) unum Villæ N.

At which place and field session, before his Sub-Chamberlain's court, the third party shall be required to confirm by oath in that oath formula: that at that time, when the present session was postponed for him, he was ill, with an illness truly permitted by God, so help him GOD and the Holy Passion of JESUS Christ. Reprotestatur Generosus N. Actor, quod Judicium suum rite & legitime rectilinio, a Termino a Quo, per Quem, & ad Quem, duxit & determinavit, in fundamento Granicierum anni supra exarati judicio suo reproductarum.

Finito & determinato Ductu Partis actoreæ, ad ^[*Ductus Partis Citatæ.*] revisionem Ductûs Partis citatæ judicium Succamerariale accessit. Tandem vergente sole ad occasum, Actum præsentem ad diem crastinum, Ministeriali Regni suprascripto, Judicij sui Apparitori reclamare demandavit. Tandem die crastino, videlicet feria tertia, post idem Festum N. anno præsenti, reasumpta Jurisdictione sui, ad Comparitionem Partium omnium, ad continuationem Ductus Partis citatæ processit; quo in Termino ex acclamatione Ministerialis, Pars citata demonstravit judicio suo tres monticulos, non procul a se distantes, in Borra densa, vario genere arborum excreta, penes viam publicam de Oppido N. ad Oppidum N. per Villam N. tendentem, & ibidem concurrentiam trinarum Hereditatum, allegavit esse, non verò in eo loco, a quo Pars Actorea Ductum suum incepit; quo in Loco &

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

not in a straight line, proceeding obliquely; and further regarding the unjust demonstration of the allegedly ploughed-up wall mounds in the field called *Długie Pole*, which field from antiquity belongs entirely to his hereditary village of [name], which he offers to prove by sworn inquiry. The Honourable [names], plaintiff, counter-protests that his court duly and legitimately led and determined the boundary path in a straight line from the starting point, through the intermediate points, to the end point, on the basis of the boundaries of the year written above, reproduced before his court.

The plaintiff's party's boundary path having been concluded and determined, the Sub-Chamberlain's court ^[*The cited party's boundary path.*] proceeded to the review of the cited party's boundary path. The sun declining toward the west, he ordered the present act to be adjourned to the morrow by the above-named court messenger, the apparitor of his court. On the following day, namely the Tuesday after the same feast of [name] in the present year, the jurisdiction having been reassumed and called by the court messenger, proceeding to the review: the resolution of the corner point and the particular corner point being suspended, the court proceeded first to the review of the cited party's boundary path. At which session, on the call of the court messenger, the cited party demonstrated to his court three hillocks standing not far away, in a dense pine forest grown with various kinds of trees, beside a public road running from the town of [name] to the town of [name] through the village of [name], and there alleged the convergence of three estates to be, and not at the place from which the plaintiff's party commenced its boundary path; at which place and

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-42">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-42">p. 42</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

Termino ad acclamationem Ministerialis, personaliter comparens Generosus NN. collateraliter adcitatus, veram in eo loco Angularitatem situatam esse Judicialiter, Parti citatæ agnovit, & firmiter allegavit. Protestatur Pars Actorea, nempe Generosus N. tam contra Partem originaliter citatam, quam Collateralem, respectu indebitæ tam remonstrationis, ac si Scopulorum angularium, quam agnitionis per Collateralem eorundem, uti nullis documentis probantium, circa quam Protestationem coram suo judicio factam, petijt Pars Actorea, referationem eorundem Scopulorum angularium, cujus affectationi annuendo, eosdem Scopulos in præsentia Partium omnium referare demandavit; in quibus referatis, nil inventum, a quibus Scopulis, seu Monticulis a septemtrione ad meridiem, progrediendo Pars citata, demonstravit judicio, monticulum Scopuli, allegando eundem verum esse Scopulum parietalem, octuaginta quinque passibus a Scopulis angularibus referatis distantem, cujus referationem eadem Pars citata ducens affectavit. Affectationi ejusdem Partis annuendo, eundem Scopulum referavit, in quo referato, nil repertum est, non nisi rupes, alias terra conglutinata ad instar lateris, contra quem Scopulum protestatus est Generosus NN. Actor, de illegali ejusdem monticuli, pro Scopulo parietali sumpto, demonstratione. Et quia hæc Borra, ab antiquo ad Hereditatem ejus Villæ

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

session, at the call of the court messenger, the Honourable [names], collaterally summoned, appearing in person, judicially acknowledged to the cited party that the true corner point was situated at that place, and firmly alleged it. The plaintiff's party, namely the Honourable [names], protests against both the originally cited party and the collateral heir, regarding the improper demonstration of allegedly corner mounds and the acknowledgement thereof by the collateral heir, as proved by no documents; in connection with which protest made before his court, the plaintiff's party requested the opening of those corner mounds, granting the application therefor, he ordered those same mounds to be opened in the presence of all parties; in which, when opened, nothing was found. From those mounds or hillocks, proceeding from the north toward the south, the cited party demonstrated to the court a hillock resembling a mound, alleging it to be the true first wall mound, standing eighty-five paces from the opened corner mounds, under the very forest called *Nart*, whose opening the same cited leading party requested. Granting the application of that same party, he opened the same mound, in which, when opened, nothing was found save rock, that is, earth congealed in the manner of brick; against which mound the Honourable [names], plaintiff, protested regarding the unlawful demonstration of that hillock as a wall mound. And because that pine forest from antiquity belongs to his estate of the village of [name].

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-43">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-43">p. 43</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

spectat. Ducente ulterius Parte citata ad plagam meridionalem, demonstravit judicio, Signum graniciale in arbore Pinatica, ad instar crucis excisum, & non procul ab eo loco, ferme uno stadio, alterum Signum, primo simile in arbore Fagina excisum. Contra quam demonstrationem Signorum ac si granicialium protestatus est Generosus N. Actor, reproducta Visione officiosa per Ministerialem & duos Nobiles, eo in loco peracta, & coram Actis Castrensibus feria N. Anno N. recognita, earundem incisionum in officiose privata authoritate, per antecedaneum Heredem Villæ N. excisorum. Ulteriori tractu & ductu Pars citata ad meridiem proficiscendo, per Sylvam, devenit ad vias transversales, unam de Oppido N. ad Oppidum N. publicam, alteram de Villa N. ad Villam N. tendentes, circa quas vias, demonstravit Scopulum verisimilem, cui Pars actorea inficiando, referationem expetijt, quo referato, inventi sunt carbones, in minori quantitate, quos Pars actorea allegabat, e radicibus combustis, stante conflagratione sylvæ ejusdem esse. Ulterius continuando Ductum suum Pars citata, ad fluviolum seu rivulum ex sylvis ab occidente, versus orientem profluentem, quem transcendendo, devenit ad locum sublimorem, & Argulam Fruticetosam, & per eandem transeundo rectilinio, medio stadio ab eadem Argula, ostendit Judicio Monticulum, eundem, pro Scopulo

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

The cited party leading further toward the southern quarter, demonstrated to the court a boundary marker cut in the manner of a cross in a pine tree, and not far from that place, about one furlong away, another marker, similar to the first, cut in a beech tree. Against which demonstration of allegedly boundary markers the Honourable [names], plaintiff, protested, reproducing an official inspection carried out by the court messenger and two noblemen at that place, acknowledged before the municipal records on the [day] of [name] in the year [name], of those same incisions cut unofficially by private authority by the former heir of the village of [name]. Proceeding further and leading southward through the forest, the cited party reached transverse roads — one public road from the town of [name] to the town of [name], another running from the village of [name] to the village of [name] — beside which roads he demonstrated a credible mound; the plaintiff's party denying it, requested its opening; in which, when opened, charcoal was found in smaller quantity, which the plaintiff's party alleged was from roots burned at the time of a fire in that same forest. The cited party continuing its boundary path further, toward a small river or stream flowing from the forests from the west toward the east; crossing it, reached a higher place and a bushy alder thicket, and passing through it in a straight line, half a furlong from that alder thicket, showed the court a hillock, asserting it to be a mound;

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-44">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-44">p. 44</a></h2>

asserendo, Judicium, propter meliorem informationem Judicij sui, eundem referare demandavit, in quo referato, nil inventum est; Tandem ulteriorem Ductum continuando, & se se declinando oblique, ad Partem sinistram, versus plagam inter meridiem & orientem hiemalem, Judicium adduxit, ad meditullium camporum, ubi allegabat Scopulos esse disaratos, per supra nominatum Generosam Actorem, quam allegationem nullâ contestatione Litis probat. Protestante Generoso N. Actore, contra Partem ducentem, de indebita Judicij sui ductione, per proprios Fundos & Campos, nulli Controversiæ ab ævo subjacentes, tum ratione conculcationis frumentorum Ductu suo, & quia is Fundus & Campi, ab antiquo sunt Curiales, ad Villam & Hereditatem sui Protestantis, spectantes; ulterius eodem Ductu per Campos progrediendo, in hanc, & in aliam partem, Pars ducens se se divertendo, devenit ad eam Angularitatem, & tres Scopulos angulares, seu aciales, ubi Pars originaliter actorea, Ductum suum finivit, eo etiam in loco, & eadem citata Pars Ductum suum determinavit. Protestatur Pars Actorea de nullitate, & invaliditate, totius Ductus, uti non a Termino a Quo, & per Quem, uti summam obliquitatem in se continentis, nec ullis documentis toto tractu probati. Reprotestante Parte citata, quod rite & legitime Judicium Succamerariale duxit, tum respectu indebite

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

the court, for better information to itself, ordered that same mound to be opened; in which, when opened, nothing was found. Finally, continuing its further boundary path and inclining obliquely to the left toward the quarter between the south and the winter east, the court was led to the middle of the fields where it alleged the mounds had been ploughed up by the above-named Honourable [names], plaintiff — which allegation it proves by no contestation of the case. The Honourable [names], plaintiff, protesting against the leading party regarding the improper conduct of his court through his own plots and fields, subject to no dispute since antiquity, and also regarding the trampling of grain by its boundary path, and because those plots and fields have from antiquity been manorial, belonging to the village and estate of the protestant himself; continuing further through the fields by the same boundary path, diverting itself this way and that, the leading party reached that corner point and those three corner or particular corner mounds where the originally plaintiff's party had concluded its boundary path; there also the cited party determined its boundary path. The plaintiff's party protests the nullity and invalidity of the entire boundary path, as not having been conducted from the starting point through the intermediate points, containing as it does the greatest obliqueness, and proved by no documents throughout its course. The cited party counter-protesting that it led the Sub-Chamberlain's court duly and legitimately, and also regarding the wrongfully

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-45">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-45">p. 45</a></h2>

objecti condictaminis, cum Collaterali Generoso N. qui nullo condictamine cum Protestante processit, non nisi secundum notitiam, a senibus Incolis Villæ suæ acceptam, Judicio in concurrentia trinarum Hereditatum, Acilitatem suam & Determinium Parietis Judicio Succamerariali demonstravit, & exinde sibi Protestanti, Scopulum Angularem, agnovit.

Quibus finitis Ductibus Partium utrarumque; ac bene, & diligenter revisis, ad sufficientiorem probationem Ductuum, inquisitionem per Partes tam principaliter agentes, quam Collateralem, Generosam NN. respectu ejusdem Acialitatis expediendam censet, injungendo, quatenus eædem Partes, præevijs conscribendis interrogatorijs & sibi ad invicem Pars, Partis, communicandis, expediant, idque; in instanti sub Succubitione in Causa, & super incorruptionem testium, juramentum præstare demandat, additur Ministerialis ad pronunciandam juramenti rotham, idque; instantanee. Quod Juramentum, si quidem Partes, tam principaliter agentes, quam Collateralis Generosus NN. rothisante sibi Ministeriali, in facie Judicij sui, præstiterunt, proinde ad expediendam eandem Inquisitionem, primo Partis actoreæ, ac post Partis citatæ, Judicium accessit; tandem declinante sole ad occasum Actum suum ad diem crastinam prorogavit & limitavit; & eundem Ministeriali suprafato, Judicij

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

alleged collusion with the collateral heir, the Honourable [names], who proceeded with no collusion with the protestant, but only according to the knowledge received from elderly residents of his village, demonstrated to the Sub-Chamberlain's court his particular corner point and the determination of the boundary wall at the convergence of three estates, and from that point acknowledged to the protestant the corner mound.

The boundary paths of both parties having been concluded and carefully and diligently reviewed, for more sufficient proof of the boundary paths the court considers it fitting that a sworn inquiry be conducted by both the principal acting parties and the collateral heir, the Honourable [names], regarding that same particular corner point; enjoining that the said parties, having first drawn up interrogatories and communicated them to one another, party to party, shall conduct it immediately under penalty of loss of the case; and orders the parties to swear an oath on the impartiality of the witnesses; the court messenger is added for the pronouncing of the oath formula, and this immediately. Which oath, since the parties — both the principal acting parties and the collateral heir, the Honourable [names] — with the court messenger reading the formula to them, swore before his court; he therefore proceeded to conduct the same sworn inquiry, first for the plaintiff's party and then for the cited party; then, the sun declining toward the west, he extended and limited his act to the morrow, and ordered the court messenger aforesaid,

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-46">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-46">p. 46</a></h2>

sui apparitori reclamare injunxit. Tandem die crastina adveniente, videlicet feria NN. expeditis Inquisitionibus, Partium tam principaliter agentium, tam respectu Angularitatis, quam totius universalis Ductûs. Jam verò per Collateralem de sola acialitate ejus, & concurrentia trinarum Hereditatum, in eo loco, ubi Parti originaliter citatæ agnoverat. Judicium Succamerariale, post expeditas easdem Inquisitiones, & introducto Negotio: ad lectionem earundem condescendit; quibus lectis & per omnes circumstantias perpensis, & trutinatis, siquidem Judicio, tam ijsdem Inquisitionibus, quam & Documentis Partis actoreæ, nempe Graniciebus Anni N. ex Actis Succamerarialibus depromptis, eandem Angularitatem, per partem actoream demonstratam, circa viam publicam stratam, ad Oppidum N. ex Civitate N. tendentem, veram & legitimam esse, proinde adjudicato eodem Loco, pro erigendis Scopulis Angularibus, & Aciali Generoso N. Collateraliter adcitato, probabiliori Ductu Partis actoreæ, uti Scopulis & signis granicialibus, in eisdem repertis. Rectilinio, a septemtrione, a Termino a Quo, per Quem, & ad Terminum ad Quem, per Generosam NN. collateraliter adcitatum judicialiter agnitum, & secum cum Parte citata, in eodem concordantem, ad Plagam meridionalem situatum. Jam verò Partis citatæ, reprobato, uti obliquitatem in se continenti, & nullis documentis

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

apparitor of his court, to adjourn the same. The following day arriving, namely the [day] of [names], the sworn inquiries of the parties — both principal acting parties regarding both the corner point and the entire general boundary path, and that of the collateral heir regarding his particular corner point alone and the convergence of three estates at the place where he had acknowledged it to the originally cited party — having been concluded. The Sub-Chamberlain's court, after the completion of those same sworn inquiries and the introduction of the matter, descended to the reading thereof; which having been read and weighed and examined in all their circumstances — since the court finds, both from those same sworn inquiries and from the documentary evidence of the plaintiff's party, namely the boundaries of the year [name] drawn from the Sub-Chamberlain's records, that the same corner point demonstrated by the plaintiff's party, beside the paved public road running from the city of [name] to the town of [name], is true and legitimate; adjudging therefore that same place for the erection of corner mounds and the particular corner mound for the Honourable [names], collaterally summoned, and the more probable boundary path of the plaintiff's party, as supported by mounds and boundary markers found therein; conducted in a straight line from the north, from the starting point, through the intermediate points, to the end point situated toward the southern quarter, judicially acknowledged by the Honourable [names], collaterally summoned, and agreeing with the cited party in the same. And the cited party's boundary path having been rejected as containing obliqueness and confirmed by no documentary

</div>
</section>

literatorijs, ac Inquisitionibus firmato, nulli Parti favendo, sed solum DEUM præ oculis habendo, ad erectionem, & sipationem Scopulorum primo angularium, ac post parietalium, Judicium accessit. Movit Pars tam principaliter citata, quam collateralis, ad Judicia Tribunalitia Regni Petricoviensia, non admissa, Protestatur Pars de gravamine, Judicium, cum Parte actorea, reprotestatur, quod nullum gravamen, tam Parti citatæ, quam collaterali, per adjudicationem Ductûs, visis literatorijs Documentis, & dictante conscientia sui, probabiliorem Ductum Parti actoreæ adinvenit, & eidem adjudicavit, & iterum ad sipationem Scopulorum angularium processit, imprimis unum Scopulum angularem Parti actoreæ, videlicet Hereditati Villæ N. ad orientem, alterum Parti citatæ & Hereditati ejus Villæ N. ad occidentem, utrosque Scopulos ex una Parte viæ stratæ de Oppido N. ad Villam N. tendentis, tertium ex altera Parte Viæ ad septemtrionem Hereditati Villæ N. collaterali, in æquali distantia quinque; cubitorum usualium situatos, sipavit & de terra in altum erexit, & in quemlibet, signa metallia, videlicet: carbones, lateres, ac dimoscorium ferreum, cum lagena vitrea millio impleta, cum imposita in eandem charta papyrea, Descriptione Actus hodierni, per quem sunt Sipati Scopuli, ut posteritati pateat, in casu alicujus Differentiæ

</div>

evidence or sworn inquiries — favouring no party but having only GOD before its eyes — the court proceeded to the erection and raising of the mounds, first the corner mounds and then the wall mounds. The cited party — both the principal respondent and the collateral heir — moved to the Crown Tribunal courts of the Kingdom at Piotrków, not admitted. The party protests the grievance; the court, together with the plaintiff's party, counter-protests that no grievance was done to either the cited party or the collateral heir through the adjudication of the boundary path — having examined the documentary evidence and in accordance with its conscience, it found the plaintiff's party's boundary path to be more probable and adjudged it to that party — and again proceeded to the erection of the corner mounds; first raising one corner mound for the plaintiff's party, namely for the estate of the village of [name], toward the east, another for the cited party and its estate of the village of [name], toward the west — both mounds on one side of the paved road running from the town of [name] to the village of [name] — and a third on the other side of the road toward the north for the collateral estate of the village of [name]; all situated at an equal distance of five ordinary cubits; raised and built up from earth; and in each, metal markers, namely charcoal, brick, and iron slag, together with a glass bottle filled with millet, with a paper sheet placed inside it bearing a description of today's act by which the mounds were raised, so that it may be clear to posterity in the event of any difference

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-48">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-48">p. 48</a></h2>

exoriundæ. Tandem ab eisdem angularibus Scopulis, ad meridiem eodem Ductu Partis actoreæ progrediendo, sipavit primum Scopulum parietalem, in eo loco, ubi referatio antiqui Scopuli ex mandato Judicij sui subsecuta est, & noviter de Terra erexit, octuaginta passibus ab Angularibus distantem, & ulterius ab eodem Scopulo parietali, quantò rectius, per Borram densam, vario arborum genere excretam, ad Plagam meridionalem eundo, sipavit Scopulum alterum, primo similem in æquali distantia, octuaginta passibus, a primo erectum, & ab eodem ulteriori Ductu, eadem Borra progrediendo, sipavit Scopulum tertium, in loco sublimori, secus locum lutosum, plus minus quatuor, vel quinque; stadiorum exstantem, quæ loca lutosa transeundo, ex altera parte eorundem, medio stadio, videlicet: quinquaginta passibus, ab eodem Loco lutoso, in loco elevatiori, recta lineâ ad meridiem, sipatus est Scopulus, in numero Quartus, deinde ulterius ad Meridiem, eadem Borra gressus suos dirigendo, devenit ad Fluviolum, seu Torrentem, ab occasû, versus orientem, profluentem, in cujus Ripa in loco elevatiori, sipatus est Scopulus parietalis, in numero quintus, a quarto octuaginta passibus distans, quem transgrediendo, devenit Judicium ad viam publicam de Oppido N. ad Villam N. tendentem, non transcendendo eandem viam, imo penes ipsam est

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

arising. Then proceeding from those same corner mounds toward the south along the same boundary path of the plaintiff's party, he raised the first wall mound at the place where the opening of the ancient mound was carried out by order of his court, and raised it anew from earth, eighty paces from the corner mounds; and proceeding further from that wall mound as straight as possible through the dense pine forest grown with various kinds of trees toward the southern quarter, he raised a second mound, similar to the first, at an equal distance, eighty paces from the first; and from the same, proceeding further along the same path through the same pine forest, he raised a third mound in a more elevated place, beside a boggy place standing at more or less four or five furlongs; crossing those boggy places, on the other side of them, half a furlong away, namely fifty paces from that boggy place, in a more elevated place, in a straight line toward the south, a mound was raised, the fourth in number; then proceeding further toward the south, directing his steps through the same pine forest, he reached a small river or torrent flowing from the west toward the east, on whose bank in a more elevated place a wall mound was raised, the fifth in number, eighty paces from the fourth; crossing it, the court reached a public road running from the town of [name] to the village of [name], not crossing that road — indeed beside it is

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-49">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-49">p. 49</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

Scopulus, in numero Sextus centum passibus a quinto distans. Transcensa eadem via, rectilinio ad meridiem, exeundo ex eadem Borra, sipatus est Scopulus sub ipsa Borra in confinio agrorum *Dlugie Pole* vocitorum, ad Hereditatem Villæ N. Actoris, ab ævo spectantium, in numero Septimus, à sexto octuaginta passibus distans, ab eodemque; septimo Scopulo ad meridiem, quantò rectius, per eosdem agros & campos proficiscendo, fossam aratro ad id comparato, erigere, injunxit, usque; ad aciales Scopulos adtingentem, quos ad affectationem Partium, tam principaliter agentium, quam Collateralis, uti nulli Controversiæ subjectos renovare, & in altum erigere, demandavit, & signis metallicis tam parietales omnes, quam & aciales, carbonibus, latere, & dimoscorio ferreo, in quemlibet eorum, distinctim impositis, obsignavit. Post quam sipationem Scopulorum tam angularium, seu acialium, quam parietalium. Judicium Succamerariale, decernit, quatenus Pars Ductum lucrans actorea, comprobet Met Septimus ^[*Injuncta Comprobatio Ductûs.*] genere sibi similibus, adhærendo Statuto Regni, in ultimo Scopulo suo Aciali, in eam juramenti Rotham, prout Ductu suo, nihil Terræ & Hereditatis Citato admit, nec ab illius Hereditate alienavit, idque; instantanee additus Ministerialis ad pronunciandam juramenti rotham (si interponet motionem Citatus, sistendo Juramentum Parti Ductum lucranti

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

the mound, the sixth in number, a hundred paces from the fifth. Crossing that road, proceeding in a straight line toward the south, exiting the same pine forest, a mound was raised under the very edge of the pine forest on the boundary of the fields called *Długie Pole*, belonging from antiquity to the estate of the village of [name] of the plaintiff, the seventh in number, eighty paces from the sixth; and from that same seventh mound toward the south, proceeding as straight as possible through those same fields and open country, he ordered a ditch to be raised with a plough prepared for that purpose, extending to the particular corner mounds; which, at the application of the parties — both the principal acting parties and the collateral heir — as being subject to no dispute, he ordered to be renewed and raised up; and marked them all with metal markers, both all the wall mounds and the particular corner mounds, with charcoal, brick, and iron slag placed separately in each of them. After which erection of mounds — both corner and particular corner mounds, and wall mounds — the Sub-Chamberlain's court decrees that the plaintiff's party ^[*The confirmation of the boundary path enjoined.*] that has won the boundary path shall confirm it as the seventh with witnesses of equal rank, adhering to the statute of the Kingdom, at his final particular corner mound, in that oath formula: that by his boundary path he has taken no land or estate from the respondent, nor has alienated any from that hereditary estate; and this immediately — the court messenger is added for the pronouncing of the oath formula. (If the respondent shall interpose a motion, suspending the oath for the party that has won the boundary path,

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-50">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-50">p. 50</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

tunc debet fieri admissa, ad Judicia Tribunalis Regni, ejusdem Provinciæ, ubi Bona consistunt: si verò non interponet motionem, accedat Judicium ad attentandum Juramentum) quod Juramentum idem Generosus NN. una cum sex Testibus, genere sibi similibus, NN. rothisante sibi Ministeriali Rotha superius descripta, ad mentem Statuti in facie Judicij sui, & attentante Parte, & quidem Generosus NN. Actor ad Imaginem Crucifixi Domini, jam vero præfati Testes, erectis in Cælum duobus digitis, flexis genibus, in ultimo Scopulo Aciali præstiterunt; Proinde earundem Granicierum robur perpetuæ firmitatis Judicium habere vult & declarat, & Ministeriali alta sua & sonorosa voce in præsentia omnium ad hunc Actum congregatorum, publicare, & proclamare, demandavit. Quas nemo violare, aut alio quovis modo annihilare audeat sub pæna duorum millium Marcarum, per Judicium competens decernenda. (Si verò Partes sibi abinvicem pascua communia concedent, scribatur) Et quoniam Partes utræque; mutua pascua communia in Borris & Campis desertis, Hereditatum suarum, sibi ab invicem, juxta antiquum usum concedunt, proinde Judicium decernit, quatenus Partes utræque ijsdem pascuis liberis ab invicem concessis utantur citra tamen damnum.

* * *

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

then the matter must be admitted to the Tribunal courts of the Kingdom of that province where the estates are situated; but if no motion is interposed, the court shall proceed to attempt the oath.) Which oath the Honourable [names] together with six witnesses of equal rank, [names], with the court messenger reading the formula to them as described above, according to the intent of the statute before the face of his court and with the party present — the Honourable [names], plaintiff, before the image of the Crucified Lord; and the aforesaid witnesses, with two fingers raised to heaven and on bended knee, at the final particular corner mound swore; therefore the court wills and declares that those same boundaries shall have the force of perpetual validity, and ordered the court messenger to proclaim and declare this with a loud and sonorous voice in the presence of all assembled for this act. Which boundaries let no one dare violate or annihilate in any way, under a penalty of two thousand marks to be decreed by the competent court. (If the parties shall grant one another mutual common pasture, it shall be written:) And since both parties mutually grant to one another common pasture in the pine forests and deserted open country of their estates, according to ancient usage, the court therefore decrees that both parties shall enjoy those same freely granted mutual common pastures, yet without damage.

* * *

</div>

Actum in locò Campestri Differentiarum inter Bona Villæ N. Magnifici NN. Hereditariæ ab una, & Bona Villæ N. Generosi NN. Hereditariæ, sub ipsa Borra N. vocitata, non procul a stagno ad Villam N. spectante, in concurrentia trinarum Hereditatum, tum etiam Villæ N. Generosi N. Collateralis, penes viam stratam de Villa N. ad Villam N. tendentem, ubi eo in loco, nulli exstant Scopuli Angulares earundem Hereditatum, ab aliquot decem annis, per subditos, Generosi NN. de Villa N. disjectos, Feria N. post Festum N. Anno Domini N.

Coram Magnificis NN. Siradiensi, & NN. Lanciciensi Camerarijs Granicialibus, Palatinatuum eorundem, nec non NN. Petricoviensi Judice, atque; NN. Notario Vielunensi Castrensibus, vigore Decreti Tribunalitij Regni Petricoviensis Feria sexta post Festum NN. Anno præsenti, inter Partes infrascriptas prolati, quo mediante injuncta est Condescensio Officiorum Succamerarialium in fundum Bonorum suprà in Actu connotatorum, seu ad locum

##### *Decree on Serious Contested Matters, Issued at the Field Place of the Differences pursuant to the Intent of a Tribunal Decree.*

Done at the field place of the differences between the estates of the village of [name], hereditary property of the Right Honourable [names], on the one part, and the estates of the village of [name], hereditary property of the Honourable [names], beneath the very pine forest called [name], not far from the mere belonging to the village of [name], at the convergence of three estates, and likewise of the village of [name] of the Honourable [name], collateral heir, beside a paved road running from the village of [name] to the village of [name], where at that place no corner mounds of those estates are standing, having been destroyed some ten years past by the subjects of the Honourable [names] of the village of [name]; on the [day] after the feast of [name], in the year of the Lord [name].

Before the Right Honourable [names], Boundary Chamberlain of Sieradz, and [names], Boundary Chamberlain of Łęczyca, of those same palatinates, as well as [names], Judge of Piotrków, and [names], Notary of Wieluń, of the municipal courts; by virtue of the decree of the Crown Tribunal of Piotrków, issued on the Friday after the feast of [names] in the present year between the parties named below, whereby the field hearing of the Sub-Chamberlain's offices was enjoined at the seat of the estates noted above in the act, or at the place of

</div>
</section>

</div>

dispute, and there the settling and final decision of the claims in respect of the boundaries mutually brought before the Tribunal court, on account of the aforesaid right of path instituted by the offices — and indeed, for the originally plaintiff's party, the offices of Sieradz or Wieluń or Inowłodź, and for the cited party who is likewise plaintiff, the offices of Łęczyca or Gąsawa or Sochaczew or Warsaw; the absence of one of the Right Honourable Sub-Chamberlains notwithstanding, provided that one Sub-Chamberlain or his official is present on behalf of either party — they shall first of all order or he shall order the production of documents establishing the boundary case, party to party, with a prior summary thereof drawn up and signed; they shall immediately enjoin a sworn confirmation of the real production thereof; they shall investigate, with a prior sworn inquiry if necessary, the convergence of three estates and the corner mounds, wherever they may be found; they shall review and note the boundary paths of the parties; they shall open or he shall open the old mounds; they shall erect new ones and renew the old; they shall adjudge to the party that demonstrates the more probable boundary path; they shall order or he shall order the party that wins the boundary path to confirm at the final mound according to the intent of the statute; and they shall finally adjudicate between the parties, every appeal and extension cut off. For the settling of the parties' claims in the case of fact and

Quorum decisioni tam Judicij Succamerarialis quam Officiorum, Castrensium, seu unius Officij vel Officialis decisioni parere & acquiescere debebunt & tenebuntur, sub pæna Bannitionis, illo in Termino Condescensionis publicanda. De præmissis in se latiori existente, ita in Termino hodierno, ex præinferto Decreto Tribunalis Regni, fundata jurisdictione sua, cum securitate Actûs, & per Ministerialem Regni generalem providum NN. de Villa N. authenticum & juratum, præsentibus Generosis ac Nobilibus NN. & alijs diversi

Et quoniam Partes utræque; eadem documenta comportarunt, & ad comprobationem juratoriam, super realem comportationem eorundem se se trahunt, proinde admissibilibus ijsdem Partibus adinventis, additur Ministerialis ad pronunciandam juramenti rotham; Et quoniam Partes utræque; in facie Judicij sui, ad Imaginem Crucifixi Christi Domini, rothisante sibi Ministeriali in eam Juramenti, Rotham, prout omnia documenta ad Causam præsentem spectantia, in Judicio reposita, realiter comportarunt, nec quidquam in damnum & præjudicium Partis, penes se detinuerunt neque; celarunt, DEO & Sancta JESU Christi Passione, ita ipsum adjuvante, præstiterunt, (Si vero Partes sibi indulgent juramentum, quod licitum est Partibus, tunc scribatur) & quoniam Partes, hocce juramentum super realem comportationem sibi ab invicem indulserunt, proinde satisfecisse Decreto Judicij sui adinvenit, &

Partibus ijsdem in Causa præsenti procedere mandat. Anno præsenti reassumpta Jurisdictione sua, & per Ministerialem Regni acclamata, in procedendo: suspensa resolutione Angularitatis, & Acialitatis, ad revisionem Ductuum, Partium utrarumque; & quidem primò Partis originaliter Actoreæ Magnifici NN. Bonorum Villæ N. Heres Judicium processit. Itaque; incepit Ductum ^[*Inchoavit Ductum Actor.*] suum Magnificus NN. Bonorum Villæ N. Heres, a concurrentia trinarum Hereditatum, & loco fundatæ Jurisdictionis, devenit progrediendo a plaga occidentis Hyemalis (vel etiam scribatur occidentis æstivalis prout est in re.) ad plagam orientis æstivalis, medio tantum stadio ad Fluviolum N. vocitatum, quem Fluviolum pro Limite naturali sumpsit, & eodem Fluviolo plus minus decem stadijs eundo, uti Limite naturali, devenit ad summitatem stagni ad Bona Villæ N. Hereditatis Generosi NN. citati, & per medium ejusdem stagni, Parietem sibi sumendo, adduxit Judicium ad dimissorium aquæ, vulgo *upust*; allegando quod medietas ejusdem stagni ad Hereditatem ejus Villæ N. ab antiquo spectabant, & ad præsens spectare debet. orders those same parties to proceed in the present case. The sun at last declining toward the west, he ordered the present act to be adjourned to the morrow by the aforesaid court messenger. The Right Honourable [names], heir of the estates of the village of [name], ^[*The plaintiff commenced his boundary path.*] therefore began and commenced his boundary path from the convergence of three estates and the place of the founded jurisdiction, proceeding from the quarter of the winter west (or likewise the summer west may be written, as may be the case) toward the quarter of the summer east, for half a furlong only to the small river called [name]; which small river he took as the natural boundary, and proceeding along that same small river for more or less ten furlongs, using it as the natural boundary, he reached the upper end of the mere belonging to the estates of the village of [name], hereditary property of the Honourable [names], cited party; and taking the midpoint of that same mere as his boundary wall, he led the court to the water sluice, commonly called *upust* [T.N. 1]; alleging that one half of that same mere had from antiquity belonged, and ought at present to belong, to his estate of the village of [name]. At which place, the Honourable [names], heir of the estates of the village of [name],

Heres, contra Magnificum NN. respectu indebitæ usurpationis medietatis stagni sui ab antiquo hereditarij, ad Villam ejus N. pertinentis, & diversione certa inter Fratres antecedanæos Heredes, de Data & Actu ejus in Bonis N. conscripta, Actisque; Castrensibus N. Feria N. Anno N. roborata probantis, tum ratione demonstrationis Ductûs eodem Fluviolo, pro limite naturali sumpto, quoniam utræque; Ripæ, ad Hereditatem ejus spectant. Reprotestante Magnifico Actore, contra præmissam protestationem Partis citatæ, quia ipse in fundamento legis Positivæ, deficientibus Graniciebus, & Scopulis inter easdem Hereditates, Limitem naturalem sibi sumpsit, & ulterius eodem torrente a Dimissario profluente, ad orientem progrediendo, plus quam una parte milliaris eundo, adduxit Judicium ad Scopulos Angulares, seu Aciales, non procul ab eodem Fluviolo, ferme uno stadio a ripa ejusdem, in loco elevatiori, per Judicium Succamerariale in Anno N. ex mente Decreti Tribunalitij situatos & erectos esse sat notabiles, summam rotunditatem & circulum habentes, in quo loco, & circa eosdem Scopulos Angulares, & Aciales, Ductum suum finivit & determinavit. Et ibidem personaliter comparens Generosus NN. Collateraliter adcitatus, eandem Angularitatem & Acilitatem ejusdem Magnifici NN. Parieti suo attingentem, & Bonis citatæ Partis videlicet Villæ N.

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

protested against the Right Honourable [names] regarding the wrongful usurpation of one half of his mere, hereditary from antiquity and belonging to his village of [name], and proving it by a certain division made between the former co-heir brothers, drawn up in writing at [name] and confirmed before the municipal records of [name] on the [day] of [name] in the year [name]; and also regarding the demonstration of the boundary path along that same small river taken as the natural boundary, since both banks belong to his estate. The Right Honourable plaintiff counter-protesting against the aforesaid protest of the cited party — because he, on the basis of positive law, in the absence of boundaries and mounds between those same estates, took the natural boundary for himself — and proceeding further along the same torrent flowing from the sluice toward the east, going more than one part of a mile, he led the court to the corner mounds or particular corner mounds not far from that same small river, almost one furlong from its bank, in a more elevated place, erected and situated by the Sub-Chamberlain's court in the year [name] pursuant to the intent of a Tribunal decree, sufficiently notable, having the greatest roundness and a circle; at which place and around those same corner and particular corner mounds he concluded and determined his boundary path. And the Honourable [names], collaterally summoned, appearing there in person, judicially acknowledged that same corner point and particular corner point of the Right Honourable [names], meeting his boundary wall, and the estates of the cited party, namely the village of [name],

ut, veram & legitimam esse Judicialiter agnovit.

Finito & determinato Ductu, circa eandem Angularitatem supra exaratam, ad Revisionem Ductûs Partis citatæ, Judicium se se contulit. Et imprimis Pars citata initium Ductûs sumpsit, ab ijsdem Scopulis Angularibus, ubi Pars Actorea, determinavit Ductum suum, ab oriente, versus occidentem, & omisso eodem Fluviolo, quem Pars Actorea, pro Limite naturali esse allegavit, ad plagam eandem occidentalem, progrediendo, demonstravit Judicio Monticulum, Scopulo similem antiquum centum passibus, ab Angularibus distantem, allegando eundem primum & verum esse Scopulum Parietalem, non nisi pecoribus destructum, sub ipsa sylva Nart vocitata, exstantem, contra quam remonstrationem Scopuli, protestatus est Magnificus NN. allegando, non esse verum Scopulum, sed Monticulum, a quo ulterius eadem sylva Pinatica non adeo densa Ductum suum, versus orientem, dirigendo, devenit ad viam stratam publicam de oppido N. ad Villam N. tendentem, allegando, eandem viam esse Granicialem, & eadem parietaliter sumpta, ulterius proficiscendo, per Borram vario arborum genere excretam, ferme medio milliari cundo, stetit penes locum lutosum, *Bialy Lug* vocitatum, & ibidem Concurrentiam trinarum Hereditatum, allegando, Ductum suum finivit & determinavit, & in eo loco,

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

to be true and legitimate.

The boundary path having been concluded and determined, regarding the same corner point written above, the court proceeded to the review of the cited party's boundary path. First the cited party took the commencement of its boundary path from those same corner mounds where the plaintiff's party had determined its boundary path, from the east toward the west; and setting aside that same small river which the plaintiff's party had alleged to be the natural boundary, proceeding toward that same western quarter, he demonstrated to the court an ancient hillock resembling a mound, standing a hundred paces from the corner mounds, alleging it to be the first and true wall mound, destroyed only by cattle, standing under the very forest called *Nart*. Against which demonstration of the mound, the Right Honourable [names] protested, alleging it to be not a true mound but a hillock. Proceeding further from it through the same pine forest, not particularly dense, directing his boundary path toward the east, the cited party reached a paved public road running from the town of [name] to the village of [name], alleging that same road to be the boundary road; taking it as a wall boundary and proceeding further through the pine forest grown with various kinds of trees, going almost half a mile, stood beside a boggy place called *Biały Łąg*, and there alleging the convergence of three estates, concluded and determined his boundary path; at which place,

</div>
</section>

personaliter comparens, Generosus NN. Bonorum Villæ N. Heres collateraliter adcitatus, veram concurrentiam trinarum Hereditatum, unius sui Collateralis, & binarum Partium litigantium, esse agnovit, allegando, quod in hoc loco, non verò in eo, a quo Pars Actorea Ductum suum inchoavit, dissipati erant Scopuli, non per subditos, citatæ Partis, verum Magnifici Actoris. (Scribatur Protestatio de Nullitate totius Ductûs quam etiam contra collateralem)

Quibus finitis, & Judicialiter determinatis, Ductibus Partium utrarumque; Judicium ad meliorem informationem Judicij sui, & sufficientiorem probationem Partium, Inquisitionem expediendam, per testes senes, fide dignos nullo crimine notatos, per Partes tam principaliter agentes, ratione totius Ductûs, quam Collateralem, respectu Angularitatis, & concurrentiæ trinarum Hereditatum, in Loco per eundem agnito, censet, decernendo quatenus Partes eædem, Inquisitionem prætactam, præevijs porrigendis Interrogatorijs, & sibi ab invicem communicandis, expediant, idque; instantanèe, & additur Ministerialis ad pronunciandam Juramenti Rotham, super incorruptionem testium, quod Juramentum, siquidem Partes tam principaliter agentes, quam Collateralis, in facie Judicij sui Rothisante sibi Ministeriali, ad Imaginem Crucifixi Domini, flexis genibûs Rotha solita Juramentum corporale, præstiterunt,

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

the Honourable [names], heir of the estates of the village of [name], collaterally summoned, appearing in person, acknowledged the true convergence of three estates — of one collateral heir's estate and of the two litigating parties — to be there, alleging that at this place, and not at the place from which the plaintiff's party commenced its boundary path, the mounds had been destroyed — not by the subjects of the cited party, but of the Right Honourable plaintiff. (A protest regarding the nullity of the entire boundary path, and likewise against the collateral heir, shall be written.)

Both boundary paths of the parties having been concluded and judicially determined, the court considers, for better information to itself and more sufficient proof from the parties, that a sworn inquiry shall be conducted — by elderly witnesses of good repute and free from any charge of crime — by the parties, both the principal acting parties regarding the entire boundary path and the collateral heir regarding the corner point and the convergence of three estates at the place acknowledged by him; decreeing that those same parties shall conduct the aforesaid sworn inquiry, with prior interrogatories drawn up and communicated to one another, immediately; and the court messenger is added for the pronouncing of the oath formula regarding the impartiality of the witnesses. Which oath, since the parties — both the principal acting parties and the collateral heir — with the court messenger reading the formula to them, before the face of his court, before the image of the Crucified Lord, on bended knees, in the customary formula, swore a bodily oath;

</div>

proinde ad expediendam eandem Judicium accessit, & Actum præsentem ad diem crastinam Ministeriali reclamare injunxit; Tandem Die crastina adveniente, videlicet Feria N. In directa responsione ad lectionem Inquisitionum per Partes omnes, tam principaliter agentes, quam collateralem, Generosam NN. eductarum, Juramento comprobatarum, & in Judicio suo repositarum, condescendit. Quibus lectis, & per omnes circumstantias perpensis, & trutinatis, ^[*Decretum Adjudicationis Ductûs.*] resolvendo suspensionem Angularitatis superius factam. Licet quidem Pars collateraliter adcitata, Angularitatem seu Acilitatem, ac si in Concurrentia trinarum Hereditatum, circa locum lutosum, *Bialy Lug* vocitatum, & penes viam stratam de Oppido N. ad Villam N. tendentem, judicialiter agnovit, verum quoniam Pars citata simul & Actorea, eandem Angularitatem, & totum Ductum via eadem publica, sumptum, nullis Documentis Authenticis, neque; Inquisitionibus, præsenti in Termino, probat, proinde reprobato Ductu totali, Partis citatæ, jam verò probabiliori Magnifici NN. Actoris adinvento; eundem Fluviolum seu Torrentem, pro perpetuo Limite & Parietè ijsdem Hereditatibus assignat, & ad sipationem Scopulorum Angularium in eo loco fundatæ Jurisdictionis Judicium accessit. he therefore proceeded to conduct the same inquiry, and ordered the court messenger to adjourn the present act to the morrow. In the direct response, he descended to the reading of the sworn inquiries drawn out by all parties — both the principal acting parties and the collateral heir, the Honourable [names] — confirmed on oath and deposited in his court. Which having been read and weighed and examined in all their circumstances, ^[*Decree of adjudication of the boundary path.*] resolving the suspension of the corner point made above: although the collaterally summoned party acknowledged judicially the corner point or particular corner point, as being at the convergence of three estates, near the boggy place called *Biały Łąg* and beside the paved road running from the town of [name] to the village of [name] — yet since the cited party who is also plaintiff proves that same corner point, and the entire boundary path taken along that same public road, by no authentic documents and no sworn inquiries at the present session, the court therefore, the entire boundary path of the cited party having been rejected, and the more probable boundary path of the Right Honourable [names], plaintiff, having been found, assigns that same small river or torrent as the perpetual boundary and boundary wall to those same estates; and the court proceeded to the erection of corner mounds at the place of the founded jurisdiction. And

eundem locum uti in Concurrentia trinarum Hereditatum, prout ex Inquisitionibus luculenter patet, Angularitati præfigit & determinat. From which wall mound, by that same small river or torrent, the boundary wall extends through the middle of the mere and the water sluice, commonly called *upust*. Expensarum pro reparatione ejusdem stagni quotannis, ad eundem spectare debebit) usque; ad Scopulos Aciales, seu Angulares Hereditatum NN. quos renovare & in altum erigere Judicium demandavit cum impositione signorum metallium, in quemlibet Scopulum, prout in priores imposuit, quibus erectis, & renovatis Scopulis Angularibus, & Acialibus. Judicium Succamerariale Parti Ductum lucranti, adhærendo Statuto Regni, Juramentum præstare cum sex testibus, genere sibi similibus demandat, idque; instantanee, & additur Ministerialis ad pronunciandam Juramenti Rotham, movit Pars citata, ad Judicia Tribunalitia Regni Petricoviensia; non admissa, ex ea ratione, quod finalis præcommissa est, Disjudicatio Causæ præsentis, præcisa quavis Appellatione, per Decretum Tribunalitium; quod Juramentum Magnificus NN. Actor, una cum sex testibus, genere sibi similibus, videlicet Generosis NN. flexis ad Imaginem Crucifixi JESU Christi Domini genibûs, Testes verò erectis in Cælum duobûs digitûs Juramentum corporale, in ultimo Scopulo suo Aciali, Rothisante sibi Ministeriali, in facie Judicij sui, non attentante Parte, imo temere recedente, præstitit, proinde ijsdem Graniciebus robur perpetuæ firmitatis habere debere declarat. of the costs of the annual repair of that same mere shall for all time concern that same party.) The cited party moved to the Crown Tribunal courts of the Kingdom at Piotrków; not admitted, on the ground that final adjudication of the present case, every appeal cut off, was pre-committed by the Tribunal decree. Which oath the Right Honourable [names], plaintiff, together with six witnesses of equal rank, namely the Honourable [names], on bended knees before the image of the Crucified JESUS Christ the Lord — the witnesses with two fingers raised to heaven — swore a bodily oath at his final particular corner mound, with the court messenger reading the formula, before the face of his court, the party not being present but having withdrawn rashly; he therefore declares that those same boundaries shall have the force of perpetual validity. Which boundaries let no one dare to violate or annihilate, under a penalty of two thousand marks to be decreed from now by the competent court, by virtue of the present decree.

Finito & determinato Actu Granicierum, inter Partes suprascriptas, per Officia Succamerarialia, Siradiense, & Lanciciense, Officia Castrensia infrascripta, Radomiensia & Opocznense, per Partes conducta, Die hodierna, videlicet: Feria N. Anno præsenti, reassumpta Jurisdictio sua Officiosa, & per Ministerialem Regni Generalem Providum NN. præsentibus Generosis NN. proclamata, ad executionem Decreti Tribunalitij de Actu superius exarato, procedere voluerunt. Verum quoniam Partes utræque; videlicet Magnificus NN. & Generosus NN. sistendo ulteriorem Juris gradum, ratione variarum prætensionum, & violentiarum, ex controverso Fundo pullulantium, victi amicorum suorum persuasione, reconciliando sibi vicinam amicitiam, inter se composuerunt, proinde approbata eadem Complanatione, prætensiones, prætensionibus, violentias, violentijs compensandas censet, Protestationes, Manifestationes, ac totum & integrum Processum, tollit, cassat & annihilat, nihilque; bonæ famæ, ac integerrimo honori Partibus utrisque; nocere non debere declarat, Decreti præsentis vigore.

Si vero Partes Complanationem non inijverint, tunc Officia Castrensia Proventus, ex fundis indebite possessis, & dislimitatis, ac prætensiones omnes liquidabunt, cognoscent, compensationem & restitutionem eorundem Cui a Quo competierit,

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

The boundary act between the above-named parties having been concluded and determined by the Sub-Chamberlain's offices of Sieradz and Łęczyca, and the municipal court offices named below — of Radom and Opoczno — engaged by the parties on today's day, namely the [day] of [name] in the present year; the official jurisdiction having been reassumed and proclaimed by the court messenger of the Kingdom, the Honest [names], in the presence of the Honourable [names] — wishing to proceed to the execution of the Tribunal decree of the act written above. But since both parties, namely the Right Honourable [names] and the Honourable [names], staying further proceedings in law on account of various claims and acts of violence arising from the disputed estate, overcome by the persuasion of their friends, and reconciling neighbourly friendship between themselves, came to a composition; he therefore, approving that same amicable settlement, considers claims to be compensated against claims and acts of violence against acts of violence; annuls, voids, and cancels the protests, declarations, and the entire and complete process, and declares that nothing shall be to the detriment of the good name and unblemished honour of either party, by virtue of the present decree.

If, however, the parties have not entered into an amicable settlement, then the municipal court offices shall settle, cognise, and determine the account of revenues from the plots wrongfully held and delimited and all claims, shall order compensation and restitution thereof to whom and from whom it shall be due,

</div>
</section>

demandabunt, & respectu perpetratarum Violentiarum, pænas pro qualitate facti irrogabunt, & finaliter præcisa Appellatione decident.

##### *Decretum ex vi Inscriptionis Compromissi Arbitrale, per Serias Controversias prolatum.*

Actum in Campo, in loco Differentiarum penes Viam publicam de Oppido N. ad Villam N. tendentem, circa duos Scopulos Angulares, notabiles, in concurrentia trinarum Hereditatum, & Parietum, unius Villæ N. Generosi NN. alterius Villæ N. Generosi NN. Partium inter se originaliter agentium, & tertij Villæ N. Generosi N. Feria N. post Festum N. proxima, Anno Dni NN.

Coram Magnifico NN. Succamerario Palatinatus N. ad hunc Actum inscripto Superarbitro, tum Generosis NN. ex Parte Actorea Generosis verò NN. ex Parte Generosi N. citati, a partibus inscriptis Arbitris, (per tres ad minimum debent inscribere partes Arbitros, quisque; pro sua parte, ad dignoscendas Granicies, si verò alium, non Succamerarium pro super Arbitro eligent, tunc inter Arbitros debet esse Camerarius Granicialis, sine cujus præsentia, Actus Granicialis Limitum terminari non potest) vigore Inscriptionis Compromissorialis, de Actu ejus

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

and in respect of the acts of violence perpetrated shall impose penalties according to the nature of the act, and shall finally decide, every appeal cut off.

---

**Translator's Note**

[T.N. 1] *Upust*: a Polish term for a water sluice or mill-gate — the opening or device by which water is released from a mill pond or mere. The term is retained in italics and the Latin gloss (*vulgo upust*) indicates Paciorkowski himself treated it as a vernacular word requiring explanation within the Latin text.

##### *Arbitral Decree on Serious Contested Matters, Issued by Virtue of a Registered Compromissorial Instrument.*

Done in the field, at the place of the differences beside a public road running from the town of [name] to the village of [name], near two notable corner mounds at the convergence of three estates and boundary walls — one of the village of [name] of the Honourable [names], the second of the village of [name] of the Honourable [names], the parties originally acting between themselves, and the third of the village of [name] of the Honourable [name]; on the [day] after the feast of [name], next in order, in the year of the Lord [names].

Before the Right Honourable [names], Sub-Chamberlain of the Palatinate of [name], registered for this act as chief arbitrator; and before the Honourable [names] from the plaintiff's party, and the Honourable [names] from the part of the Honourable [name], cited party, as arbitrators registered by the parties — (the parties must register a minimum of three arbitrators each, each for his own part, for the determination of the boundaries; if, however, they choose someone other than the Sub-Chamberlain as chief arbitrator, then among the arbitrators there must be a Boundary Chamberlain, without whose presence a boundary act of limits cannot be concluded) — by virtue of the compromissorial instrument, of the act of

</div>

Succamerarius N. una cum Generosis suprafatis Arbitris, per tres a qualibet Parte locatis, ad locum controversum condescendendo, Jurisdictionem suam cum securitate Actûs præsentis, in præsentia Generosorum ac Nobilium NN. & aliorum diversi status & conditionis hominum, ad hunc Actum congregatorum, fundavit, & eandem Ministeriali Regni generali provido NN. proclamare demandavit, & Partes ad sui comparendum, acclamare fecit. the [day] of [name] in the year [name]; by which instrument power was granted and conceded to the Right Honourable chief arbitrator, together with the Honourable arbitrators, to withdraw from all sessions and from the Tribunal forum, in the matter of the boundaries brought mutually against one another before the Crown Tribunal court; to adjudicate and finally decide; to cognise all documents and decrees issued in that case; to adjudge to whichever party the more probable boundary path shall belong; to erect mounds and renew the ancient ones; to enjoin and attempt the oath at the final mound for the party that has won the boundary path; to cognise the claims of the estate; and to finally adjudicate between the parties in all matters, every appeal and extension cut off. (The circumscription of the mounds erected shall be written from the foregoing act, yet without the confirmation, since no party has won the boundary path.)

##### *Decree Between Estates Never Previously Delimited, on Serious Contested Matters.*

Done at the field place of the differences between the estates of the village of [name] belonging from antiquity to the parish church, and the estates of the village of [name], hereditary property of the Honourable [name], in the pine forest called [name], near three corner mounds of the two aforesaid estates and of a third of the Honourable [name], collaterally summoned, situated at the convergence of three of those same estates, sufficiently notable; on the Monday next after the Sunday of [name], in the year of the Lord [names].

Before the Honourable [names], Boundary Chamberlain of the Palatinate of Sieradz, and of the District of Piotrków, by virtue of the referral of the boundary case from the Land Court of Sieradz, and likewise by virtue of the summons letters issued by the Right Honourable Sub-Chamberlain of the Palatinate of Sieradz, to both the cited party and the collateral heirs, with the session fixed for today as contained in the act, falling duly; of which summons letters

Partis citatæ, Tertium Hereditati Villæ N. Parti collateraliter adcitatæ, & eosdem omnes tres signis metallis, videlicet: vitro carbonibus, latere, & dimoscorio ferreo, obsignavit, a quibus Scopulis Angularibus a septentrione versus meridiem procedendo, sipatus est Scopulus Parietalis sexaginta passibus ab Angularibus distans, (Ulterior sipatio Scopulorum conscribatur, tam Parietalium, quam alterius Angularitatis, observentur Positiones locorum, & distantia Scopuli a Scopulo) Quibus omnibus sipatis tam Angularibus quam Parietalibus Scopulis, Judicium decernit, quatenus idem Reverendus Plebanus, comprobet in eam Juramenti Rotham, adhærendo statuto Regni, in ultimo Scopulo cum sex testibus Nobilibus, prout Ductu suo, nil Fundi & Terræ Villæ N. præfati citati hereditariæ ademit, & suæ Villæ & Hereditati Ecclesiasticæ appropriavit; Additur Ministerialis ad pronunciandam Juramenti Rotham, & quoniam

Proceeding from those corner mounds from the north toward the south, a wall mound was raised sixty paces from the corner mounds. All the corner and wall mounds having thus been raised, the court decrees that the said Very Reverend Parish Priest shall confirm the same in that oath formula, adhering to the statute of the Kingdom, at the final mound with six noble witnesses — that by his boundary path he has taken nothing of the estate and land of the aforesaid hereditary village of [name] of the cited party and appropriated it to his own village and ecclesiastical estate. The court messenger is added for the pronouncing of the oath formula; and since

idem Admodum Reverendus Plebanus, satisfaciendo Decreto Judicij sui, juramentum corporale ad Imaginem Crucifixi Domini, observata Juramentorum solennitate, una cum sex Nobilibus testibus NN. Rotha superius descripta, in facie Judicij sui, attentante Parte, præstitit, proinde earundem Granicierum robur perpetuæ firmitatis habere vult, & declarat, quas Granicies nemo violare aut annihilare audeat, sub pæna duorum millium Marcarum polonicalium per Judicium competens decernenda, Decreti præsentis vigore.

Hoc notandum, si Pars citata, motionem interposuisset a præstando Juramento, debet esse admissa uti in primo Termino Condescensionis, ex Decreto Judicij Terrestris, primæ Instantiæ ad Judicium Tribunalis Regni, & fiat Delineatio per Officium Succamerariale Mappæ, cum expressione vera & reali earundem Hereditatum, ac Bonorum Collateralium & Ductuum Partium utrarumque; ac Scopulorum sipatorum tam Angularium quam Parietalium, ut possit Judicium Tribunalitium cognoscere, rectiorem & probabiliorem Ductum, & eandem subscribere, cui dabitur fides, in eodem Judicio, si per juratam Personam subsequetur ejusdem Mappæ Delineatio, hoc non tantum incumbit Geometræ, sed etiam est muneris Officij Succamerarialis, debent habere in hoc experientiam.

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

the same Very Reverend Parish Priest, in satisfaction of the decree of his court, swore a bodily oath before the image of the Crucified Lord, the solemnities of oaths being observed, together with six noble witnesses [names], in the formula described above, before the face of his court, with the party present — he therefore wills and declares that those same boundaries shall have the force of perpetual validity; which boundaries let no one dare to violate or annihilate, under a penalty of two thousand Polish marks to be decreed by the competent court, by virtue of the present decree.

It is to be noted here: if the cited party had interposed a motion against the swearing of the oath, it must be admitted as at the first session of the field hearing, from the decree of the land court as the court of first instance, to the Crown Tribunal court; and a delineation of a map shall be made by the Sub-Chamberlain's office, with a true and real representation of those same estates, and of the collateral estates and the boundary paths of both parties, and of the mounds raised — both corner and wall mounds — so that the Tribunal court may be able to cognise the straighter and more probable boundary path and subscribe to it; faith shall be given to it in that same court, if the delineation of that same map is made by a sworn person. This falls not only to the surveyor but is also the duty of the Sub-Chamberlain's office; they must have experience in this.

Jako żadna Rzecz nie może być trwalsza, y doskonálsza, iák tákowa, ktora z własney Człowiéká pochodzi woli, więc My niżey ná Podpisie wyrażeni, uchodząc wielkich, y nieznośnych kosztow y expensow právnych, ktore w podobnych Spráwách závzięte ná siebie Strony ponosić zwykły, y práwie
