##### *Boundaries Between Terrestrial Estates, Made by Mutual Consent of the Parties by Virtue of an Amicable Settlement.*

Done at the field place of the differences between the estates of the village of [name] of the Honourable [names], and the estates of the village of [name] of the Honourable [names], hereditary estates, near three clearly notable corner mounds in the pine forest called [name], at the convergence of three estates, and the third of the estate of the village of [name] of the Honourable [names], collateral heir, likewise hereditary; on the Monday next after the feast of [names], in the year of the Lord [name].

Before the Honourable [names], Boundary Chamberlain of the Palatinate of Sieradz and the District of Piotrków, by virtue of a certain amicable settlement dated the [day] of [name] in the year [name], drawn up and executed between the parties contained in the act for the purpose of the delimitation of the aforesaid estates, subscribed by the hands of those making the settlement, and likewise by the collateral heirs; the tenor of which settlement follows and is as follows:

As nothing can be more lasting and more perfect than that which proceeds from the free will of man, we therefore, the undersigned below, avoiding the great and intolerable costs and legal expenses which parties who have engaged in such cases are accustomed to bear upon themselves, and which bring

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-78">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-78">p. 78</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

jeden drugiego do ostátniey przyprowadza fortuny ruiny, przez kilkánastoletnią o Gránice kłotnią, tákową między sobą Duktu y ściány, między Dziedzinámi násżemi, y Wsiámi od Narożnikov do Narożnikov uczyniliśmy Plantę, (*Opisác należy explicite, iákiemi mieyscámi idzie Dukt, sobie przyznány, chociaż y nie Rectilinio, bo w tákonych Gránicách rzadko obserwatur.*) do ktorego Rozgrániczenia uprosić sobie powinniśmy y usypánia Kopcow IMC Páná Komorniká Woiewodztwa nászego, y onego sprowádzić ná Mieysce opisáne, naydáley zá Niedziel cztery, ktore to Gránice Kopcámi przez IMC Páná Komorniká oznáczone wiecznie z Sukcessorámi Nászemi, trzymác y dotrzymác obliguiemy się y powinni będziemy, pod zakládem dwoch tysiécy Grzywien, in casu Kontrávencyi Forum Ziemstwá Sierádzkiego, lub Trybunáłu Koronnego do odpowiádánia náznáczamy, y ná to się przy zwykłych Pieczęciách násżych rekami własnemi podpisuiemy, Datum N. Anno N.

Quo in Termino vigore præfertæ Complanationis ad affectationem, & officiosam requisitionem, Partium Complanantium ad locum Controversum, ibidemque; fundata Jurisdictione sua, & per Ministerialem in præsentia tam Partium principaliter agentium, quam Collateralium, & aliorum diversi status, & conditionis hominum, ad hunc Actum congregatorum,

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

one almost to the final ruin of his fortune through a dispute over boundaries lasting several years — we have made between ourselves such a plan of the boundary path and boundary wall between our estates and villages, from corner to corner. (*The boundary path agreed upon shall be described explicitly, by what places it runs, even if not in a straight line, for in such boundaries the straight line is rarely observed.*) For the purpose of which delimitation we are obliged to request His Grace the Sub-Chamberlain of our Palatinate, and to bring him to the described place, at the latest within four weeks; which boundaries marked by mounds by His Grace the Sub-Chamberlain, together with our successors, we bind and shall be obliged to keep and observe for ever, under a pledge of two thousand marks — in case of contravention, we designate the forum of the Land Court of Sieradz or of the Crown Tribunal for the rendering of account — and to this we subscribe with our own hands under our customary seals. Given at [name], in the year [name].

At which session, by virtue of the aforesaid amicable settlement, at the application and official request of the parties to the settlement, at the disputed place, the jurisdiction having been founded there, and proclaimed by the court messenger in the presence of both the principal acting parties and the collateral heirs and other persons of various rank and condition assembled for this act,

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-79">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-79">p. 79</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

virtute Complanationis præfertæ, ad sipationem Scopulorum, Officium accessit. Et inprimis Scopulos Angulares renovare fecit, cum impositione signorum metallicorum, carbonum, laterum, & dimoscorij ferrei, a quibus Scopulis ab occasu versus orientem procedendo, sipatus est Scopulus, Parietalis, in loco dicto N. in numero Primus etc. (& sic consequenter describatur Sipatio eorundem Scopulorum, potest etiam circumscribi Ductus idiomate *polonico*)

Quibus sipatis Scopulis tam Angularibus, quam Parietalibus, & determinatis Graniciebus, Authoritate Officij sui, robur perpetuæ firmitatis habere vult, & declarat, & easdem præfato Ministeriali suprascripto proclamare, injunxit, si quis ex Collateralibus velit contradicere eisdem Graniciebus, nunc contradicat, quas Granicies Partes omnes tenere & observare debebunt, sub pæna duorum millium Marcarum, Actus præsentis vigore.

#### PRÆMONITIO
##### *Ad Lectorem, Litem Excitantem.*

Universis & Singulis litigantibus, ex sinceritate cordis opto, ut simili methodo, breviori via amicabili, & Complanationis, velint procedere, & differentias Granicierum inter se sopire, evitando

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

the office proceeded to the erection of mounds by virtue of the aforesaid amicable settlement. And first it caused the corner mounds to be renewed, with the placing of metal markers — charcoal, brick, and iron slag; proceeding from those mounds from the west toward the east, a wall mound was raised at the place called [name], first in number, etc. (and thus the erection of those same mounds shall be described in sequence; the boundary path may also be set out in the Polish language.)

All the corner and wall mounds having thus been raised and the boundaries determined, by the authority of his office he wills and declares the same to have the force of perpetual validity, and ordered the court messenger aforesaid to proclaim the same; if any of the collateral heirs wishes to contest those same boundaries, let him do so now. Which boundaries all the parties shall be bound to keep and observe, under a penalty of two thousand marks, by virtue of the present act.

#### FOREWARNING
##### *To the Reader Who Would Stir up Litigation.*

To all and each of those who litigate, I wish sincerely that they would be pleased to proceed by the same method — the shorter, amicable way of settlement — and to resolve the boundary differences among themselves, avoiding

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-80">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-80">p. 80</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

immensas Litis expensas, subjectiones tam in Judicijs Tribunalis Regni, quam in Termino Condescensionis, per longam Annorum seriem, quia potest dici quod Causa Granicialis, (& maxime inter potentes) est immortalis.

Qui habet animum dislimitandi Bona & Hereditatem suam cum vicina Hereditate, prius discat esse domi, qualis debeat esse foris ante Litis contestationem: Et quia nemo in sua causa Judex, consulat Jurisperitos Causæ Granicialis, & consumatis Viris, Documenta revideat, & mature ac diligenter eadem trutinet, si quadrent cum situ & positione locorum in Ductu, quem sibi usurpare vult. Relationi & allegationi subditorum minime confidat, quia sæpissime fallunt, sed oculis suis & documentis magis credendum, quia subditorum allegatio, est adulatoria & servilis, quæ seducit, & incitat ad Litem, aliter placendo Domino suo privatim allegant, aliter in Judicio, circa Inquisitionem demonstrant, vel ignoranter testimonia perhibent, & exinde perjuria cum offensa Divinæ Majestatis fiunt: multos novi, qui fundati in allegatione subditorum, non considerata bene causa rei gerendæ, Causam amiserunt; Et si nonnulli forte Ductum lucrati, substantia tamen exuti, Creditoribus Hereditatem suam porrigendo, Successoribus vero suis, scrinia Documentorum, lapsæ fortunæ, & defunctæ pecuniæ relinquendo, perierunt.

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

the immense costs of litigation, and subjections both in the Crown Tribunal courts and at the field hearing session, over a long series of years; for it may be said that a boundary case — and most of all between powerful parties — is immortal.

He who has the intention of delimiting his estates and inheritance from a neighbouring inheritance, let him first learn to be at home what he ought to be abroad before the contestation of the litigation. And since no one is judge in his own cause, let him consult those versed in the law in boundary cases, and learned men; let him review the documents and weigh them carefully and diligently, whether they correspond with the position and situation of the places in the boundary path which he wishes to claim for himself. Let him place no trust at all in the reports and allegations of his subjects, because they very often deceive; but his own eyes and the documents are more to be believed. For the allegation of subjects is flattering and servile, which seduces and incites to litigation: they allege one thing in private to please their master, and show another in court around the sworn inquiry, or give testimony out of ignorance; and from this come perjuries with offence to the Divine Majesty. I have known many who, relying on the allegation of their subjects and not having well considered the grounds of the matter to be pursued, lost their case. And even if some perchance won the boundary path, they nevertheless perished stripped of their substance — handing over their estate to creditors, and leaving to their successors only the chests of documents of a ruined fortune and exhausted funds.

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-81">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-81">p. 81</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

Arripere sibi mensuram, expensarum, & fructum rei lucrandæ, Belli & Juris dubius eventus, initia eorum semper sunt læta, progressu dura, eventu dubia, & quisque; bellator in fine perdit, qui a fine non incipit;

Et hoc adverte, quod si Tu, vi & potentia sui, Vicino tuo ab ortu rapies, Collateralis tibi ab occasu potest eripere.

Et maxime vitanda sint, etiamsi justa juramenta, & gravis Rotha ejus, Statuto descripta valde pretiosa est particula fundi, juramento evicta, & lucrata; nemini svadeo, tam caro emere, melius sine discrimine conscientiæ, via breviori amicitiæ, rem concludere.

Incitat etiam varios, & maxime Magnates punctum honoris ad Litem, utuntur omnibus medijs per punctum honoris, lucrari negotium & Judicatum favorabile: Si juste succedat eis feliciter, si injuste lucrabitur, onerat graviter conscientiam sui, & tenetur ad restitutionem in foro conscientiæ.

Consideres ergo Lector, has reflexiones, & observa, si vis quiete vivere, quod tibi tuta conscientia opto, visis tot exemplis, in varijs Personis practicatis.

* * *

#### DE COMMISSIONIBUS,
##### *Seu Fundorum Terrestrium, cum Bonis Regalibus.*

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

Let a man take the measure of the costs and the fruit of the thing to be won. The outcome of war and of law is uncertain; their beginnings are always pleasing, their progress hard, their outcome doubtful — and every combatant loses in the end who does not begin from the end.

And mark this: that if you by force and power shall seize from your neighbour to the east, the collateral heir may wrest from you from the west.

And above all things they are to be avoided — even with just oaths, and the solemn formula weighing heavily upon him — that parcel of estate won and gained by oath is very precious; I advise no one to buy it so dearly; it is better to conclude the matter by the shorter path of friendship, without hazard to one's conscience.

A point of honour also incites various men — and great men above all — to litigation; they use every means for the sake of a point of honour to win the matter and a favourable judgment. If it falls out justly for them, good; but if they win unjustly, it weighs heavily upon their conscience, and they are obliged to make restitution before the forum of conscience.

Consider therefore, Reader, these reflections, and take note of them if you wish to live in peace — which I wish you with a clear conscience, having seen so many examples played out in the lives of various persons.

* * *

#### ON ROYAL COMMISSIONS,
##### *Or the Delimitation of Terrestrial Estates with Royal Estates.*

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-82">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-82">p. 82</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

Antequam norma, & idea Actus Commissionalis, ratione Granicierum inter Bona Sacræ Regiæ Majestatis ab una, & Terrestria Nobilium Hereditaria, ab altera Parte erectorum, erigendorumve exponatur, differentia, imprimis quantum videlicet Commissiones, a Disterminio, sive Dislimitatione Bonorum ab utrinque; Terrestrium, supra explicato distant, consideranda, quæ ex inferius annotatis facile colligi potest.

Et imprimis quod ad Actum futurum Commissorialem, Remisso Judicij Terrestris ad Campum, quæ in Processu supra exarato, inter Bona ab utrinque; Terrestria, pro fundamento est posita, non fit necessaria, imo vero loco Remissionis ipsa sola a Sacra Regia Majestate, ex Cancellaria Regni, super Personas certas ^[*Commissarij Quinque adsint cum Succamerario.*] præcipuas, binas, Ordinis Dignitarij, binas Officialium Terrestrium, quintam Succamerarij, sive Camerarij cujuscunque; Palatinatus vel Terræ, qui facilius conduci poterint, (qui propter absentiam unius, duorumve, plures poni solent) impetranda in ordine Dislimitationis Bonorum & sipationis Scopulorum Commissio, seu Instrumentum Commissionis, quod potest emanare etiam, extra fines Regni, una

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

Before the form and model of a commissarial act — in respect of the boundaries between the estates of His Sacred Royal Majesty on the one part and the hereditary terrestrial estates of the nobility on the other, erected or to be erected — is set forth, the difference, in particular how far royal commissions differ from the termination or delimitation of estates on both sides terrestrial, explained above, must first be considered; which may readily be gathered from the notes below.

And first, that for a future commissarial act, the referral of the land court to the field — which in the process described above, between estates on both sides terrestrial, is laid as its foundation — is not necessary; indeed, instead of the referral, a royal commission or instrument of commission must be obtained from His Sacred Royal Majesty, from the Chancery of the Kingdom, directed to certain ^[*Five commissioners are to be present together with the Sub-Chamberlain.*] principal persons — two of the dignitary order, two of the terrestrial officials, and a fifth of the Sub-Chamberlain or Boundary Chamberlain of whatever palatinate or land they may most readily be engaged — (who, on account of the absence of one or two, are customarily named in greater number) for the purpose of the delimitation of estates and the erection of mounds; which commission may also be issued outside the borders of the Kingdom, together

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-83">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-83">p. 83</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

cum Mandato, Tenutarijs Bonorum Regalium, & Collateralibus.

Et subinde, loco Adcitationis, Innotescentiales^[*Innotescentiales, & Mandatum 2bus 7manis ante Terminum præcedere debent.*] Literæ, Dominorum Commissariorum, quam Collaterale, pro certo præfixo Termino, & loco per Ministerialem & duos Nobiles, duabus ante Terminum septimanis, una cum Mandatis Regijs extradendæ & deferendæ, quarum positarum Relationem; idem Ministerialis recognoscere debet, coram Actis proprij Districtus, ipseque; Actus Commissionalis, infra unius Anni & sex Septimanarum spatium finiri debet.

Deinde quod in Judicio & Actu Commissionali, virtute legis & Constitutionis Regni, in hoc libro contentarum nulla concedatur Dilatio, eo, quod sit unius temporis Actus, præterquam, si una die perfici non possit Negotium, in sequentem immediatum diem transferri, & postridie continuari & exequi poterit.

Ad hæc nulla datur ab eo Judicio Commissionali,^[*In Actu Commissionis nulla datur Dilatio. Nulla datur appellatio.*] ejusque; Decreto ad superius quodvis Regni Subsellium appellatio, eo quod sit Judicium absolutum, absolutamque; judicandi, Bonaque; a Regijs distinguendi, vigore Legum Regni (conscientia tantum sua ad id obligata & onerata) habet facultatem, & potestatem.

Porro quo ad figuram Judicij hujus attinet, quod^[*Subditi Regales tenentur comprobare Scopulos non vero Tenutarius.*] super Actoratum & lucratum Ductum, non Tenutarius Bonorum Regalium, verum Coloni eorundem

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

with a mandate to the leaseholders of the royal estates and to the collateral heirs.

And thereafter, in place of a summons, the summons letters ^[*The summons letters and mandate must precede the session by two weeks.*] of the lord commissioners, both to the Captain and to the collateral heir, for a certain fixed session and place, shall be issued and served by the court messenger and two noblemen two weeks before the session, together with the royal mandates; the service return of the same, once served, the same court messenger must acknowledge before the records of his own district; and the commissarial act itself must be concluded within the space of one year and six weeks.

Furthermore, that in the court and act of a commission, by virtue of the laws and constitutions of the Kingdom contained in this book, no postponement is granted, on the ground that it is an act of a single occasion; except that if the matter cannot be concluded in one day, it may be transferred to the next immediately following day and continued and executed on the morrow.

Moreover, no appeal from that commissarial court ^[*In the commissarial act no postponement is granted. No appeal is granted.*] and its decree lies to any higher court of the Kingdom, on the ground that it is an absolute court and has, by virtue of the laws of the Kingdom (its conscience alone obligated and burdened thereto), the absolute faculty and power of judging and distinguishing estates from royal ones.

Furthermore, as regards the form of this court: that ^[*The royal subjects are required to confirm the mounds, not the leaseholder.*] in respect of the right of path and the won boundary path, not the leaseholder of the royal estates but the peasant settlers of those same

</div>
</section>

Bonorum seu subditi villani, super veram & justam Ductionem & Erectionem Scopulorum, in ipsa Determinatione Granicierum, videlicet sex subditi, ex Communitate, Juramento comprobare tenentur lucratum Ductum, Rotha statuto Regni descripta.

Si vero Heres Bonorum Terrestrium lucrabitur Ductum, tunc non subditi ejus, verum ipsemet Nobilis cum sex Testibus genere sibi similibus, Rotha Statuto Regni descripta comprobare lucratum Ductum tenebitur.

Præterea quando Partes utrasque; deficiunt in Probationibus, ^[*Commissarij in defectu Granicierum limites faciunt.*] Literatorijs, & signis granicialibus visibilibus, aut tantum dubijs extantibus, tunc in tali casu reprobatis Ductibus Partium utrarumque; Judicium Commissoriale, ex re oculis subjecta, & moderamine Judicij sui Granicies præfatas, præcluso Juramento Partium, ex æquo & justo determinabit.

^[*Prosecutoriales contra Actum Commissionis vana & irrita.*] Insuper Citationes quævis, & Adcitationes ad quodcunque; Judicium Regni, tam ratione non admissæ Motionis, seu Appellationis, tam in Accessorijs, quam Definitiva sententia, vel ad tollendum, sive relevandum Actum Commissorialem, quocunque; Juris prætextu, & ratione emanatæ, vanæ & irritæ sunt, quando Actus Commissionalis in pleno completo, solita forma Juris & præevijs omnibus solennitatibus, ad id necessarijs, determinatus est.

Cæterum pæna, ratione Disjectionis Scopulorum,

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

estates, that is the village subjects, are required to confirm by oath the won boundary path regarding the true and just conduct and erection of the mounds at the very determination of the boundaries — namely six subjects from the community, in the oath formula prescribed by the statute of the Kingdom.

If, however, the heir of terrestrial estates wins the boundary path, then not his subjects but the nobleman himself, together with six witnesses of equal rank, shall be required to confirm the won boundary path in the oath formula prescribed by the statute of the Kingdom.

Moreover, when both parties are wanting in documentary proof ^[*The commissioners make the boundaries in the absence of boundary markers.*] and visible boundary markers, or only doubtful ones are standing, then in such a case, both parties' boundary paths having been rejected, the commissarial court shall determine the aforesaid boundaries from the thing presented to the eyes and by the moderation of its court, on equitable and just grounds, the oath barred to the parties.

^[*Prosecutorial letters against the commissarial act are void and of no effect.*] Furthermore, any citations and summonses to any court of the Kingdom whatsoever — whether on account of a motion or appeal not admitted, whether in accessory matters or in the final sentence, or for the purpose of quashing or relieving the commissarial act, issued on whatever legal pretext and ground — are void and of no effect, once the commissarial act has been determined in full and complete form, in the customary legal form and with all prior solemnities necessary for that purpose.

Furthermore, the penalty for the destruction of mounds

per Commissarios sipatorum, longe major quam Terrestrium, cum restauratione, per totidem Commissarios, & Officium Succamerariale, a Sacra Regia Majestate designatos, sumptu Tenutarij Bonorum Regalium.

Ad extremum quod Differentiæ, occasione Granicierum, & Limitum, inter Bona ab utraque; Parte^[*Inter Bona Regalia, & Terrestria, non datur Complanatio.*] Terrestria, per amicabilem Compositionem, terminari & complanari possint, secus inter Bona Regalia, & Terrestria eadem methodo, & via amicabili, concludi & terminari non possunt, ex ea ratione, quod sint Bona Reipublicæ, quæ sine consensu omnium Ordinum Regni, alienari, ita & in parte quavis minui, avelli, detrimentumque; pati, non possunt.

##### *Decretum Commissoriale, ex Serijs Partium Controversijs, per Moderamen Judicij prolatum.*

Actum in loco Campestri Differentiarum, inter Bona Villæ N. Generosi NN. Hereditaria ex una, & Bona Sacræ Regiæ Majestatis Villæ N. ad Capitaneatum N. spectantis, Tenutæ ad præsens Magnifici NN. ex altera, Partibus, circa viam publicam, de Oppido N. ad Villam N. tendentem, & circa tres Scopulos Aciales, seu Angulares, unum Bonorum præfatorum Villæ N. alterum vero Bonorum Villæ N.

</div>

erected by the commissioners is far greater than for terrestrial ones, with restoration at the expense of the leaseholder of the royal estates, through the same number of commissioners and the Sub-Chamberlain's office designated by His Sacred Royal Majesty.

Finally, ^[*Between royal and terrestrial estates no amicable settlement is granted.*] whereas differences occasioned by the boundaries and limits between estates on both sides terrestrial may be resolved and settled by amicable composition, those between royal and terrestrial estates cannot be concluded and resolved by the same method and by amicable means, for the reason that they are estates of the Commonwealth, which cannot, without the consent of all the orders of the Kingdom, be alienated, nor in any part diminished, severed, or made to suffer detriment.

AUGUSTUS THE THIRD, by the Grace of God King of Poland, Grand Duke of Lithuania, Ruthenia, Prussia, Masovia, Samogitia, Livonia, Kiev, Volhynia, Podolia, Podlachia, Smolensk, Severia, and likewise Hereditary Duke of Saxony, Prince and Elector.

N. Villarumque; eo pertinentium. Tenutæ Magnifici NN. Palatini N. NN. Capitanei nostri ab una: & Bona Villæ N. Generosi NN. Hereditaria Partibus ab altera. Quod quidem negotium Nos componi, & complanari cupientes, sinceritatibus & fidelitatibus Vestris, quarum fides & integritas ac in rebus peragendis dexteritas Nobis commendata & perspecta est, committendum esse duximus, prout præsentibus committimus. Injungentes, ac mandantes eisdem, ut communicato consilio, de tempore, & die ad id idoneo, ac inter se constituto, vocatisque; ad sui præsentiam literis Innotescentiæ suæ Partibus, quarum interest in locum Differentiarum conveniant, ibique; intellectis & auditis earundem Partium Propositionibus, & responsionibus, receptoque; senum fide dignorum sufficienti testimonio, mediante ipsorum, si opus fuerit, corporali Juramento, ac re ipsa oculis subjecta, positioneque; ac veritate illius ad plenum cognita & perspecta, neutri Partium favendo, sed solum DEUM & Justitiam ejus præ oculis habendo, dicta Bona dislimitent, & distingvant, scopulisque; & Signis metallicis, circa talia fieri solitis, illa consignent; diligenter tamen provideant, ne hac Dislimitatione Bona nostra Regalia aliquomodo imminuantur. In quo conscientias sinceritatum & fidelitatum vestrarum oneramus. Quidquid vero in eo Negotio statuerint, ac decreverint, id ratum & gratum habendo, a Partibus

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

of [name] and the villages belonging to it, leased to the Right Honourable [names], Palatine of [name], [names], Our Captain, on the one part; and the hereditary estates of the village of [name] of the Honourable [names], on the other part. Which matter We, desiring it to be settled and composed, have thought it fitting to commit to your Sincerity and Fidelity, in whose good faith, integrity, and skill in conducting affairs We have confidence and knowledge; as We commit it by these presents. Enjoining and commanding the same, that by mutually consulting together on a time and day suitable for that purpose, and fixing it among themselves, and having summoned the parties whose interest it concerns by their summons letters to their presence, they convene at the place of the differences; and there, having heard and listened to the submissions and responses of those same parties, and received the sufficient testimony of elderly witnesses of good repute, by means of their bodily oath if necessary, and having fully cognised and examined the thing itself presented to the eyes and the position and truth of that place — favouring neither party, but having only GOD and His justice before their eyes — let them delimit and distinguish the said estates, and mark them with mounds and metal markers, in the manner customary in such matters; being diligent, however, to provide that by this delimitation Our royal estates be in no way diminished. In which matter We burden the consciences of your Sincerity and Fidelity. Whatever, moreover, they shall enact and decree in that matter, We will to be ratified and acceptable, and

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-88">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-88">p. 88</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

dictis, inviolabiliter teneri, & observari, volumus. Absentia unius, & plurimum sinceritatum & fidelitatum Vestrarum modo ij & tot adsint, qui & quot de Jure adesse debent, non obstante. *Datum* etc.

Quibus quidem Commissionis Sacræ Regiæ Majestatis Literis perlectis, In Termino itaque; hodierno, per Nos, seu Literas Innotescentiarum, uti Commissarios a Sacra Regia Majestate editas, Partibus assignato, fundata Jurisdictione sua Commissionali, & cum omni securitate Actus præsentis, per Ministerialem Regni generalem providum de Villa N. Authenticum, & Juratum præsentibus Generosis ac Nobilibus NN. ac alijs diversi status & conditionis hominibus, ad hunc Actum congregatis, proclamata; Quarum Literarum Innotescentialium Magnifico Capitaneo, seu Tenutario Bonorum Regalium Oppidi N. & Villarum eo pertinentium Tenutario, editarum Tenor sequitur ejusmodi. Nos NN. NN. Commissarij ^[*Innotescentiales M. Capitaneo a Commissarijs.*] a Sacra Regia Majestate, ad infra scripta peragenda, assignati, & deputati, Tibi Magnifico seu Generoso NN. Bonorum Regalium N. Tenutario, vel Capitaneo Innotescimus; Oblatas esse nobis Literas Commissionis Sacræ Regiæ Majestatis, quibus sua Regia Majestas, nobis committere & mandare dignata est, quatenus Differentias Occasione Limitum inter Bona Regalia suprascripta N. Tenutæ ad præsens Tuæ ex una; & Bona Villæ N. Generosi NN.

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

that it be kept and observed inviolably by the said parties. The absence of one or of several of your Sincerity and Fidelity notwithstanding, provided those are present and in such number as by law ought to be present. *Given* etc.

Those Commission Letters of His Sacred Royal Majesty having been read out, at today's session assigned to the parties by Us, or by our summons letters, as commissioners from His Sacred Royal Majesty, the commissarial jurisdiction having been founded and proclaimed with all security of the present act by the court messenger of the Kingdom, the Honest [name] of the village of [name], authentic and sworn, in the presence of the Honourable and Noble [names] and others of various rank and condition assembled for this act; the tenor of which summons letters issued to the Right Honourable Captain or leaseholder of the royal estates of the town of [name] and the villages belonging thereto, follows in this form: ^[*Summons letters to the Right Honourable Captain from the commissioners.*] We, [names] [names], commissioners designated and deputed by His Sacred Royal Majesty to perform the matters written below, give notice to you, the Right Honourable or Honourable [names], leaseholder or Captain of the royal estates of [name]: that the Commission Letters of His Sacred Royal Majesty have been presented to us, by which His Royal Majesty has seen fit to commit and command us to compose the differences occasioned by the boundaries between the above-named royal estates of [name], presently Your leaseholding, on the one part; and the hereditary estates of the village of [name] of the Honourable [names],

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-89">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-89">p. 89</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

Hereditaria ex altera, Partibus componamus, Scopulisque; & signis metallicis, & alijs circa talia, fieri Solitis, excepto, primo a senibus fide dignis sufficienti testimonio, mediante ipsorum si opus fuerit, corporali Juramento, distingvamus, consignemus, dislimitemusque; literis Commissionis ipsius, de quibus Tibi in Termino advenienti dicetur de his in suo tenore latioribus. Quocirca Tibi & Communitati subditorum Villæ prætactæ N. vigore Regio, & nostra qua hac in parte fungimur Commissionali Authoritate, mandamus, ut coram Nobis, Judicioque; Nostro Commissionali, communitas vera subditorum cum Assistentia Tui, uti Tenutarij Feria N. post Festum N. proxima, proxime ventura in loco Differentiarum præfatarum, ubi a Parte Actorea, locati & constituti fuerimus, ad Instantiam præfati Generosi NN. Villæ N. Heredis Actoris personaliter, legitime, peremptorieque; compareatis, seu compareas, Negotiumque; Dislimitationis Bonorum præfatorum, judicialiter attentes, & si quid de Jure competierit, respondeas: certificando Te, quod si in Termino præfixo comparueris, sive non, nos nihilominus ad ea quæ nobis ex præscripto legum incumbunt, procedemus. Contumacia Tui & subditorum præfatorum Bonorum Regalium, Possesioni Tuæ subjectorum, & Contradictione non obstante. Datum in Locis Residentiarum Nostrarum Feria N. proxima Anno Dni NN.

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

on the other part; to distinguish them by mounds and metal markers and others customarily done in such matters, except that first the sufficient testimony of elderly witnesses of good repute shall be received, by their bodily oath if necessary; and to mark and delimit them — of the Commission Letters themselves, of which fuller account will be given to You at the approaching session, in their tenor. Wherefore We command You and the community of the subjects of the aforesaid village of [name], by royal authority and Our own commissarial authority which We exercise in this matter, that You and the true community of the subjects, with Your assistance as leaseholder, appear personally, legitimately, and peremptorily before Us and Our commissarial court, on the [day] next after the feast of [name], next and immediately to come, at the aforesaid place of the differences, where We shall have been situated and stationed by the plaintiff's party, at the instance of the aforesaid Honourable [names], heir of the village of [name], plaintiff; and that You judicially attempt the matter of the delimitation of the aforesaid estates, and respond to whatever by law shall be due. Certifying You that whether You appear or not at the fixed session, We shall nonetheless proceed to those matters that are incumbent upon Us from the prescription of the laws. Your contumacy and that of the subjects of the aforesaid royal estates subject to Your possession, and Your opposition, notwithstanding. Given at Our places of residence on the [day] next, in the year of the Lord [names].

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-90">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-90">p. 90</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

^[*Mandatum S.R.M. M. Capitaneo.*] Literarum vero Mandati Sacræ Regiæ Majestatis eidem Magnifico Capitaneo dati, series est talis: AUGUSTUS Tertius Dei Gratia Rex Poloniæ, Magnus Dux Lithvaniæ, Russiæ, Prussiæ, Masowiæ, Samogotiæ Kijowiæ, Volhyniæ, Podolæ, Podlachiæ, Livoniæ Smolensciæ, Severiæ, Czerniechoviæque; nec non Hereditarius Dux Saxoniæ, Princeps & Elector.

Magnifico seu Generoso Bonorum nostrorum NN. Capitaneo, seu Tenutario, sincere & fideliter nobis dilecto. Designavimus certos Commissarios Nostros, ad faciendos & constituendos Limites, inter Bona nostra Regalia Oppidi seu Villæ N. Tenutæ Fidelitatis Tuæ ab una, & Bona Villæ N. Generosi seu Nobilis N. hereditaria ex altera Partibus: Quapropter requirimus Fidelitatem Tuam, ut quam primum Literis Innotescentiæ per prædictos Commissarios Nostros vocata fuerit, in loco Differentiarum præfatarum coram illis compareat, Bonorum Dislimitationi intersit, & quidquid in eo negotio decreverint, firmiter teneat, & observet. Provideat tamen ne Bonis nostris Regalibus aliquid Detrimenti ea Dislimitatione inferatur. Pro gratia Nostra. Datum etc. Cujus Mandati recognita Relatio, Positionis in Bonis N. coram Actis Castrensibus N. Feria N. Anno Dni NN. per Ministerialem Regni generalem providum NN. de Villa N. una cum Duobus Nobilibus NN.

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

^[*Royal mandate to the Right Honourable Captain.*] The series of the Royal Mandate of His Sacred Royal Majesty given to the same Right Honourable Captain is as follows: AUGUSTUS THE THIRD, by the Grace of God King of Poland, Grand Duke of Lithuania, Ruthenia, Prussia, Masovia, Samogitia, Kiev, Volhynia, Podolia, Podlachia, Livonia, Smolensk, Severia, and Chernigov, and likewise Hereditary Duke of Saxony, Prince and Elector.

To the Right Honourable or Honourable [names] [names], Captain or leaseholder of Our estates, sincerely and faithfully beloved by Us. We have designated certain of Our commissioners to make and establish boundaries between Our royal estates of the town or village of [name] of the leaseholding of Your Fidelity on the one part, and the hereditary estates of the village of [name] of the Honourable or Noble [name] on the other part. Wherefore We require Your Fidelity, that as soon as it shall have been summoned by the summons letters of the aforesaid Our commissioners, it appear before them at the aforesaid place of the differences, be present at the delimitation of the estates, and keep and observe firmly whatever they shall decree in that matter. Let it take care, however, that no detriment be done to Our royal estates by that delimitation. For Our grace. *Given* etc. The service return of which mandate, acknowledged for service in the estates of [name] before the municipal records of [name] on the [day] of [name] in the year of the Lord [names], by the court messenger of the Kingdom, the Honest [names] of the village of [name], together with two noblemen [names].

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-91">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-91">p. 91</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

Postmodum emanatæ sunt literæ Innotescentiales, Magnificorum Commissariorum NN. contra Generosum N. Bonorum Villæ N. Heredem Collateralem, quarum positarum, Literarum, recognita relatio per eundem Ministerialem, coram Actis Castrensibus N. simul cum mandato Sacræ Regiæ Majestatis, ^[*Innotescentiales G. Collaterali.*] cujus tenor sequitur talis: Actum in Castro N. Feria N. Anno N. etc. (Scribatur ex Authentico Extractu Relationis) Nos &c Commissarij per Sacram Regiam Majestatem ad infrascripta peragenda assignati, & deputati. Tibi Generoso NN. Bonorum Villæ N. Heredi Collaterali: innotescimus, oblatas esse nobis Literas Commissionis Sacræ Regiæ Majestatis, quibus nobis committere, & mandare dignata est, quatenus Differentias occasione Limitum, inter Bona Regalia N. Tenutæ Magnifici NN. Capitanei N. ab una, & Bona Villæ N. Generosi N. Actoris Hereditaria. Partibus ab altera, componamus, distingvamus Scopulisque; & Signis metallicis circa talia fieri solitis, consignemus & dislimitemus. Quoniam vero Bona tua præfata, N. Bonis principaliter dislimitandis N. contigua esse feruntur: idcirco Tibi, vigore Regio & nostra Commissionali Authoritate mandamus, ut coram nobis Judicioque; nostro Commissionali Feria N. post Festum N. proximeque; ventura, in Loco Differentiarum præfatarum, ubi per præfatam Partem Actoream locati, &

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

Thereafter were issued the summons letters of the Right Honourable Commissioners [names] against the Honourable [name], heir of the estates of the village of [name], collateral heir; the acknowledged service return of those same served letters by the same court messenger, before the municipal records of [name], together with the mandate of His Sacred Royal Majesty; ^[*Summons letters to the Honourable collateral heir.*] the tenor of which follows in this form: Done at the castle of [name], on the [day] of [name] in the year [name] etc. (Written from the authentic extract of the service return.) We etc., commissioners designated and deputed by His Sacred Royal Majesty to perform the matters written below. To you, the Honourable [names], heir of the estates of the village of [name], collateral heir: we give notice that the Commission Letters of His Sacred Royal Majesty have been presented to us, by which His Majesty has seen fit to commit and command us to compose the differences occasioned by the boundaries between the royal estates of [name] of the leaseholding of the Right Honourable [names], Captain of [name], on the one part, and the hereditary estates of the village of [name] of the Honourable [name], plaintiff, on the other part; to distinguish them by mounds and metal markers customarily used in such matters, and to mark and delimit them. Since, however, your aforesaid estates of [name] are said to lie contiguous to the estates principally to be delimited: we therefore command You, by royal authority and Our commissarial authority which We exercise in this matter, that You appear personally and peremptorily before Us and Our commissarial court, on the [day] next after the feast of [name], next and immediately to come, at the aforesaid place of the differences, where We shall have been situated and

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-92">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-92">p. 92</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

Constituti fuerimus, ad instantiam suprascripti Heredis N. Actoris personaliter & peremptorie compareas; Negotium hoc Dislimitandorum Bonorum præfatorum, Judicialiter attentaturus, Parietemque; tuum a Bonis tuis hereditarijs Villæ N. & Acialitatem, seu Angularitatem, in Concurrentia trinarum Hereditatum existentem, agniturus, & sic quid de Jure competierit responsurus certificando Te, quod si in Termino præfixo comparueris, vel ne, Nos nihilominus ad ea, quæ Nobis ex Officio nostro, & præscripto Legum incumbunt, contra Te absentia, contumacia & contradictione Tua illegali non obstante procedemus. Datum in loco Residentiarum Nostrarum Feria N. etc.

Similiter Mandatum Sacræ Regiæ Majestatis eidem Citato Collaterali Villæ N. Heredi in eum eventum dati tenoris talis: AUGUSTUS Tertius etc. Scribatur Titulus ex superiori Actu.

Tibi Generoso NN. Bonorum Villæ N. Heredi Fid. nobis Dilecto. Generose Fid. Nobis Dilecte. Designavimus certos Nostros Commissarios ad faciendos Limites inter Bona Nostra Regalia Oppidi N. & Villarum eo pertinentium Tenutæ ad præsens Magnifici seu Generosi NN. & N. Nobilis NN. Hereditaria ex altera Partibus. Quoniam vero Bona suprascripta N. Fid. Tuæ hereditaria, eisdem Bonis dislimitandis contigua adjacere dicuntur, ideo Fid. Tuæ

</div>

stationed by the aforesaid plaintiff's party, at the instance of the above-named heir [name], plaintiff; and that You judicially attempt this matter of the delimitation of the aforesaid estates, and acknowledge Your boundary wall from Your hereditary estates of the village of [name] and Your corner point or particular corner point existing at the convergence of three estates, and respond to whatever by law shall be due. Certifying You that whether You appear at the fixed session or not, We shall nonetheless proceed to those matters that are incumbent upon Us from Our office and the prescription of the laws, against You — Your absence, contumacy, and unlawful opposition notwithstanding. *Given at Our place of residence on the [day]* etc.

Likewise the Royal Mandate of His Sacred Royal Majesty given to the same cited collateral heir of the village of [name], of the following tenor: AUGUSTUS THE THIRD etc. The title shall be written from the foregoing act.

To the Honourable [names], heir of the estates of the village of [name], faithfully beloved by Us. Honourable, faithfully beloved by Us. We have designated certain of Our commissioners to make boundaries between Our royal estates of the town of [name] and the villages belonging to it, presently leased to the Right Honourable or Honourable [names] and [name], Noble [names], and his hereditary estates on the other part. Since, however, the above-named hereditary estates of [name] of Your Fidelity are said to lie adjacent to those same estates to be delimited, We therefore command Your Fidelity

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-93">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-93">p. 93</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

mandamus, ut quam primum Literis Innotescentiæ per prædictos Commissarios nostros vocatus fueris in locum Differentiarum coram illis compareas, Parietemque; Bonorum tuorum attentes, & ubi agnoscendus erit, agnoscas: Quidquid autem in eo Negotio decreverint, firmiter inviolabiliterque; teneas, & observes: Pro Gratia Nostra: Datum Varfaviæ etc. (Hoc notandum, si plures sunt Collaterales, in altero confinio Parietis, similiter adcitentur per Innotescentiales & Mandatum)

Post quam Ingrossationem, Judicium Commissariale Partes ad sui comparendum, acclamare fecit, quibus acclamatis in eo loco, personaliter adveniens Generosus NN. Bonorum Villæ N. Heres; non agnoscendo hic in eo loco veram concurrentiam trinarum ^[*Protestatur Collateralis non agnoscendo Actori in eo loco concurrentiam trinarum Hereditatum & Angularitatem.*] Hereditatum, sed alibi, ubi se demonstraturum offert, & hi Scopuli, quos Pars Actorea sumit sibi pro Angularibus, non sunt veri Scopuli sed Monticuli, & eo nomine, reproduxit coram suo Judicio Commissionali, Granicies seu Actum Officiosum, ex Actis Succamerarialibus N. depromptum, de Actu ejus N. Anno N. ex quibus elicitur in alio loco Angularitatem, seu Acialitatem horum Bonorum exstasse, & eo nomine Protestatus est contra eundem Generosum NN. Actorem, de indebita locatione Judicij sui, uti in proprio Fundo ejus, nulli Controversiæ obnoxio, non nisi in avulsionem fundi sui Protestantis

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

that as soon as You shall have been summoned by the summons letters of the aforesaid Our commissioners, You appear before them at the place of the differences, attempt the boundary wall of Your estates, and where it is to be acknowledged, acknowledge it. Whatever, moreover, they shall decree in that matter, keep and observe it firmly and inviolably. For Our grace. *Given at Warsaw* etc. (It is to be noted that if there are several collateral heirs on the other boundary of the boundary wall, they are likewise to be summoned by summons letters and mandate.)

After which enrolment, the commissarial court caused the parties to be called to appear. Those having been called, the Honourable [names], heir of the estates of the village of [name], personally arriving at that place, ^[*The collateral heir protests, not acknowledging for the plaintiff the convergence of three estates and the corner point at that place.*] not acknowledging there the true convergence of three estates, but elsewhere, where he offers to demonstrate it; and the mounds which the plaintiff's party takes as corner mounds for itself are not true mounds but hillocks — and on that account reproduced before his commissarial court the boundaries or official act drawn from the Sub-Chamberlain's records of [name], of the act of [name] in the year [name], from which it appears that the corner point or particular corner point of those estates existed in another place, and on that account protested against the same Honourable [names], plaintiff, regarding the improper founding of his court, as being on his own property, subject to no dispute, proceeding only to the avulsion of the hereditary estate of the protestant

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-94">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-94">p. 94</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

^[*Reprotestatur Actor de indebita retrusione.*] Hereditarij, procedendo. Reprotestatus est Generosus NN. Actor, contra Generosum N. de indebita ejus Retrusione tam sui Actoris quam Judicij Commissionalis ex eo loco fundatæ Jurisdictionis, allegando, quod is Actus Granicialis, quem Pars Collateralis producit, minime spectat, ad hunc locum fundatæ Jurisdictionis, verum ad aliam Angularitatem & Parietem ejusdem Villæ ejus Hereditariæ, ratione cujus indebitæ Remonstrationis solenniter protestatus est. Similiter^[*Protestatur Capitaneus non agnoscendo Angularitatem in loco fundatam Jurisdictionis.*] Protestatur Magnificus Capitaneus, non agnoscendo in eo loco fundatæ Jurisdictionis, veram esse Angularitatem, neque; concurrentiam trinarum Hereditatum præfatarum, sed alibi, ubi in Ductu suo demonstraturum se offert & declarat. Quibus connotatis Partium Protestationibus, & Reprotestationibus, ijsdem Partibus, Comparitionem inscribere mandat. Et quidem Actorea Generoso^[*Comparitio*] NN. personaliter Magnifico NN. Capitaneo N. ac laboriosis NN. Advocato, totaque; Communitate Colonorum præfatæ Villæ Regalis, citatis, in assistentia Domini sui, personaliter, Generoso NN. Bonorum Villæ N. Collateralium Herede itidem personaliter, suo coram Judicio comparentibus, & secum Judicialiter Controvertentibus, Judicium Commissoriale Sacræ Regiæ Majestatis, sufficienti Comparitione adinveta Partibus ijsdem in Causa præsenti experiri mandat, In experimento Controversijs exauditis ijsdemque;

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

^[*The plaintiff counter-protests regarding the improper repulsion.*] The Honourable [names], plaintiff, counter-protested against the Honourable [name] regarding his improper repulsion of both the plaintiff himself and the commissarial court from the place of the founded jurisdiction, alleging that the boundary act which the collateral party produces does not bear upon this place of the founded jurisdiction at all, but upon another corner point and boundary wall of that same hereditary village; regarding which improper demonstration he solemnly protested. ^[*The Captain likewise protests, not acknowledging the corner point at the place of the founded jurisdiction.*] The Right Honourable Captain likewise protests, not acknowledging at the place of the founded jurisdiction the true corner point, nor the convergence of the three aforesaid estates, but elsewhere, where he offers and declares he will demonstrate it in his boundary path. Having noted the protests and counter-protests of the parties, he orders the registration of appearance for those same parties. The plaintiff's party with ^[*Appearance.*] the Honourable [names] in person; the Right Honourable [names], Captain of [name], and the Honest [names], advocate, and the whole community of the settlers of the aforesaid royal village, cited, in the assistance of their master, in person; the Honourable [names], heir of the collateral estates of the village of [name], likewise in person; appearing before his court and judicially contesting the matter between themselves. The Royal commissarial court of His Sacred Royal Majesty, finding the appearance of the parties sufficient, orders those same parties to proceed to trial in the present case. In the trial, the contested submissions having been heard and

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-95">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-95">p. 95</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

bene ac diligenter trutinatis, suspensa Decisione Angularitatis, seu Acialitatis, præfatorum Bonorum, ^[*merito suspensa decisione Angularitatis.*] uti tam per Generosum NN. Collateralem, quam Magnificum Capitaneum, non agnitæ, imo denegatæ, ad Revisionem Ductuum per Partes demonstrandorum, ^[*Ad revisionem Ductuum Judicium accessit.*] Judicium se se contulit. Tandem vergente sole ad occasum, Actum suum Commissorialem ad diem crastinam Ministeriali Regni generali provido NN. Judicij sui Apparitori, reclamare injunxit. Tandem adveniente die crastina, Feria tertia post Festum NN. Anno præsenti, reassumpta Jurisdictione sua Commissionali, & per præfatum Ministerialem acclamata, ad Revisionem Ductus primo Partis Actoreæ sese contulit, & eandem sequi declaravit. Et imprimis inchoavit Ductum suum Pars Actorea, ^[*Ductus Partis Actoreæ.*] a loco fundatæ Jurisdictionis, & ab ijsdem Scopulis tribus Angularibus, ab occidente, ad orientem, quanto rectius eundo, demonstravit Judicio, Monticulum, Scopulo similem, quinquaginta passibus ab Angularibus distantem, in initio Borræ, densæ, vario arborum genere excretæ, contra quam Demonstrationem Scopuli protestati sunt, tam Magnificus Capitaneus, quam subditi ejus, negando esse verum Scopulum, sed Monticulum, a formicis exstructum, & in verificationem, ejus referationem expetijt, cujus Affectationi de Jure concessibili annuendo, eundem referare Judicium demandavit, in quo

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

weighed carefully and diligently; the decision of the corner point or particular corner point ^[*The decision of the corner point duly suspended.*] of the aforesaid estates being suspended — as not having been acknowledged, indeed denied, both by the Honourable [names], collateral heir, and by the Right Honourable Captain — ^[*The court proceeded to the review of the boundary paths.*] the court proceeded to the review of the boundary paths to be demonstrated by the parties. The sun at last declining toward the west, he ordered the commissarial act to be adjourned to the morrow by the court messenger of the Kingdom, the Honest [names], apparitor of his court. The morrow arriving, namely the Tuesday after the feast of [names] in the present year, the commissarial jurisdiction having been reassumed and proclaimed by the aforesaid court messenger, he proceeded to the review of the plaintiff's party's boundary path first, and declared that it should follow the same. And first the plaintiff's party commenced its boundary path ^[*The plaintiff's party's boundary path.*] from the place of the founded jurisdiction and from those same three corner mounds, from the west toward the east, as straight as possible; and demonstrated to the court a hillock resembling a mound, fifty paces from the corner mounds, at the edge of a dense pine forest grown with various kinds of trees; against which demonstration of the mound, both the Right Honourable Captain and his subjects protested, denying it to be a true mound but a hillock built by ants; and in verification thereof requested its opening, granting which application as allowable by law,

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-96">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-96">p. 96</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

referato nil inventum nonnisi formicæ, cum terra mixta, ulterius ad eandem plagam orientis eundo, devenit in eadem Borra ad locum dictum *Czarny Smug,* in quo loco Judicio demonstravit, Monticulum ad instar Scopuli nullum circulum, nec rotunditatem habentem; quo ad affectationem Partis referato, nil repertum, ulterius ad eandem plagam Ductum suum continuando, ad loca Lutosa, madida, quæ cum difficili transitu transgrediendo, *Białe Ługi* vocitata, Demonstravit Judicio, in loco elevatiori, Monticulum oblongum, non vero Scopulo similem, & similiter eodem referato, nulla sunt Signa Metallia reperta, & ulterius Ductu eodem exciso, Borra præfata progrediendo, curvatim non vero rectilinio versus orientem hiemalem, declinando se in hanc & aliam partem, demonstravit Judicio Monticulum, non postremæ altitudinis, sumendo eundem sibi, pro Parietali Scopulo, ratione cujus Remonstrationis, Protestatus est Magnificus Capitaneus, de illegali ostensione, asserendo eundem, per eversionem arboris, in radicibus excretum esse, quo referato affectante Parte, inventi sunt radices arboris eversæ, & Pars trunci putrefacti. In ulteriori tractu Ductus sui in partem meridionalem sese declinando, devenit ad Fluviolum, a Molendino N. Villæ N. profluentem, quem Fluviolum sumpsit sibi Pars Actorea, pro Pariete & Limite naturali, & eodem Fluviolo

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

in which, when opened, nothing was found save ants mixed with earth. Proceeding further toward the same eastern quarter, through that same pine forest, the court reached a place called *Czarny Smug*, at which place it demonstrated to the court a hillock in the manner of a mound, having no circle or roundness; in which, when opened at the application of the party, nothing was found. Continuing its boundary path further toward the same quarter, and crossing with difficulty the boggy and waterlogged places called *Białe Ługi*, it demonstrated to the court, in a more elevated place, an oblong hillock, not resembling a mound at all; and likewise, when the same was opened, no metal markers were found. And proceeding further through the same pine forest with the path cleared, curving and not in a straight line toward the winter east, inclining this way and that, it demonstrated to the court a hillock of no small height, taking it as a wall mound; regarding which demonstration the Right Honourable Captain protested regarding the unlawful display, asserting that it had grown from the roots of a felled tree; the party having requested the opening, the roots of the felled tree and part of a rotted trunk were found therein. Continuing its boundary path further and inclining toward the southern quarter, the court reached a small river flowing from the mill of [name] of the village of [name]; which small river the plaintiff's party took as the boundary wall and natural boundary, and proceeding along that same small river

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-97">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-97">p. 97</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

ducente, ferme quarta parte milliaris, ex eodem egrediendo, imo omisso, locis siccis fruticetosis, tribus, vel quatuor stadijs proficiscendo, demonstravit Judicio fossam manu hominis erutam, allegando, eandem Granicialem esse, & Parietem repræsentantem, quo in loco protestatus est Magnificus Capitaneus contra præfatum Generosum NN. de illegali ductione Judicij Commissionalis, & demonstrationis Ductus, illegaliter, allegando, quod is Fluviolus, a Molendino Capitaneatus profluens, non est Limes naturalis, præfatarum Hereditatum, quia ejus utrasque; Ripæ, & fundus est proprius, Bonorum Regalium, & ad Villam N. spectat, quam etiam eadem Fossa, non est Granicialis, non nisi circa argillam, ad præsens fruticetosam, erecta. Qua Fossa uno stadio tantum tendente, devenit ad locum, dictum *Ostra Górká,* in quo demonstravit Scopulos Aciales, seu Angulares, Parietem suæ Hereditatis, & Bonorum Regalium Villæ N. concludentes, & determinantes, circa Acialem Scopulum Collateralis Generosi NN. Bonorum Villæ N. Heredis, determinavit, Ductum suum. Contra quem totum Ductum protestatus est^[*Protestatur M. Capitaneus contra totum Ductum Partis Actoreæ.*] idem Magnificus Capitaneus, tum de nullitate ejusdem, uti obliquitatem summam in se continentis, primo ad orientem, ac post ad meridiem, in hanc & in aliam partem sese declinando curvatim, partim Fundis proprijs, Bonorum Regalium, Hereditatis

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

for almost one quarter of a mile, and thereafter leaving it and proceeding through dry scrubland for three or four furlongs, it demonstrated to the court a ditch dug by human hand, alleging it to be a boundary ditch representing the boundary wall; at which place the Right Honourable Captain protested against the aforesaid Honourable [names] regarding the unlawful conduct of the commissarial court and the illegal demonstration of the boundary path, alleging that the small river flowing from the Captain's mill is not the natural boundary of the aforesaid estates, since both its banks and bed are the proper property of the royal estates and belong to the village of [name]; and likewise that the ditch is not a boundary ditch, but has been erected only around a clay deposit, at present grown over with scrub. That ditch extending for only one furlong, the court reached a place called *Ostra Górka*, at which it demonstrated particular corner mounds or corner mounds, concluding and determining the boundary wall of its estate and of the royal estates of the village of [name]; and beside the particular corner mound of the collateral heir, the Honourable [names], heir of the estates of the village of [name], determined its boundary path. Against that entire boundary path the same Right Honourable Captain protested ^[*The Captain protests against the entire boundary path of the plaintiff's party.*] — regarding its nullity as containing the greatest obliqueness, first toward the east and then toward the south, inclining this way and that in a curve, conducted partly through the proper plots of the royal estates of the estate

</section>

^[*Determinatio Ductus Partis Actoreæ.*] Villæ N. partim Fluviolo Parietaliter pro Limite sumpto, nullis Documentis firmati, neque; Scopulis realibus demonstrati. In quo loco, petijt eadem Pars Actorea ad Judicium Illustris & Magnificorum Commissariorum, Generosum NN. Villæ N. Heredem, tanquam determinati Ductus, ultimum Collateralem acclamari, juxta Literas Innotescentiæ Commissionalis, ad sui Instantiam, contra eundem editas, tenoris talis (Inferatur similis & conformis, prout contra primum principij Ductus, Collateralem, Innotescentiæ, mutato nomine, & cognomine, & Bonorum ipsius, tum & vigore mandati Sacræ Regiæ Majestatis, ad eundem Citatum, eo fine directum. Simile quoque; Mandatum ut supra) Cujus postulationi Illustris & Magnifici Commissarij præfatum Generosum NN. Villæ N. Heredem, ultimum Collateralem, ad Judicium suum acclamari mandaverunt; ^[*Agnitio Angularitatis per Collateralem ultimum seu Acialitatis.*] Qui acclamatus, per Ministerialem suprascriptum, in eo loco personaliter comparuit, & ibidem in eodem loco *Ostra Górká* dicto, concurrentiam Parietum trinarum Hereditatum, seu veram Acialitatem, Villæ N. ^[*Determination of the plaintiff's party's boundary path.*] of the village of [name], partly with the small river taken wall-fashion as a natural boundary, confirmed by no documents and demonstrated by no real mounds. At which place the same plaintiff's party requested of the court of the Illustrious and Right Honourable Commissioners that the Honourable [names], heir of the village of [name], as the final collateral heir of the determined boundary path, be called to the court, pursuant to the commissarial summons letters issued at his instance against the same, of the following tenor: (Similar and conforming summons letters as against the first collateral heir at the commencement of the boundary path shall be inserted, with the name and surname and estates changed, and likewise by virtue of the royal mandate directed to that same cited heir for that purpose; a similar mandate as above.) To which application the Illustrious and Right Honourable Commissioners ordered the aforesaid Honourable [names], heir of the village of [name], as the final collateral heir, to be called to their court; ^[*Acknowledgement of the corner point or particular corner point by the final collateral heir.*] who, called by the aforesaid court messenger, appeared in person at that place, and there at that same place called *Ostra Górka*, acknowledged the convergence of the boundary walls of three estates, or the true particular corner point, of the village of [name], hereditary property of the Honourable plaintiff, and of the village of [name] of the royal estates, and of his own third collateral estate;

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-99">

& Graniciebus Anni NN. probavit.

Ductu itaque; Partis Actoreæ Judicium Commissoriale reviso, & fideliter connotato, ad Revisionem Ductus Magnifici Capitanei Citati sese contulit. Et^[*Ductus M. Capitanei.*] imprimis incepit Ductum suum ab hac Angularitate, seu Acialitate, per Generosum NN. Collateralem agnita, & processit versus septemtrionem, a meridie, & devenit ad Sylvam Quercineam, *Dąbrowá* vocitatam, & ibidem penes viam, non adeo tritam, de Villa N. ad Villam N. tendentem, demonstravit Judicio Monticulum, allegando esse primum Scopulum Parietalem, ferme tribus stadijs, ab eadem Angularitate distantem, cujus referationem Pars Actorea affectavit. In quo referato, nil repertum, non nisi terra videtur esse mixta, a quo Scopulo Rectilinio progrediendo eadem Sylva Quercinea, devenit ad vias transversales, quæ tendunt, una earum ex Oppido N. ad Villam N. altera de Villa N. ad Villam N. inter quas vias ostendit Judicio Monticulum ad instar Scopuli, quo referato, nil inventum est, ulteriori Ductu procedendo, ab eodem referato acsi Scopulo, declinando se in mediam plagam orientis & septemtrionis, adduxit Judicium ad locum *Białe Ługi* lutosum & madidum, & confinio eadem Loca transgrediendo, demonstravit Judicio Incisionem, in Arbore Betulinea, ad instar Crucis, & ab eadem Arbore versus Septemtrionem procedendo Sylva, partim

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

and proved it by the boundaries of the year [names].

The plaintiff's boundary path having been reviewed and faithfully noted by the commissarial court, it proceeded to the review of the Right Honourable Captain's boundary path. And ^[*The Right Honourable Captain's boundary path.*] first he commenced his boundary path from this corner point or particular corner point, acknowledged by the Honourable [names], collateral heir, and proceeded toward the north from the south, and reached a oak forest called *Dąbrowa*, and there beside a road not much frequented running from the village of [name] to the village of [name], demonstrated to the court a hillock, alleging it to be the first wall mound, almost three furlongs from that same corner point; the plaintiff's party requested its opening; in which, when opened, nothing was found save what appears to be mixed earth. From that mound, proceeding in a straight line through that same oak forest, he reached transverse roads — one running from the town of [name] to the village of [name], the other from the village of [name] to the village of [name] — between which roads he showed the court a hillock in the manner of a mound; in which, when opened, nothing was found. Proceeding further along the boundary path from that opened, allegedly mound, inclining toward the midpoint between the east and the north, he led the court to the boggy and waterlogged place *Białe Ługi*, and crossing the boundary of that same place, demonstrated to the court an incision in a birch tree in the manner of a cross; and from that tree proceeding toward the north through a forest grown partly

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-100">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-100">p. 100</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

Pinatica, partim Quercinea, partim Coryllis, excreta, ferme sex stadijs, ab illa arbore Betulinea, demonstravit Judicio similem incisionem in arbore Acerna, quas incisiones Parietem designantes, allegavit. Protestatus est Generosus NN. Actor, contra illegalem demonstrationem Magnifici Capitanei, & subditorum ejus, tam Monticulorum, quam incisionum noviter non pridiem per subditos ejus factarum, ratione cujus illiciti aufus, Protestationem & visionem Ministerialis una cum Nobilibus NN. earundem incisionum, coram Actis Castrensibus N. Anno N. intulit, & præsenti in Termino probat.

Reprotestante Magnifico Capitaneo, quod eædem incisiones antiquitus factæ sunt, & Scopuli vere reales, quia antiquitas non observabat signa metallia imponere. Connotatis itaque; Protestationibus adduxit Judicium ad confinium Sylvæ ejusdem, & sub ipsa Sylva, versus campos, & agros Hereditatis *Villæ* N. Ductum suum, in concurrentia trinarum Hereditatum, ^[*Determinatio Ductus M. Capitanei.*] attentante Parte Collateraliter Adcitata, & eadem agnoscente, Ductum suum determinavit. Protestatur Pars Actorea de nullitate, & invaliditate Ductus totalis, uti oblique nec Per Quem, nec ad Quem, determinati.

Quibus finitis & revisis Ductibus Partium utrarumque; Actum præsentem declinante sole ad occasum ad diem crastinam limitat, & eandem Ministeriali

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

of pine, partly of oak, partly of hazel, for almost six furlongs from that birch tree, demonstrated to the court a similar incision in a maple tree, alleging those incisions as marking the boundary wall. The Honourable [names], plaintiff, protested against the unlawful demonstration by the Right Honourable Captain and his subjects — both of the hillocks and of the incisions freshly made not long ago by his subjects; regarding which unlawful act, he produced a protest and an inspection by the court messenger together with noblemen [names] of those same incisions, before the municipal records of [name] in the year [name], and proves it at the present session.

The Right Honourable Captain counter-protesting that those same incisions were made from antiquity, and the mounds are truly real, because antiquity was not in the habit of placing metal markers. Having thus noted the protests, he led the court to the boundary of that same forest, and beneath the very forest, toward the fields and plots of the estate of the village of [name], at the convergence of three estates, ^[*Determination of the Right Honourable Captain's boundary path.*] with the collaterally summoned party present and acknowledging the same, determined his boundary path. The plaintiff's party protests the nullity and invalidity of the entire boundary path, as having been conducted obliquely, neither through the intermediate points nor to the end point.

Both boundary paths of the parties having thus been concluded and reviewed, he limits the present act as the sun declines toward the west, to the morrow, and orders the court messenger

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-101">
