reclamare demandat. Tandem adveniente die crastina, Feria N. Anno N. reassumpta itaque; Jurisdictione, ex suprascripta Limitatione incidenti, Partibus^[*In prosecutione.*] in Causa procedere injunxit. In Prosecutione causæ, ad sufficientiorem Probationem Ductuum, necessariam ^[*Injuncta Inquisitio.*] esse Inquisitionem adinvenit decernendo, quatenus Partes utrasque; tum Collateralis Generosus NN. in ambigua Angularitate se sistens, & quidem Partes principaliter agentes, tam respectu Angularitatis, quam universalis Ductus. Jam vero Generosus NN. Collateralis, quo ad solam Angularitatem, seu Acialitatem sui, ubi nam reperiri poterit vera concurrentia trinarum Hereditatum, eandem Inquisitionem expediant, & Testes inducant, præevijs porrigendis Interrogatorijs, & sibi abinvicem communicandis, idque; in instanti, & super Incorruptionem Testium, additur Ministerialis, ad pronunciandam Juramenti Rotham. Quod Juramentum, siquidem Partes, tam principaliter agentes, quam Collateralis, in facie Judicij sui, ad Imaginem Crucifixi Domini, Rothisante sibi Ministeriali præstiterunt. Proinde Judicium ad expediendam eandem Inquisitionem processit, & Partibus Testes, primo Actoreæ, ac post Citatæ, inducere mandat. Induxerunt, eduxerunt, & Juramentum præstiterunt. Quibus eductis, & expeditis Inquisitionibus, ad lectionem earundem condescendit. Quibus lectis, & per omnes circumstantias, matura deliberatione

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

to adjourn the same. The morrow arriving, namely the [day] of [name] in the year [name], the jurisdiction having been reassumed from the abovementioned limiting incident, ^[*In the prosecution.*] he ordered the parties to proceed in the case. In the prosecution of the case, he finds ^[*A sworn inquiry enjoined.*] that a sworn inquiry is necessary for more sufficient proof of the boundary paths; decreeing that both parties, and likewise the collateral heir, the Honourable [names], standing in the position of an uncertain corner point — and indeed the principal acting parties in respect both of the corner point and of the general boundary path; and the Honourable [names], collateral heir, as regards his corner point or particular corner point only, where the true convergence of three estates may be found — shall conduct that sworn inquiry and call witnesses, with prior interrogatories drawn up and communicated to one another, and this immediately; and the court messenger is added for the pronouncing of the oath formula regarding the impartiality of the witnesses. Which oath, since the parties — both the principal acting parties and the collateral heir — before the face of his court, before the image of the Crucified Lord, with the court messenger reading the formula to them, swore; he therefore proceeded to conduct that same sworn inquiry, and orders the parties to call witnesses — first for the plaintiff's party and then for the cited party. They called, produced, and swore. The sworn inquiries having been produced and concluded, he descended to the reading thereof. Which having been read and weighed and examined in all their circumstances with the mature deliberation

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-102">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-102">p. 102</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

Judicij sui perpensis, & trutinatis, siquidem utrasque;^[*Decretum Definitivi.*] Partes deficiunt in Probationibus, id circo Judicium resolvendo suspensionem cognitionis Angularitatis, seu Acialitatis, Bonorum principaliter agentium, & Generosi NN. Collateralis inante factam, reprobatis earundem utrarumque; Partium Ductibus, uti nullis Documentis literatorijs, nec Inquisitionibus probatis, non nisi in sola & una Angularitate, Partes utrasque; & Collateralis sibi agnoverunt concurrentiam trinarum Hereditatum: Jam vero Ductus oblique una Pars, alteri, per Fundos proprios continuasse, loco Scopulorum Monticulos, Judicio demonstrasse, & ferme per quatuor partes orbis, primo versus Orientem, ac post versus meridiem, & occidentem, a plaga septemtrionali incipiendo Ductus suos expedivisse, proinde ex rationibus præallegatis, Judicium Commissoriale ex re oculis subjecta, tum in Parte ex testimonijs Juratorijs senum, neutri Partium favendo, solum DEUM & Conscientiam sui, præ oculis habendo, Ductum ex arbitrio sui dirigere, & Scopulos erigere (præcluso Juramento, ne perjuria per quamcunque; Partem fiant) declarat, & locum dictum NN. pro Scopulis Angularibus ijsdem suprafatis Hereditatibus, assignat, & ad sipationem Scopulorum, primo Angularium, in eodem loco assignato, Judicium accessit, & quidem primo Parti Actoreæ & Hereditati ejus, sipatus est Scopulus Angularis, ad orientem,

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

of his court — since both ^[*Final decree.*] parties are wanting in their proofs — the court, resolving the suspension of the cognisance of the corner point or particular corner point of the principal acting parties' estates and of the Honourable [names], collateral heir, made previously: both those same parties' boundary paths having been rejected as proved by no documentary evidence or sworn inquiries — save that in the one and only corner point both parties and the collateral heir acknowledged to one another the convergence of three estates; whereas in the boundary paths one party had continued obliquely into the other's proper plots, demonstrated hillocks to the court in place of mounds, and conducted its boundary paths through almost all four quarters of the compass, starting from the north and going first toward the east, then toward the south and west — the commissarial court, for the reasons alleged above, from the thing presented to the eyes and in part from the sworn testimonies of elderly witnesses, favouring neither party and having only GOD and its conscience before its eyes, declares that it directs the boundary path at the arbitration of its court and erects the mounds (the oath barred, so that perjuries may not occur on the part of either party); and assigns the place called [names] for the corner mounds of those same aforesaid estates, and the court proceeded to the erection of mounds — first the corner mounds at that same assigned place; and indeed first for the plaintiff's party and its estate a corner mound was raised toward the east,

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-103">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-103">p. 103</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

Bonis vero Regalibus, ad Occidentem, Generoso vero NN. Collaterali & Hereditati ejus Villæ N. ad septemtrionem, cum impositione Signorum metallicorum, videlicet: carbonum, laterum, & dimoscorio ferrei, & lagenarum vitrearum millio impletarum, cum Descriptione in charta pergamenea, Actus præsentis, per quem fit Dislimitatio horum Bonorum, Anni & Actus ut Posteritati maneat, in casu alicujus exoriundæ Quæstionis. Et ab eadem Angularitate, versus meridiem sipatus est Scopulus primus Parietalis, octuaginta passibus ab Angularitate distans, (& sic scribatur, & accurate describatur, Ductus, & sipatio Scopulorum, ut quadret Descriptio ejusdem cum Positione locorum,) & devenit ad Angulares, seu Aciales Scopulos, in Plaga meridionali situatos, juxta agnitionem Collateralis & eosdem Scopulos renovare injunxit, cum impositione signorum metallicorum, prout in primæva Angularitate impositorum. Quibus rite ac legitime determinatis Graniciebus, Illustris & Magnifici Commissarij, Authoritate sua, easdem Granicies Partibus tenere, & inviolabiliter observare demandant, & ijsde robur perpetuæ & inviolabilis firmitatis habere volunt, & declarant, præsentibusque; decernunt, ita ut quidquid est, fundi Bonorum hereditariorum ab Angularitate primæva, usque; ad determinationem Acialitatis, in parte dextra, id totum ad Hereditatem Villæ N.

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

for the royal estates toward the west, and for the Honourable [names], collateral heir, and his estate of the village of [name] toward the north; with the placing of metal markers in each of them, namely charcoal, brick, and iron slag, and glass bottles filled with millet, with a description in parchment of the present act by which the delimitation of those estates is made, with the year and act, so that it may remain for posterity in the event of any question arising. And from that same corner point, toward the south a first wall mound was raised, eighty paces from the corner point; (and so the boundary path and the erection of the mounds shall be written out and accurately described, so that the description thereof corresponds with the position of the places); and the court reached the particular corner mounds or corner mounds situated in the southern quarter, pursuant to the acknowledgement of the collateral heir, and ordered those same mounds to be renewed with the placing of metal markers, as placed at the original corner point. The boundaries having been duly and legitimately determined, the Illustrious and Right Honourable Commissioners by their authority order the parties to keep those same boundaries and observe them inviolably, and will and declare that they shall have the force of perpetual and inviolable validity; and decree in the present that whatever is on the right side of the hereditary estates from the original corner point to the determination of the particular corner point shall belong entirely to the estate of the village of [name].

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-104">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-104">p. 104</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

Terrestris, quidquid vero in parte & manu sinistra, similiter totum, ad Bona præfata Villæ N. Sacræ Regiæ Majestatis, perpetuis Temporibus spectare & pertinere debeat: Eo etiam cauto & præcultodito, ut Hereditatum prædictarum ad præsens dislimitatarum Heredes & Tenutarij mutua sibi ab utrinque; in Fundis suis (circa tamen damnum) non denegent pascua, imo sese circa eadem confervent, & Succedanei eorum temporibus perpetuis: Quod Ministeriali suprascripto publicare & proclamare mandaverunt, Decreti præsentis vigore.

##### *Decretum Commissoriale, ex ferijs Partium Controversijs, una cum Juramento, Parti Ductum Lucrati, injuncto.*

Actum in Borra seu Sylva vario arborum genere excreta N. vocitata, in loco Differentiarum, inter Bona Villæ N. Generosi NN. Hereditaria ex una, & Bona Sacræ Regiæ Majestatis Villæ N. ad Capitaneatum N. spectantis, Tenutæ ad præsens Magnifici N. ex altera Partibus, circa tres Scopulos Aciales, seu Angulares, præfatarum Hereditatum, & Tertium Villæ N. Generosi NN. Collateraliter adcitati Hereditariæ, Feria secunda post Festum NN. proxima Anno Dni NN.

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

Terrestrial estate; and whatever is on the left side and left hand, similarly entirely, shall for all time belong and pertain to the aforesaid estates of the village of [name] of His Sacred Royal Majesty; this also being provided and safeguarded: that the heirs and leaseholders of the aforesaid estates now delimited shall not deny one another mutual pasture in their respective estates (being careful, however, of damage), but shall maintain it among themselves, and their successors for all perpetual times. Which the above-named court messenger was ordered to proclaim and announce, by virtue of the present decree.

##### *Commissarial Decree on Serious Contested Matters of the Parties, with the Oath Enjoined upon the Party That Has Won the Boundary Path.*

Done in the pine forest or forest grown with various kinds of trees, called [name], at the place of the differences between the estates of the village of [name], hereditary property of the Honourable [names], on the one part, and the estates of His Sacred Royal Majesty of the village of [name] belonging to the Captaincy of [name], presently leased to the Right Honourable [name], on the other part; near three particular corner mounds or corner mounds of the aforesaid estates and the third of the village of [name], hereditary property of the Honourable [names], collaterally summoned; on the Monday next after the feast of [names], in the year of the Lord [names].

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-105">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-105">p. 105</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

Coram Illustri & Magnifico NN. Castellano N. tum Magnificis NN. Camerario Graniciali Palatinatus (intitulentur alij plures Commissarij propriis Titulis qui se subscripserant super Innotescentiales Partium ad præsens Comparentes) Commissarijs a Sacra Regia Majestate, ad decidendas, finaliterque; concludendas, & determinandas Differentias, occasione Limitum inter Bona superius descripta designatis, & deputatis, vigore Literarum Commissionis, sub titulo Sacræ Regiæ Majestatis emanatarum, Sigillo majoris vel minoris Cancellariæ Regni communitæ, sanæ, salvæ, & illæsæ, omnisque; suspicionis nota carentes, quarum tenor de verbo ad verbum sequitur, estque; talis: AUGUSTUS Tertius Dei Gratia Rex Poloniæ, (inferatur hoc instrumentum usque; ad finem) Quibus quidem Commissionis Sacræ Regiæ Majestatis Literis perlectis, in Termino itaque; hodierno, per Nos, seu Literas Innotescentiarum, uti Commissarios a Sacra Regia Majestate editas, Partibus assignato, fundata Jurisdictione sua Commissionali, & cum omni securitate Actus præsentis, per Ministerialem Regni generalem providum N. de Villa N. Authenticum & Juratum præsentibus Generosis ac Nobilibus NN. & alijs diversi status, & conditionis hominibus, ad hunc Actum congregatis, proclamata, quarum Literarum Innotescentialium tam Magnifico Capitaneo, seu Tenutario Bonorum Regalium

Before the Illustrious Right Honourable [names], Castellan of [name], and the Right Honourable [names], Boundary Chamberlain of the Palatinate (the other several commissioners are to be given their proper titles, those who subscribed themselves upon the summons letters of the parties, now present), commissioners designated and deputed by His Sacred Royal Majesty to decide, finally conclude, and determine the differences occasioned by the boundaries between the estates described above; by virtue of the Commission Letters issued under the title of His Sacred Royal Majesty, sealed with the seal of the greater or lesser Chancery of the Kingdom, sound, intact, and unimpaired, and bearing no mark of suspicion; the tenor of which, word for word, follows and is as follows: AUGUSTUS THE THIRD, by the Grace of God King of Poland (this instrument is to be inserted to the end). Those Commission Letters of His Sacred Royal Majesty having been read out, at today's session assigned to the parties by Us, or by our summons letters as commissioners from His Sacred Royal Majesty, the commissarial jurisdiction having been founded and proclaimed with all security of the present act by the court messenger of the Kingdom, the Honest [name] of the village of [name], authentic and sworn, in the presence of the Honourable and Noble [names] and others of various rank and condition assembled for this act; the tenor of the summons letters issued both to the Right Honourable Captain or leaseholder of the royal estates

of the town of [name] and the villages belonging to it, and to the community, or settlers and subjects of the village of [name], follows in this form: We, [names] [names], commissioners designated by His Sacred Royal Majesty to perform the matters written below, to you, the Right Honourable [names], leaseholder of the royal estates of [name], and to the Honest [names] [names] etc. (to be inserted as in the immediately preceding commissarial act). Judicium Commissoriale, Partes ad sui comparendum acclamare fecit, quibus acclamatis, in eo loco personaliter comparens, Generosus NN. Bonorum^[*Agnitio Angularitatis per Collateralem.*] Villæ N. Heres, agnoscendo hic in eo loco, veram concurrentiam præfatarum Hereditatum, & Scopulos reales, Partium præfatarum Angulares, sui vero Collateralis in determinio Parietis Acialem. Quo in loco protestatus est, suo & Communitatis nomine,^[*Protestatur Capitaneus contra Collateralem.*] Villæ N. Regalis, de indebita Agnitione Angularitatis, per præfatum Generosum N. Collateralem, allegando, non esse veros Scopulos verum Monticulos, non in debita forma Juris, & solito triangulo situatos, idem Magnificus Capitaneus. Quibus connotatis Partium Protestationibus, &

The commissarial court caused the parties to be called to appear. At which place the same Right Honourable Captain protested, in his own name and in the name of the community ^[*The Captain protests against the collateral heir.*] of the royal village of [name], regarding the improper acknowledgement of the corner point by the aforesaid Honourable [name], collateral heir, alleging that they are not true mounds but hillocks, not situated in the proper legal form and in the customary triangle. Both the Honourable [names], original plaintiff, and the collateral heir, the Honourable [names], counter-protested ^[*Both the plaintiff and the collateral heir counter-protest.*] against the same Right Honourable Captain, regarding his non-acknowledgement of the true corner point and convergence of three estates — denied by words alone, not by documents — and in verification thereof the plaintiff's party reproduced the boundary act conducted by the Royal Commissioners, by which it is proved that the true corner point and real mounds exist at that place according to the description of that same act; and on the basis thereof offers to demonstrate the boundaries and boundary wall of the aforesaid estates as far as his particular corner point.

Having noted the protests and counter-protests of the parties,

Reprotestationibus Judicium ijsdem Partibus Comparationem inscribere mandat, (scribatur secundum Declarationem Generosorum Patronorum) suo coram Judicio comparentibus, & secum Judicialiter controvertentibus.

Et imprimis inchoavit Ductum suum Pars Actorea (Describatur Ductus a quo incipit accurate, cum expressione claræ, positionis Locorum, & quidquid in Ductu demonstrabit, una cum Protestationibus, & Reprotestationibus, per Partes in Ductu, declaratis, & Documentis, si quæ produxerit, ad probationem ejusdem)

Cujus postulationi Illustres & Magnifici Commissarij ^[*Agnitio Angularitatis per Collateralem ultimum seu Acialitatis.*] præfatum Generosum N. Villæ N. Heredem ultimum Collateralem, ad Judicium suum acclamari

ex superiori Actu immediato) ad hunc locum:

Quibus eductis, & expeditis Inquisitionibus, ad^[*Decretum Definitivi.*] lectionem earundem condescendit. Quoniam tam ex eisdem Inquisitionibus, & Documentis Partis Actoreæ, videlicet Actu Graniciali, Anni N. ex Actis Metrices Regni depromptis, evidenter patet, Generosum Actorem, in fundamento ejusdem Actus, in solita forma Juris, per Magnificos Commissarios expediti, a Termino a Quo, videlicet ab Acialitate Hereditatis Villæ N. Generosi NN. Collateraliter Adcitati, a veris & legitimis Scopulis, & concurrentia trinarum Hereditatum, Ductum suum incepisse, & eundem videlicet per quem, Parietem Scopulis tribus tantum, verum realibus & visibilibus, (prout ex referatione patuit probasse, & eundem ad Terminum ad quem, secundum agnitionem Collateralis determinasse. Jam vero Magnificum Capitaneum summa obliquitate, non a vera concurrentia trinarum Hereditatum, incepisse, & eundem per Agros & Fundos proprios Partis Actoreæ expedivisse, nec etiam ad Quem determinasse. Proinde Judicium attentis præmissis, probabiliori Ductu Partis Actoreæ adinvento, & eidem totaliter adjudicato, jam vero eodem Magnifico Capitaneo abjudicato, uti nullis Documentis firmato, ad sipationem Scopulorum, & Renovationem antiquorum, Judicium accessit, cum hac Judiciali Declaratione, quatenus Actor post sipationem

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

the immediately foregoing act) down to this place:

The sworn inquiries having been produced and concluded, ^[*Final decree.*] he descended to the reading thereof. Since it is clearly apparent both from those same sworn inquiries and from the documents of the plaintiff's party — namely the boundary act of the year [name] drawn from the records of the Kingdom's Chancery — that the Honourable plaintiff, on the basis of that same act conducted in the customary legal form by the Right Honourable Commissioners, commenced his boundary path from the starting point — namely from the particular corner point of the estate of the village of [name] of the Honourable [names], collaterally summoned — from the true and legitimate mounds and the convergence of three estates; and continued the same, namely through the intermediate points, with only three wall mounds, true, real, and visible (as appeared from their opening, proved them); and determined the same to the end point, according to the acknowledgement of the collateral heir. Whereas the Right Honourable Captain had commenced with the greatest obliqueness, not from the true convergence of three estates, and had conducted the same through the plaintiff's party's own fields and plots, and had not determined it to the end point either. The court therefore, having regard to the foregoing, the more probable boundary path of the plaintiff's party having been found and adjudged to it in its entirety, and that same Right Honourable Captain's having been adjudged away from him as confirmed by no documents, proceeded to the erection of mounds and the renewal of the ancient ones, with this judicial declaration: that the plaintiff shall, after the erection

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-112">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-112">p. 112</a></h2>

præfatorum Scopulorum comprobet, cum sex Testibus genere sibi similibus, ad mentem Statuti lucratum Ductum in ultimo Scopulo aciali in eam Juramenti Rotham, prout Ductu suo, nil Terræ, & Hereditatis, Bonorum suprascriptorum ademit, nec ab eisdem alienavit, DEO & Sancta JESU Christi Passione ita Ipsum adjuvante.

Et imprimis Scopulum Angularem Hereditati Villæ N. Actoris, ad orientem, alterum Bonorum Regalium Villæ N. ad occidentem, tertium Hereditati & Villæ N. Collaterali ad septemtrionem, renovare & in altum erigere demandavit, cum Impositione in quemlibet eorum signorum Metallicorum, videlicet carbonum, laterum, & dimoscorij, tam ferrei, quam vitrei. A quibus Scopulis, recto tramite versus meridiem procedendo, sipatus est primus Scopulus Parietalis, sexaginta passibus ab Angularibus distans, (& sic scribatur sipatio Scopulorum Parietalium, & adultimum acialium, cum sufficienti Descriptione Locorum, distantiæ Scopuli a Scopulo, numerus eorum usque; ad determinationem eorundem.)

Quibus ritè & legitimè determinatis Graniciebus, ac sipatis Scopulis additur Ministerialis ad pronunciandam Juramenti Rotham, Parti Ductum lucranti, idque; instantanèè. Movit Pars citata videlicet Magnificus Capitaneus, ad Judicia Tribunalis Regni Petricoviensis, non admissa, insistendo legi, & Constitutioni

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

of the aforesaid mounds, confirm, with six witnesses of equal rank, according to the intent of the statute, the won boundary path at the final particular corner mound, in that oath formula: that by his boundary path he took nothing of the land and estate of the above-named estates and has not alienated anything from the same, so help him GOD and the Holy Passion of JESUS Christ.

And first he ordered the corner mound for the estate of the village of [name] of the plaintiff to be renewed and raised toward the east; a second for the royal estates of the village of [name] toward the west; and a third for the collateral estate and village of [name] toward the north; with the placing in each of them of metal markers, namely charcoal, brick, and iron and glass slag. Proceeding from those mounds by a straight path toward the south, the first wall mound was raised sixty paces from the corner mounds; (and so the erection of the wall mounds shall be written, and of the final particular corner mounds, with sufficient description of the places, the distance of each mound from the next, and their number up to the determination thereof.)

The boundaries having been duly and legitimately determined and the mounds raised, the court messenger is added immediately for the pronouncing of the oath formula for the party that has won the boundary path. The cited party — the Right Honourable Captain — moved to the Tribunal courts of the Kingdom at Piotrków; not admitted, adhering to the law and the constitution

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-113">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-113">p. 113</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

Anni 1588vi. Protestatur Pars de gravamine, Judicium cum Parte reprotestatur, quod Juramentum, siquidem Pars Actorea Ductum lucrans, satisfaciendo legi Positivæ, & Decreto Judicij sui, in facie Judicij sui, post recessum Partis citatæ ab attentando Juramento, Rothisante sibi Ministeriali, flexis genibus, in ultimo Scopulo aciali ad Imaginem Crucifixi Christi Domini; Jam vero Testes, erectis in Cælum duobus digitis, proinde eandem Partem Ductum lucrante satisfecisse Decreto Judicij sui arbitratur, & ijsdem Graniciebus authoritate Actus præsentis robur perpetuæ firmitatis habere vult & declarat, & quidquid est Fundi Hereditatis Villæ N. a primæva Angularitate versus Meridiem, in parte sinistra, id totum ad Hereditatem Villæ præfatæ, N. Quidquid vero in parte dextra, similiter id totum ad Bona præfata Villæ N. Sacræ Regiæ Majestatis perpetuis temporibus spectare & pertinere debebit. Quod Ministeriali suprascripto NN. publicare & proclamare mandaverunt. Quod vero spectat Prætensiones Partis Actoreæ, respectu indebite perceptorum proventuum, ex Fundis per Magnificum Capitaneum sibi appropriatis, & perpetratarum violentiarum, Judicium Commissoriale causam præsentem, uti Judicio suo non competentem, ad forum competens pro finali disjudicatione remittit judicandam. Decreti præsentis vigore.

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

of the year 1588. The party protests the grievance; the court counter-protests with the party that, the oath having been sworn — since the plaintiff's party having won the boundary path, in satisfaction of positive law and the decree of his court, before the face of his court, after the cited party withdrew from attempting the oath, with the court messenger reading the formula, on bended knee, at the final particular corner mound before the image of the Crucified Christ the Lord, and the witnesses with two fingers raised to heaven — he considers that same party having won the boundary path to have satisfied the decree of his court; and wills and declares by the authority of the present act that those same boundaries shall have the force of perpetual validity. And whatever is the property of the estate of the village of [name] from the original corner point toward the south, on the left side, shall belong entirely to the estate of the aforesaid village of [name]; and whatever is on the right side, likewise all entirely, shall for all time belong and pertain to the aforesaid estates of the village of [name] of His Sacred Royal Majesty. Which the above-named court messenger [names] was ordered to proclaim and announce. As regards, however, the claims of the plaintiff's party in respect of revenues wrongfully received from the plots appropriated by the Right Honourable Captain, and of acts of violence perpetrated, the commissarial court refers the present case, as not falling within the competence of his court, to the competent forum for final adjudication. By virtue of the present decree.

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-114">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-114">p. 114</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

##### *Quo ad Dislimitationem Bonorum Terrestrium cum Regalibus, in Contumaciam Tenutarij.*

PRæviis omnibus Solemnitatibus, ad causam Granicierum, spectantibus, ac etiam editis literis Innotescentialibus, & Mandato, Capitaneo, vel Tenutario Bonorum, Regalium, ac Communitati subditorum, tum Collateralibus, ad normam in primitivo Actu Commissionis exaratam, & recognita Relatione Ministerialis, in proprio Territorio positarum.

Si Capitaneus, vel Tenutarius Bonorum Regalium, disparet, in Termino per Magnificos Commissarios, literis suis Innotescentialibus assignato, tunc Judicium Commissoriale in pleno numero ad mentem legis, una cum Succamerario, vel ejus Officio, fundata Jurisdictione & Inscriptione Actus, ubi Pars Actorea locaverit, non obstante contumacia ejusdem Capitanei, seu Tenutarij Bonorum, ac Communitatis subditorum, ejusdem Capitaneatus, procedet, & solita forma Juris Ductum Parti Actoreæ expedire, & Judicio suo demonstrare, præevijs etiam Inquisitionibus, in verificatione Ductus ejusdem; demandabit, & Causam pro justo & æquo disjudicabit, & Granicies determinabit, etiam non obstante cujusvis Collateralis

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

##### *As Regards the Delimitation of Terrestrial Estates with Royal Ones, in the Contumacy of the Leaseholder.*

All the formalities pertaining to the boundary case having been completed beforehand, and the summons letters and mandate having also been issued to the Captain or leaseholder of the royal estates and to the community of subjects, as well as to the collateral heirs, according to the form set out in the original commissarial act; and the service return of the court messenger acknowledged in the proper district once served.

If the Captain or leaseholder of the royal estates fails to appear at the session assigned by the Right Honourable Commissioners by their summons letters, then the commissarial court in its full number, according to the intent of the law, together with the Sub-Chamberlain or his office, the jurisdiction having been founded and the act enrolled at the place where the plaintiff's party has stationed them, shall proceed notwithstanding the contumacy of the Captain or leaseholder of the estates and of the community of subjects of that Captaincy; and shall, in the customary legal form, order the plaintiff's party to conduct and demonstrate its boundary path before the court, with prior sworn inquiries also in verification of that boundary path; and shall adjudicate the case justly and equitably and determine the boundaries, even notwithstanding any collateral heir's

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-115x">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-115x">p. 115x</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

Natura Granicierum, est adinstar Ædificii, quod habet Quatuor Parietes & Angulos, alias Węgielniki. Jam vero quæ Hæreditas est magnæ longitudinis, & latitudinis, & Vicinæ Hæreditates attingunt suis Parietibus eandem longævam Hæreditatem; tunc in tali passu, una Hæreditas non tantum Quatuor, verum Quinque vel Sex, Septem, & Octo, potest habere Parietes, & totidem Angularitates, ad normam præsentis Delineationis.

Fluvius qui dislimitat Hæreditates uti Limes Naturalis.

Ejusdem Fluvy Novus Meatus Qui non censetur pro Limite.

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

The nature of boundaries is in the manner of a building that has four walls and corners, otherwise known as *węgielniki*. An estate of great length and breadth, however, whose neighbouring estates meet it with their own boundary walls — in such a case one estate may have not only four but five, six, seven, or even eight boundary walls, and as many corner points, according to the model of the present diagram. [T.N. 1]

A river delimiting estates as a natural boundary.

The same river's new course, which is not reckoned as the boundary.

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-115">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-115">p. 115</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

absentia, ac in ultimo Scopulo Parti Actoreæ, si Ductum lucrabitur cum sex Testibus, genere similibus, Juramentum præstare demandabit. Post quod præstitum robur perpetuæ firmitatis, ijsdem Graniciebus habere declarabit.

Si vero reprobabitur Ductus Partis Actoreæ, & Judicium ex Arbitro sui & re oculis subjecta, alium diriget, tunc tales Granicies sine Juramento terminentur.

* * *

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

absence; and at the final mound, if the plaintiff's party shall have won the boundary path, it shall order the party to swear an oath with six witnesses of equal rank. After which oath has been sworn, it shall declare those same boundaries to have the force of perpetual validity.

If, however, the plaintiff's party's boundary path shall be rejected, and the court shall direct another from the arbitration of its own court and from the thing presented to the eyes, then such boundaries shall be concluded without an oath.

* * *

---

**Translator's Note**

[T.N. 1] *Page 115x* interpolates a diagram page between pp. 115 and 116. The text on this page describes in prose the analogy between a boundary system and a building with walls and corners, and provides two brief captions for a diagram of a river serving as a natural boundary alongside a new course of the same river that is not counted as the boundary. The diagram itself is not reproduced in the Markdown source; the captions are translated as they appear.

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-116">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-116">p. 116</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

##### *De Commissionibus & Commissarijs, ad Dislimitationem Bonorum Regalium & Terrestrium.*

Ad Commissiones olim dabantur duo Dignitarij & Succamerarius ad Limites.

##### *Sed deinceps quinque sint Commissarij*

^[*Herburt Fol. 95.*] Item cum inter Bona nostra, inscripta vel non inscripta, & subditorum nostrorum convicinantium, nonnunquam Injuriæ & oppressiones, nonnunquam etiam cædes committerentur: & fierent difficultates plurimæ, pro limitatione Granicierum nostris aut Capitaneis, aut Tenutarijs, sub umbra Regij Nominis Nostri Bonorumque; Nostrorum, subditosque; Nostros vicinos, aut opprimentibus, aut limitationem Granicierum difficultantibus: idcirco ex innata Nobis virtute, & benignitate Regia, propter omnem subditorum nostrorum justitiam & pacem, ex hac parte habendam promittimus: quia quibuslibet subditis nostris, cujuslibet status & conditionis existentibus, Granicies ex parte Bonorum nostrorum petentibus, Nos & Successores Nostri, secundum Statuta antiqua, ad discernendam æquitatem, Commissarios illius Terræ & Districtus in quibus Bona limitanda consistunt,

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

##### *On Royal Commissions and Commissioners, for the Delimitation of Royal and Terrestrial Estates.*

Formerly two dignitaries and a Sub-Chamberlain were given for commissions on boundaries.

##### *But henceforth five commissioners shall be appointed.*

^[*Herburt, fol. 95.*] Likewise, since injuries and oppressions, and sometimes even acts of killing, were committed between Our registered and unregistered estates and those of Our neighbouring subjects; and since many difficulties arose in the delimitation of the boundaries, with Our Captains or leaseholders oppressing Our neighbouring subjects under cover of Our royal name and Our estates, or obstructing the delimitation of the boundaries — therefore, from the virtue and royal benevolence innate in Us, for the sake of all justice and peace for Our subjects in this matter, We promise: that to all Our subjects of whatever rank and condition, seeking boundaries from the side of Our estates, We and Our successors, according to the ancient statutes, for the discernment of equity, shall appoint and shall cause to be appointed the commissioners of that land and district in which the estates to be delimited are situated,

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-117">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-117">p. 117</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

videlicet duos Dignitarios & duos Officiales, cum suo Camerario deputabimus, & deputari debeant.

##### *Commissarij, accepto ex utraque parte a senibus Juramento, Limitationem faciant.*

^[*Herburt Ibidem.*] Qui quidem Commissarij postquam exiverint, debent ex utraque; parte senes advocare: a quibus recepto Juramento, juxta eorum recognitionem & testimonium ad limitationem procedent. Si vero ipsi senes discordes fuerint: ex tunc Commissarij juxta suas conscientias, concordando discordias senum, secundum eorum privatum Judicium, limitationem ipsam prosequentur & terminabunt. Ubi autem & ipsi Commissarij discordarent, volumus: quod ex Ipsorum sententia, qui plures in voce concordes fuerint, authoritas atque; potestas dependeat definita terminandi: nostrorum Officialium, videlicet Capitanei, aut Tenutarij, contradictione non obstante.

##### *Si Commissarij desint in proprio Districtu.*

^[*Herburt Ibidem.*] Si autem Dignitarij ad limites ipsos designandi, essent pauci, in illa Terra seu Palatinatu: in quo limites fieri debent: aut forte essent absentes: eo casu, ex vicino Palatinatu, vel etiam ex remotiori,

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

namely two dignitaries and two officials, together with the Boundary Chamberlain thereof.

##### *The commissioners, having received an oath from the elderly witnesses on both sides, shall make the delimitation.*

^[*Herburt, ibid.*] Which commissioners, after they have gone out, must summon the elderly witnesses from both sides; from whom, having received an oath, they shall proceed to the delimitation according to their recognition and testimony. If, however, those same elderly witnesses are in disagreement, then the commissioners according to their consciences, reconciling the disagreements of the elderly witnesses according to their own private judgment, shall pursue and conclude the delimitation itself. Where, moreover, the commissioners themselves should disagree, We will that the defined authority and power to conclude shall rest with the sentence of those who are in agreement by a majority of voices — the opposition of Our officials, namely the Captain or leaseholder, notwithstanding.

##### *If commissioners are lacking in the proper district.*

^[*Herburt, ibid.*] If, however, there were few dignitaries in that land or palatinate in which the boundaries are to be made who could be designated for those boundaries, or if they happened to be absent — in that case, from a neighbouring palatinate, or even from a more distant one,

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-118">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-118">p. 118</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

Commissarios designabimus, ad ipsos limites faciendos; non alios tamen, nisi tales: qui in illis Terris, ubi limites essent faciendi, possessionati fuerint.

##### *Capitanei Bona Regalia defendant, usque ad Dislimitationes,*

^[*Herburt Fol. 49.*] Constituimus: ne deinceps Præfecti arcium, & Civitatum Nostrarum, sed soli Nobiles Commissiones ^[*Nobilis Commissionem impeditur tenebitur.*] obtineant, ad fines terminandos; ita tamen ut nihilominus Præfectis seu Capitaneis nostris (sicut veteri more receptum est: & Statuto cavetur) liceat Bona nostra defendere, ad Declarationem usque; finium.

##### *Capitaneus, seu Tenutarius Bonorum Regalium, ad Limitationem Bonorum exeat; & Commissarios provideat si libera Bona possidet.*

^[*Herburt Fol. 96.*] Capitaneo seu Tenutario Bonorum nostrorum mandabimus: ut pariter cum Commissarijs eisdem ad limitanda Bona eadem exeat, descendat, & Commissarios expensis provideat: in his præsertim Bonis quæ habemus libera & sine obligatione.

##### *Commissionis terminus ad limitanda Bona, duæ Septimanæ.*

^[*Herburt Ibidem.*] Innotescentia Commissionalis una cum Mandato

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

We shall designate commissioners for the making of those same boundaries; none, however, other than those who shall have possessions in those lands where the boundaries are to be made.

##### *The Captains shall defend the royal estates until the delimitation.*

^[*Herburt, fol. 49.*] We enact: that henceforth the Prefects of Our castles and cities shall not obtain commissions for the fixing of boundaries, but noblemen alone; ^[*A nobleman who obstructs a commission shall be liable.*] yet so that nevertheless it shall be lawful for Our Prefects or Captains (as has been received by ancient custom and is provided by statute) to defend Our estates until the declaration of the boundaries.

##### *The Captain or leaseholder of the royal estates shall go out to the delimitation of the estates, and shall provide for the commissioners if he holds free estates.*

^[*Herburt, fol. 96.*] We shall command Our Captain or leaseholder of Our estates to go out and descend together with those same commissioners to delimit those same estates, and to provide for the commissioners at his expense — in particular with respect to those estates which We hold free and without encumbrance.

##### *The session of the commission for delimiting the estates: two weeks.*

^[*Herburt, ibid.*] The commissarial summons together with the mandate

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-119">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-119">p. 119</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

ad Capitaneum, per Ministerialem & duos Nobiles, inferenda sunt duabus hebdomadis ante limitationem ita ut tam de tempore, quam de loco certum Capitaneum faciant.

Præterea Commissarij nostri prædicti, duabus septimanis antequam simul conveniant ad faciendos limites, Capitaneum seu Tenutarium nostrum, de^[*Mandatum ponatur in Præsentia duorum Nobilium.*] tempore & loco sui conventus certiorem facient per Ministerialem, & duos Nobiles, ac etiam per literas suas proprias & Mandatum Nostrum.

##### *De honore, ac Securitate Commissariorum.*

^[*Herburt Ibidem.*] Quod si quis plebeius, in Negotio & Judicio limitum, ^[*Violator Actus Commissionis ad loci Capitanei mendis pænis remittatur.*] vel alio quocunque; contra Commissarios inveheretur verbis, illosque; inhonoraret: talis turri, vel alioquin Sessione dimidij Anni, puniri debet. Cujus pænæ executionem, Capitaneus illius loci faciet: sub pæna in Statuto descripta, contra illos: qui eius executionem facere negligerent. Quod si quis, etiam plebeius, arma arriperet & vibraret: licet etiam neminem vulneraret: Collo luat pænas quod etiam Capitaneus exequatur. Nobilitas autem pro talibus excessibus, juxta Statutum puniatur.

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

to the Captain must be served by the court messenger and two noblemen two weeks before the delimitation, so as to certify the Captain both as to the time and as to the place.

Moreover, Our aforesaid commissioners, two weeks before they convene together for the making of the boundaries, shall inform Our Captain or leaseholder ^[*The mandate shall be served in the presence of two noblemen.*] of the time and place of their convening through the court messenger and two noblemen, and also through their own letters and Our mandate.

##### *On the honour and security of the commissioners.*

^[*Herburt, ibid.*] If any commoner, in the matter and court of the boundaries, ^[*A violator of the commissarial act shall be referred to the Captain of that place for due penalties.*] or on any other occasion, should inveigh against the commissioners in words and dishonour them — such a person shall be punished with imprisonment, or otherwise with a sitting of half a year. The execution of that penalty the Captain of that place shall carry out, under the penalty prescribed in the statute against those who should neglect to carry out its execution. Should any person, even a commoner, seize and brandish arms — even if he wounded no one — he shall pay the penalty with his neck; which the Captain shall likewise execute. Nobility, however, for such excesses shall be punished according to the statute.

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-120">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-120">p. 120</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

##### *Quod a Commissarijs, in Limitatione Bonorum semel Decretum est: id non innovetur.*

^[*Herburt Ibidem.*] Ut quod per Commissarios nostros (quo ad Limitationem Bonorum nostrorum Regalium, & Nobilium, concedimus) semel Decretum fuerit, amplius non innovetur, statuimus: ut post limitationem Bonorum Nostrorum Regalium, ac Nobilium, Capitanei Nostri in fundis Nobilium injurias non inferant: subditi vero nostri, in fundis dislimitatis nullam usum fructuum habeant, neque; alvearia, neque; alias utilitates.

##### *Quidquid Commissarij fecerint, ratum sit. nec Revisores dabuntur.*

^[*Herburt Fol. 97.*] Nos vero deputabimus Commissarios Nostros, qui faciant limites: ac illorum Judicium, inhibitionibus nostris non impediemus: Assesores, seu Commissarios, nec etiam Revisorem, post factos limites designabimus. Sed quidquid Commissarij Nostri ^[*Commissarij inter Bona Regalia limites faciant non obstante Contumacia & Contradictione Possessoris.*] decreverint, illud ratum & firmum esse debebit. Præterea cum Commissarij Nostri convenient, ad locum limitum: sive Capitaneus, seu Tenutarius noster exiverit, sive non: eduxerit senes, aut non: ipsi Commissarij nihilominus limites ipsos prosequi, & finire debebunt: Capitanei nostri, ac etiam illius, qui quæritur de Injurijs limitum, seu Motione, & Appellatione,

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

##### *What has once been decreed by the commissioners in the delimitation of estates shall not be renewed.*

^[*Herburt, ibid.*] In order that what has once been decreed by Our commissioners (which We grant with respect to the delimitation of Our royal and noble estates) shall no longer be renewed, We enact: that after the delimitation of Our royal and noble estates, Our Captains shall do no injury in the plots of the nobility; and Our subjects shall have no usufruct in the delimited plots, neither bee-keeping nor any other advantage.

##### *Whatever the commissioners shall have done shall stand. No reviewers shall be appointed.*

^[*Herburt, fol. 97.*] We shall appoint Our commissioners to make the boundaries; and their judgment We shall not obstruct by Our inhibitions; We shall designate neither assessors nor commissioners, nor a reviewer, after the boundaries have been made. But whatever Our Commissioners ^[*Commissioners shall make the boundaries between the royal estates notwithstanding the contumacy and opposition of the possessor.*] shall have decreed, that shall be ratified and firm. Moreover, when Our commissioners convene at the place of the boundaries — whether Our Captain or leaseholder has gone out or not, whether he has produced the elderly witnesses or not — the commissioners themselves shall nonetheless pursue and conclude those same boundaries: the opposition and likewise the absence of Our Captains, and of those who seek remedy on account of injuries to the boundaries by way of motion and appeal,

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-121">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-121">p. 121</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

ne, ac etiam absentia, minime obstante.

##### *De Commissarijs, inique citatis: & de aliis Judiciis.*

^[*Herburt Ibidem.*] Quicunque; citaret Commissarios, aut aliquod Judicium vel Officium, pro aliquo Processu: & talis decernetur, inique illos citasse: is tenebitur singulis separatim dare, viginti octo Marcas, pro pæna. Similiter & Parti: si eadem citaretur.

##### *Literæ Commissariorum, super Limitatione datæ, confirmatione non egent.*

^[*Herburt Fol. 271.*] Petitionibus subditorum Nostrorum inducti permittimus: ut in arbitrio sit subditorum Nostrorum petere, & obtinere a Nobis confirmationem: super ^[*Actus Commissionis perfectus non indiget approbatione.*] literis Limitum editis, a quinque; Commissarijs per Nos, secundum Statuti tenorem deputatis. Quæ quidem Literæ, quibus Commissio & Mandatum Nostrum fuerit insertum, & Sigilla Commissariorum apposita, authoritatem & robur perpetuum obtinere debebunt, etiamsi nulla desuper interveniat Confirmatio Nostra.

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

notwithstanding in the slightest.

##### *On commissioners unjustly cited, and on other courts.*

^[*Herburt, ibid.*] Whoever should cite the commissioners, or any court or office, on account of any process — and such person shall be determined to have cited them unjustly — shall be required to give twenty-eight marks to each separately as a penalty. Likewise to the party, if the same should be cited.

##### *Letters of the commissioners issued for the delimitation need no confirmation.*

^[*Herburt, fol. 271.*] Induced by the petitions of Our subjects, We permit: that it shall be in the discretion of Our subjects to petition and obtain from Us a confirmation of the letters of boundaries issued by five commissioners ^[*A completed commissarial act requires no approval.*] deputed by Us according to the tenor of the statute. Which letters, in which Our commission and mandate shall have been inserted and the seals of the commissioners affixed, shall obtain authority and perpetual force, even if no confirmation of Ours intervenes thereupon.

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-122">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-122">p. 122</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

##### *Bona Regalia non Dislimitata, quo modo defendi debent, & Capitanei, pro Injurijs, quomodo Jure conveniendi.*

^[*Herburt Fol. 48.*] Item statuimus: ut Tenutarij Bonorum mensæ Nostræ ^[*Capitaneo pro limitibus Forum Terrestre assignatur.*] Regiæ, si a vicinis aut alijs hominibus injuriati fuerint, teneantur stare Judicio Terrestri: postquam fuerint per injuriam passos evocati. Etsi pellant pecora, aut pecudes, ad Dominos, aut Curias Regales: occasione Differentiæ Granicierum aut ratione destructionis Seminatorum: tenebuntur dare ad cautionem fidejussoriam, super Decretum Dominorum, juxta consuetudinem Terræ diutius observatam. Quod si secus fecerint, luent pænas sicut alij Nobiles, quæ pænæ poterint peti, & exigi ab eis, etiam in pecunijs: si quas habuerint, in Bonis Regalibus inscriptas.

##### *Limites Bonorum, Signa, testimonia senum, & alia possibilia judicia, faciunt,*

^[*Herburt Fol. 267.*] Cæterum ad tollendas difficultates in faciendis limitibus, statuimus: ut Commissarij Nostri Deum solum præ oculis habentes, nulli partium faventes, conspectis in primis signis metallibus, & rei veritate diligenter investigata, tam ex senum, etiam si opus fuerit Juratorum testimoniis, quam alijs possibilibus Indicijs, limites inter Bona nostra Regalia, &

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

##### *How royal estates that have not been delimited are to be defended, and how Captains are to be proceeded against in law for injuries.*

^[*Herburt, fol. 48.*] Likewise We enact: that leaseholders of the estates of Our Royal Table, ^[*The land court is assigned as the forum for the Captain in matters of boundaries.*] if they suffer injury from neighbours or others, shall be required to stand before the land court, after they have been summoned on account of the injury suffered. Even if they drive cattle or livestock to royal lordships or courts on account of a difference of boundaries, or on account of the destruction of crops — they shall be required to give to suretyship for a decree of the lords, according to the custom of the land long observed. If they do otherwise, they shall pay penalties like other nobles; which penalties may be sought and exacted from them, even in money, if any they shall have inscribed in the royal estates.

##### *The boundaries of estates, metal markers, the testimony of elderly witnesses, and other possible evidences, make boundaries.*

^[*Herburt, fol. 267.*] Moreover, for the removal of difficulties in the making of boundaries, We enact: that Our commissioners, having only GOD before their eyes and favouring neither party, having first examined the metal markers and diligently investigated the truth of the matter — both from the testimony of elderly witnesses, sworn if necessary, and from other possible indications — shall establish the boundaries between Our royal

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-123">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-123">p. 123</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

Hereditaria, pro Arbitrio & conscientia sua constituant.

##### *Dum Limites facti erunt, Capitanei injuriam ne faciant.*

^[*Herburt Ibidem.*] Ne etiam amplius in discrepationem revocetur: quod semel per Commissarios nostros, ad dis limitandum Bona Nostra Regalia & Nobilium datos, definitur: Statuimus; ut post Dislimitationem Bonorum Nostrorum Regalium, & Nobilium, injurias Capitanei Nostri, aut Officiales, in Fundis Nobilium non faciant. Sed & subditi Nostri, talibus fundis ablimitatis, neque; apisterijs, neque; ulla ratione uti debent.

##### *Granice Stárostowie przez Komisarzow usypáne wiecznie trzymáć máią.*

^[*Konst. 1550 Fol. 598.*] Aby też co raz przez Komisarze násże, ktore ku Rozgrániczeniu Imion násżych Krolewskich, á Ziemiáńskich, daiemy, wznawiano nigdy nie było, ustanawiamy, aby po rozgraniczeniu Imion Násżych Krolewskich, á Ziemiáńskich, krzywdy Starostowie Nasi, álbo Urzędnicy w grunciech Ziemiańskich

</div>

and hereditary estates according to their arbitration and conscience.

##### *When the boundaries have been made, the Captains shall not commit injury.*

^[*Herburt, ibid.*] So that what is defined once by Our commissioners appointed for the delimitation of Our royal and noble estates may no longer be called into dispute: We enact that after the delimitation of Our royal and noble estates, Our Captains or officials shall not commit injury in the noble plots. And Our subjects too shall have no use of such delimited plots — neither in bee-keeping nor on any account.

* * *

^[*Constitution 1550, fol. 598.*] So that what has once been made by Our commissioners, whom We appoint for the delimitation of Our royal estates and those of the nobility, shall never be renewed: We enact that after the delimitation of Our royal estates and those of the nobility, Our Starostas or officials shall not

nieczyniłi. A poddani nasi takowych gruntow odgrániczonych ani barciami, áni żadnym sposobem używać máią. Konstytucyą 1550. Fol. 598. Załuski.

##### *Státut około usypania Gránic między Imieniem Nászym, á Imiony Ziemskiemi w mocy zostáwiamy.*

^[*Kon. 1562. Fol. 621. Tomo 2do*] A ktoryby Starostá rozsypał Granice: áby ie powinien był usypáć ad Festum Divi JOANNIS Baptistæ proximè venturum, sub privatione Officij Capitaneatûs, vel Tenutæ Zamkow: y máią być trzymane mocnie wedle kopcu temporibus perpetuis. A gdzieby był, kto o nie pozwan ad instantiam Instigatoris Officij Nostri ex Delatione Capitanei vel Tenutarij, tedy wszystkich od tákowych pozwow czyniemy wolnemi.

##### *O Gránicách w Xiéstwie Mázowieckim,*

^[*Kon. 1569. Fol. 790. Tomo 2do*] Granice w Xiéstwie Mazowieckim przez Komisarze od Nas dane, między grunty Naszemi á Szlácheckiemi, prawnie, to iest, że Staroście álbo Dzierżawcy Dobr Nasżych, Mandat Nász z innotescencyą Komisarską w czas, wedle Statutu o tym obmawaiące, wydáne są, albo ná potym będą wydáne,

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

commit injuries in the noble plots. And Our subjects shall have no use of such delimited plots — neither in bee-keeping nor in any manner. Constitution 1550, fol. 598. Załuski.

##### *We leave in force the statute concerning the erection of boundaries between Our estate and the terrestrial estates.*

^[*Constitution 1562, fol. 621, vol. 2.*] And any Starosta who destroys the boundaries: he shall be required to erect them again by the feast of Saint JOHN the Baptist next to come, under pain of deprivation of the office of the Captaincy or of the leasehold of the castle; and they shall be kept firmly with mounds in perpetuity. And where anyone should be summoned in respect of them at the instance of the Instigator of Our office from the denunciation of the Captain or leaseholder, We exempt all persons from such summonses.

##### *On boundaries in the Duchy of Masovia.*

^[*Constitution 1569, fol. 790, vol. 2.*] Boundaries in the Duchy of Masovia, made by commissioners appointed by Us between Our plots and those of the nobility, lawfully — that is, where Our mandate with the commissarial summons, issued in time according to the statute dealing with this matter, has been or shall in the future be issued to the Starosta or leaseholder of Our estates;

</div>
</section>

y ktore ná potym będą czynione, chociażby gdzie Ekonom Nasż nie był przy Ich czynieniu, ktory do Granic takowych, wedle prawa y zachowania starego, żadnego interesse nie ma, áni na potym mieć może, wszystkie mocnie máią być dzierżane przez Starosty, y Dzierzawce Dobr Nasżych, y przez Poddane Nasze, wiecznemi czasy, ktore tą konstytucyą potwierdzamy, y wedle Praw dawnych Koronnych, wedle ktorych też w Mazowszu Komisarze bywáią, y máią być dawani, z Kancellaryi Nasżey potwierdzenia Ich máią być wydawane, kto iedno tego potrzebować będzie. A ktorzy Szlachta są od Starost nomine Instigatoris nostri, spozywani o krzywdy iakie albo szkody, ktoreby się w tych Granicach zawierały te wszystkie od takowych Pozwow wolne czyniemy.

##### *O Gránice rozsypáne.*

^[*Kon. 1578. Fol. 977. Tomo 2do.*] Granice ktore są juridice przez Komisarze Naszę między dobry Naszemi á Szlacheckiemi skończone, áby Ich Starostowie y Dzierzawcy Nasi, y Poddani w Dobrach Nasżych będący, gwałcić y rozsypować nie śmieli. A ieżeliby ktore od konstytucyi, Anni Millesimi, Quingentesimi, Sexagesimi, Secundi, rozsypali, aby ie suis sumptibus przez Komisarze usypali, przed Świętem Sw. JANA Chrzciciela blisko przyszłego, zapłaciwszy stronie sto grzywien

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

and which shall in the future be made, even where Our steward was not present at their making, who in respect of such boundaries has and can have no interest in the future according to the law and ancient observance — all shall be firmly kept by the Starostas and leaseholders of Our estates and by Our subjects for ever; which We confirm by this constitution, and according to the old laws of the Crown, according to which also in Masovia commissioners are and shall be appointed, the confirmations of those commissioners shall be issued from Our Chancery, to whoever shall have need of it. And those noblemen who are summoned on behalf of the Starostas in the name of Our Instigator, for any injuries or losses that might be contained within those boundaries — all of them We exempt from such summonses.

##### *On destroyed boundaries.*

^[*Constitution 1578, fol. 977, vol. 2.*] Boundaries that have been juridically concluded by Our commissioners between Our estates and those of the nobility — let neither Our Starostas and leaseholders nor the subjects in Our estates dare to violate or destroy them. And if any have been destroyed since the constitution of the year one thousand five hundred and sixty-two, they shall erect them again at their own expense through the commissioners, before the feast of Saint JOHN the Baptist next to come, having paid the party one hundred marks

</div>

winy, toties quoties citabuntur. ##### *O Komisyách y Komisarzách na Granicách.*

^[*Kon. 1588. Fol. 1210. Tomo 2do.*] Komisye na granice zawsze máią być z Kancellaryi Nasżey bez wszelakiey trudności każdemu potrzebuiącemu dawane. A Komisarze przy Podkomorzym, albo Komorniku oney Ziemie, mogą być nie tylko z onego, ale y z inszego bliższego Woiewodztwa, chociażby w onym Woiewodztwie, albo Ziemi, żadney osiadłości nie mieli. Ktorzy Komisarze gdy się na innotescencyą podpiszą, powinni na termin ziachać, y onę Komisyą wexekucyą przywieść, uczynieniem granic. A gdyby się iednego dnia skończyć nie mogły, tedy subsequentibus diebus, nie odieżdżaiąc skończyć ie máią, aby ona Komisya Szlachcicowi nie zpełzła. A gdzieby Komisarz podpisawszy się na innotescencyą, nie przyiechał na termin, przepada stronie sto grzywien o ktorą forum ma mieć na Trybunale, excepta vera

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

as a fine, as often as they shall be summoned. And if Our subjects — namely commoners — should obstinately, without the command of the Starosta or Our leaseholder, destroy such boundaries — they shall be punished by a fine of fifty marks, as often as they shall be summoned; notwithstanding, the Starosta or leaseholder shall at their expense bring out the commissioners for the repair of the destroyed boundaries, at the latest within twelve weeks of their destruction.

##### *On royal commissions and commissioners for boundaries.*

^[*Constitution 1588, fol. 1210, vol. 2.*] Royal commissions for boundaries shall always be given from Our Chancery without any difficulty to whoever has need of them. And the commissioners, together with the Sub-Chamberlain or Boundary Chamberlain of that land, may be appointed not only from that but also from another closer palatinate, even if they have no residence in that palatinate or land. Which commissioners, when they subscribe themselves upon the summons letters, are required to arrive at the session and bring that commission to execution by making the boundaries. And if they cannot be concluded in one day, they are to conclude them on the following days without departing, so that the commission shall not lapse for the nobleman. And where a commissioner, having subscribed himself upon the summons letters, should fail to arrive at the session, he forfeits to the party one hundred marks, for which the forum shall be before the Tribunal, excepting true

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-127">

infirmitate, ktorą wedle prawa powinien poprzysiądź. Starosta tak iako y Szlachcic nie ma mieć więcey pocztu iedno trzydzieści osob z Przyiaciołmi y z sługami: á poddanych naywięcey po dwudziestu dla ukazania znakow, y sypania Kopcow. A iżby się perjuria z obrażeniem BOZYM nie działy, máią Komisarze pierwey przypatrzyć się znakom z obu stron przez starca pokazanym, y uważyć to u siebie według sumnienia swego: y ieżliby ktora strona słuszne znaki y podobieństwa pokazała: tedy tey dowod przez starce máią pokazać. A ieśliby z obu stron niepewne znaki pokazali, tedy nie odbieraiąc przysiąg z obu stron, dla krzywoprzysięstwa, máią Granice wedle sumnienia swego, przychyliwszy się do Statutu & juxta æquitatem uczynić. A iż Starostowie y Dzierzawcy niektorzy dobr Nasżych, zwykli excedere modum in defensione bonorum: tedy Statuta pierwsze w tym uczynione, tak deklaruiemy, że defensio nie ma się rózumieć płowaniem laboris proprij, tak osiewkow, iako y budowania, y każdey roboty ktoraby przez rok bez przekazy Starościney stała: ale używaniem tylko Dobr Nasżych albo pozwaniem po wyjściu roku do Ziemstwa. A gdyby Sądow Starosta albo Dzierzawca komu krzywdę, albo szkodę iaką mimo to uczynił, tedy o to forum ma mieć w Ziemstwie, gdzie one Dobra Krolewskie leżą. A ieżliby Dzierzawca osiadłości nie miał w onym Woiewodztwie

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

illness, which according to the law he is required to swear. The Starosta, like the nobleman, shall have no more of a retinue than thirty persons with friends and servants; and subjects at most twenty, for the showing of markers and the erection of mounds. And so that perjuries may not occur with offence to GOD, the commissioners are first to examine the markers shown from both sides by an elderly witness, and consider the matter among themselves according to their conscience; and if either party should show legitimate markers and probabilities: they shall require that party to prove it through elderly witnesses. And if both sides show uncertain markers, then without taking oaths from both sides, on account of the risk of perjury, they are to make the boundaries according to their conscience, inclining toward the statute and *juxta æquitatem*. And since some Starostas and leaseholders of Our estates are accustomed to exceed the measure in the defence of the estates: We therefore declare the prior statutes made in this matter as follows — that defence is not to be understood as the ploughing up of another's labour, namely crops, buildings, and any work that shall have stood for a year without the Starosta's obstruction; but only as the enjoyment of Our estates, or the bringing of an action after the year to the land court. And if a court, Starosta, or leaseholder should do anyone injury or harm beyond this, the forum for that shall be in the land court where those royal estates are situated. And if the leaseholder had no residence in that palatinate

albo Ziemi, tedy tam gdzieby ią miał, ma być exekucya na Dobrach iego nieodwłocznie czyniona wedle konstytucyi Anni 1562. A Starostom y Dzierzawcom o krzywdy y szkody, ktoreby się działy na gruncie Krolewskim, tak w Nasżych y R. P. iako w ich pożytkach, będzie wolno pozywać każdego do Ziemstwa, y gdzieby im iuramenty wedle prawa były nakazane, tak Nasżym, iako y swym Imieniem odprawować ie máią salva appellatione ad Tribunal, gdzie fine remissione kauzy takie máią być determinowane. Poddani też z Nasżych wsi będą mogli pozywać do Ziemstwa o krzywdy swe cum assistentia Capitaneorum vel Tenutariorum. Takze z Miásteczek Nasżych sine assistencia wedle zwyczaiu. Ieżliby też po rozgraniczeniu Komisarskim Szlachcic za granicami grunt Krolewski odeymował, á był o to pozwan do Ziemstwa: powinien będzie sam Sąd wyiachać, albo Komorniki przysięgłe z Woźnym wysłać na on grunt: & ex re oculis subjecta, na przyszłych Rocech Dekret uczynić, y

</div>

or land, then where he has it, execution shall be made on his estates without delay according to the constitution of the year 1562. And where he has no residence in the Crown, proceedings shall be taken against him according to the procedure agreed at this Sejm against those without possessions; and if he has a sum inscribed in the leasehold, the legal process and execution shall be extended to that sum. And Starostas and leaseholders shall be free to summon anyone before the land court for injuries and losses that occur on the royal plot, both in Our benefit and that of the Commonwealth and in their own; and where oaths are prescribed for them by law, they are to discharge them both in Our name and in their own, saving the appeal to the Tribunal, where such cases are to be determined without remission of the cause. The subjects of Our villages too shall be able to summon before the land court for their own injuries *cum assistentia Capitaneorum vel Tenutariorum*. Likewise those from Our towns without assistance, according to custom. And where boundaries do not exist, the judges shall be required to send out a commission and conclude the boundaries for the nobleman without interruption. If also, after a commissarial delimitation, a nobleman should take away royal land beyond the boundaries, and be summoned for it before the land court: the court itself shall be required to go out, or to send sworn boundary officials with the court messenger to that plot; and *ex re oculis subjecta*, at the next sitting shall make a decree, and

##### *Wárunek Komisyi Gránicznych.*

^[*Kon. 1598. Fol 1456. Tomo 2do.*] Przychylaiąc się do starodawnych Praw, pozwy wszystkie na Seym, albo za Dworem, na Komisarze y stronę, o rozgraniczenie Dobr Nasżych wszelakich z Dobry Ziemskiemi, przez Dzierzawce, albo przez Instygatora Nasżego wydane kasuiemy: deklaruiąc, że Akt Komisarski, przez zupełną liczbę Komisarzow wedle Praw odprawiony, w żadną wątpliwość nie ma być przywodzony, ani posądzony, wedle Prawa dawnego.

##### *O sposobie rozgraniczenia Obywátelow Pruskich z przyległemi.*

^[*Kon. 1601. Fol. 1507. Tomo 2do.*] Iż niebyło y nie masz do tego czasu sposobu, iako by Dobra Woiewodztw, y Ziem przyległych od Ziem Pruskich, pod Nasżym roskazowaniem będących miały być rozgraniczone: a rożny iest sposob Sądow w Woiewodztwach y Ziemiach przyległych od Sądow Woiewodztw y Ziem Pruskich, y nie máią w Prusiech Podkomorzowie żadney w Prawach Granicznych Jurysdykcyi. Przeto opatruiąc aby zatrudnienia z tąd na obie stronie nie rosły; postanawiamy uchwałą Seymu ninieyszego zá wszech Stanow zezwoleniem, aby gdzie rożnice takie zachodzą, albo

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

shall order the reimbursement of losses according to the liquidation of the injured party by oath.

##### *Provision for boundary commissions.*

^[*Constitution 1598, fol. 1456, vol. 2.*] Inclining ourselves to the ancient laws, We annul all summonses to the Sejm or to the court, issued against the commissioners and the party for the delimitation of all Our estates of whatever kind from terrestrial estates, issued by leaseholders or by Our Instigator; declaring that a commissarial act, conducted by the full number of commissioners according to the laws, is not to be called into any doubt nor impugned, according to the ancient law.

##### *On the manner of delimiting the estates of the inhabitants of Prussia with their adjacent ones.*

^[*Constitution 1601, fol. 1507, vol. 2.*] Since there has not been and is not to this day any means by which the estates of the palatinates and lands adjacent to the Prussian lands under Our authority might be delimited; and the manner of the courts in the palatinates and adjacent lands differs from the courts of the palatinates and lands of Prussia, and in Prussia the Sub-Chamberlains have no jurisdiction in boundary laws — therefore, providing that difficulties from this quarter may not grow on both sides: We enact by the resolution of the present Sejm with the consent of all the estates, that where such differences arise, or

</div>
</section>

zachodzić będą, nie pozywaiąc się do Sądow Ziemskich, zaraz na pole strony, albo strona ktoraby rozgraniczenia potrzebowała, mogła pozwać: y z iedney strony, to iest od Woiewodztw y Ziem przyległych Podkomorzy: á z drugiey od Prus, Sędzia Ziemski albo Ławnik ktory: gdyż ci granice w Prusiech odprawuią, máią być wywiedzieni. Przed temi strony máią in secundo termino tanquam peremptorio stanąć, y de Actoratu rospierać się: a komu będzie nakazano prowadzić Granice, y one według prawa poprzysiądz, appellacyą od sentencyi ich, albo gdzieby się nie zgodzili, od ich rozrożnienia, wolą na Trybunał zostawuiąc, ktorey prozekucyą na tenże Sąd ukazać, między tey albo owey Ziemi kauzami gdy na Trybunale będą sądzone, á mianowicie tey Ziemi, z ktorey będzie Actor citans: á ieśliby społeczni byli Aktorowie, tedy tey, z ktorey pozew wprzod założą. A to się rozumieć ma o rozgraniczeniu dobr dziedzicznych, y o Dobrach Stołu Nasżego.

##### *Deklárácya Komisyi Granicznych Pruskich.*

^[*Kon. 1613. Fol. 185. Tomo 3tio.*] Iż się Obywatele Pruscy przez Posty swe przed Nami uskarzali, że w sprawach Granicznych, między Dobrami Nasżemi Krolewskiemi, á Dziedzicznemi Szlacheckiemi, wielkie nie tylko trudności, y uciążenie máią, ale y zwłoki, y omięszkania, nie

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

shall arise, without summoning one another before the land courts, the parties, or the party that needs delimitation, may immediately summon to the field; and from one side — that is, from the palatinates and adjacent lands — a Sub-Chamberlain; and from the other side, from Prussia, a land judge or a certain assessor — since they conduct boundaries in Prussia — shall be brought out. Before these, the parties shall appear *in secundo termino tanquam peremptorio* and dispute the right of path; and to whomever it shall be ordered to lead the boundaries and to swear them according to law, the appeal from their sentence, or where they should disagree, from their divergence, shall be left open to the Tribunal, the prosecution of which shall be directed to that same court among the cases of that land or the other, when they shall be adjudicated at the Tribunal — and in particular that land from which the plaintiff making the summons shall be; and if the plaintiffs are joint, then from that one from which they first lodge the summons. And this is to be understood with respect to the delimitation of hereditary estates and of the estates of Our Table.

##### *Declaration on the boundary commissions of Prussia.*

^[*Constitution 1613, fol. 185, vol. 3.*] Since the inhabitants of Prussia have complained before Us through their deputies that in boundary cases, between Our royal estates and hereditary noble estates, they have not only great difficulty and burden, but must bear delays and arrears

</div>
</section>

bez kosztow prożnych, ponosić muszą; á to ztąd, że y w naymnieyszych sprawach Komisarze appellacyi dopuszczáią, y przeto prosili, ábyśmy im w tym folgę iáką uczynili. My tedy, áby y w tym łaskę Nasżę znali, zezwalamy aby Differencye małe, ktoreby dwu włok Chełmińskich nieprzechodziły bez áppellacyi Pánowie Komisarze sądzić, y odprawować mogli. Wszakże gdzieby Dyfferencya liczbę włok pomienionych przechodziła, co pomiara Miernikow przysięgłych ukaże: w takowych Sprawach appellacya, a sententia, Definitiva do Nas ma być dopuszczona.

##### *O Komisyách y Komisarzách Granicznych, Woiewodztw, Kijowskiego y Bráćłáwskiego.*

^[*Kon. 1613. Fol. 172. Tomo 3tio.*] Iż dla wielkiego niebespieczeństwa y trwog częstych nieprzyiacielskich, y przemałą liczbę Senatorow, szeroką odległość Woiewodztw, Kijowskiego, y Bracławskiego; gdy się przytrafi rozgraniczenie Dobr Szlácheckich, od Dobr Nasżych, Szlachcic Senatora ná Komisyą zwieść, żadną miarą nie może: záczym ukrzywdzony nigdy sprawiedliwości y rozgraniczenia odnieść nie może. Zábiegaiąc tedy temu, ná proźbę Posłow Ziemskich tych Woiewodztw, postanawiamy, y mieć chcemy, aby Senator z Podkomorzym, y z inszemi Urzędnikami

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

not without futile expense — and this because in even the smallest cases the commissioners admit appeals, and therefore they asked Us to grant them some relief in this matter — We therefore, so that they may know Our favour in this matter too, permit that the lord commissioners may adjudicate and settle small differences that do not exceed two Chełmno *włókas* without appeal. However, where the difference exceeds the said number of *włókas*, as the measurement of sworn surveyors shall show — in such cases an appeal from the final sentence to Us shall be permitted. [T.N. 1]

##### *On boundary commissions and commissioners of the Palatinates of Kiev and Bracław.*

^[*Constitution 1613, fol. 172, vol. 3.*] Since on account of the great danger and frequent enemy alarms, the small number of senators, and the vast remoteness of the Palatinates of Kiev and Bracław — when a delimitation of noble estates from Our estates occurs, a nobleman can in no way bring a senator to the commission; whereby the injured person can never obtain justice and delimitation — therefore, providing against this, at the petition of the land deputies of those palatinates, We enact and will: that a senator together with the Sub-Chamberlain and other land officials

</div>
</section>
