<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

Ziemskiemi, w Komisyi specyfikowanemi, podpisawszy się ná odprawienie Komisyi powinien się był stawić pod winą dziesięci tysięcy grzywien na Senatora, o co forum ukrzywdzonemu ná Trybunał záchowuiemy. Ci iednak Komisarze od Nas mianowani, máią się zachować wedle Konstytucyi Anni 1588. uczynioney o Komisyach.

##### *O Rewizyách.*

^[*Kon. 1647 Fol. 125. Tomo 4to.*] Iż Nas dochodzą skargi od Posłow Ziemskich, iákoby Dzierżawcy Dobr Násżych Rewizye inter Bona ab utrinque; Nostra cum præjudicio fundi Terrestris per Commissionem antea non dislimitati odpráwować mieli: Tedy My stosuiąc się do Prawa starodawnego o Komisyách względem rozgraniczenia Dobr Nasżych z Ziemskiemi postanowionego, deklaruiemy, że Dobrá Násze przez samych Komisarzow, nie przez Rewizorow, z Dobrami Ziemskiemi rozgraniczone być máią, nie prejudykuiąc między Dobrami Nászemi, ab utrinque; przez Sekretarzow Nasżych odpráwionemi, & in futurum przez nichże odpráwowáne być máią a nie przez Komisarzow. A Rewizye takowe lubo anticipative, lubo post Commissionem odprawione, tak przed tą konstytucyą iáko y na potym fundo Terrestri & Commissioni præjudicare nie mogą.

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

specified in the commission, having subscribed himself for the conduct of the commission, shall be required to appear under a fine of ten thousand marks on the senator, for which We preserve the forum for the injured party before the Tribunal. These commissioners, however, appointed by Us, are to conduct themselves according to the constitution of the year 1588 made concerning commissions.

##### *On reviews.*

^[*Constitution 1647, fol. 125, vol. 4.*] Since complaints reach Us from the land deputies that leaseholders of Our estates are conducting reviews *inter Bona ab utrinque Nostra cum præjudicio fundi Terrestris per Commissionem antea non dislimitati* — We therefore, inclining ourselves to the ancient law concerning commissions made for the delimitation of Our estates with terrestrial ones, declare that Our estates are to be delimited from terrestrial ones by commissioners alone and not by reviewers; without prejudice as between Our estates *ab utrinque*, which have been and are to be in the future conducted by Our secretaries and not by commissioners. And such reviews, whether conducted in anticipation or after the commission, both before this constitution and in the future, cannot prejudice the terrestrial estate or the commission.

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-133">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-133">p. 133</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

##### *Wárunek Komisyi Gránicznych.*

^[*Kon. 1651. Fol. 712. Tomo 4to.*] Lubo to bespieczeństwo y powaga Komisyi Granicznych dosyć iest Prawem pospolitym obwarowana, przecię iednak żnayduią się tacy, ktorzy in convulsionem Legum publicarum, takowe Komisye juxta præscriptum legis odprawione, rożnemi sposobami, Mandatami na Seym, y za Dworem wydanemi inwalidować usiłuią. Przeto zabiegaiąc temu teraz y na potym, Konstytucye Annorum 1550. 1567. 1588. 1598. y insze wszystkie Statuta, Konstytucye, de Authoritate Commissionum reasumuiąc, Authoritate Conventus præsentis waruiemy, iż tákowy każdy ktoryby takowe Komisye legitime odprawione, chciał lubo Mandatami, lubo inszemi sposobami inwalidować, ma być karany winą piąci tysięcy grzywien inter causas Officij peremptorie w Trybunale Koronnym repetendâ.

##### *Reafumpcya Konstytucyi o Komisyach.*

^[*Kon. 1703 Fol. 111. Tomo 6to.*] Reasumuiąc dawnieysze o Komisyach Konstytucye odprawione wszystkie dotąd między Dobrami Nasżemi, á Ziemskiemi Komisye y Dekreta przez nie ferowane, według Statutow y Praw dawnych nienaruszenie zachowuiemy y stwierdzamy. Ktora Konstytucya y W. X. Litewskiemu służyć

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

##### *Provision for boundary commissions.*

^[*Constitution 1651, fol. 712, vol. 4.*] Although the security and authority of boundary commissions is sufficiently safeguarded by the common law, there are nevertheless those who, in contravention of the public laws, attempt to invalidate such commissions conducted according to the prescription of the law by various means — by mandates issued to the Sejm and to the court. Therefore, providing against this now and in the future, and reassuming the constitutions of the years 1550, 1567, 1588, 1598, and all other statutes and constitutions on the authority of commissions, by the authority of the present Sejm: We provide that every person who should wish to invalidate such legitimately conducted commissions, whether by mandates or by other means, shall be punished by a fine of five thousand marks, to be recovered before the Crown Tribunal *inter causas Officij peremptorie*.

##### *Reassumption of the constitutions on commissions.*

^[*Constitution 1703, fol. 111, vol. 6.*] Reassuming the prior constitutions concerning commissions, all commissions and decrees issued by them conducted to this day between Our estates and terrestrial ones, according to the statutes and ancient laws, We preserve inviolably and confirm. Which constitution is also to serve the Grand Duchy of Lithuania

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-134">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-134">p. 134</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

ma, względem rozgraniczenia Dobr Nasżych Stołowych, y Ziemskich w Korone, y w W. X. Litewskim leżących.

##### *Forum Ludzi Stanu Szlácheckiego, z Duchownemi in Civilibus realibus Koronnych, y W. X. Lit.*

^[*Kon. 1635. Fol. 857. Tomo 3tio.*] A zebyśmy iako in Criminalibus, ratione Fori, Stan Duchowny, z Stanem Szlacheckim, także też in civilibus realibus, do uspokoienia przyprowadzili: Postanawiamy, że co się tknie spraw reales, ratione simplicium injuriarum Civilium, Terrestrium ac Granicialium; oto ma być Forum każdego Szlachcica z Duchownym, ordinario Processu w Ziemstwie y na Polu, a per Apellationem na Trybunale máią się takowe sprawy odprawować, coram Judicio Composito; iako y drugie sprawy Duchowne. A gdyby ktora Osoba Duchowna nalazła się takowa żeby Prawo pospolite contemneret, y Bannicyą dáłá ná sobie otrzymać: tedy loci Ordinarius, ma executionem na nim uczynić, super Persona ipsius, & bonis generaliter omnibus, proprijs ipsius non tamen Terrestribus, usque; ad realem satisfactionem ukrzywdzonemu. Ludzie iednak Religij Greckiey, tak iako w pierwszey Konstytucyi, ratione Fori in Personalibus, przy swych dawnych zwyczaiach zostaią.

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

with respect to the delimitation of Our Table estates and terrestrial estates situated in the Crown and in the Grand Duchy of Lithuania.

---

**Translator's Note**

[T.N. 1] *Włóka Chełmińska* (Chełmno *włóka*, Latin *mansus Culmensis*): a unit of land area specific to Prussian law, equal to approximately 16.8 hectares or about 41.5 acres. Used as the threshold measure for whether appeals are permitted in Prussian boundary commissions.

##### *The Forum of Persons of Noble Estate with Ecclesiastical Persons in Real Civil Cases of the Crown and the Grand Duchy of Lithuania.*

^[*Constitution 1635, fol. 857, vol. 3.*] And so that, as in criminal cases on account of the forum, We might bring the ecclesiastical estate together with the noble estate to a settlement also in real civil cases: We enact that as regards real cases — on account of simple civil, terrestrial, and boundary injuries — the forum of every nobleman with an ecclesiastical person shall be, by ordinary process, before the land court and in the field; and such cases shall be conducted on appeal before the Tribunal *coram Judicio Composito*, like other ecclesiastical cases. And if any ecclesiastical person were found to be such as to hold the common law in contempt and to allow banishment to be obtained against him: the *loci Ordinarius* shall execute the same upon his person and upon all his estates generally — his own but not the terrestrial ones — until real satisfaction is given to the injured party. The people of the Greek faith, however, as in the first constitution in the matter of the forum *in Personalibus*, remain in their ancient customs.

</div>
</section>

<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-135">p. 135</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

##### *Exempty Żołnierzow.*

^[*Kon. 1627. Fol. 544. Tomo 3tio.*] Reasumuiąc Konstytucye dawne, o Exemptach Żołnierskich, za zgodą Seymową postanawiamy exceptis causis Konstytucyami Anni 1611mi & 1622. expressis Exempty tak z Kancellaryi Nasżych Oboyga Narodow iako y od Hetmanow Nasżych u wszelakich Sądow, aby przymowane były, waruiemy.

* * *

#### DE REVISIONIBUS
##### *Inter Bona ab utrinque Regalia, & Revisoribus a Sacra Regia Majestate assignatis.*

Revisiones locò Commissionum, inter Bona ab utrinque; Regalia, Diversæ tamen Possessionis & Tenutæ Capitaneorum, vel Tenutariorum, fieri solent, intercedente Differentia & controversia fundi, inter Tenutarios: Eo in passu ex Decreto Sacræ Regiæ Majestatis dantur, & deputantur, ad sopiendas Differentias, Revisores certi, non quidem ex Ordine Senatorio, & Officialium Terrestrium, verum ab Aula Regia è numero Secretariorum, Sacræ Regiæ Majestatis, selecti, vel quascunque; alias Personas

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

##### *Soldiers' Exemptions.*

^[*Constitution 1627, fol. 544, vol. 3.*] Reassuming the ancient constitutions on soldiers' exemptions, with the consent of the Sejm We enact and provide: that exemptions from both Our Chanceries of both Nations, as well as from Our Hetmans, shall be accepted before all courts — excepting the cases expressly specified by the constitutions of the years 1611 and 1622.

* * *

#### ON REVIEWS
##### *Between Estates on Both Sides Royal, and on Reviewers Assigned by His Sacred Royal Majesty.*

Reviews, in place of commissions, between estates on both sides royal but of different possession and leasehold of Captains or leaseholders, are customary when a difference and dispute over a plot arises between leaseholders. In that situation, by decree of His Sacred Royal Majesty, certain reviewers are given and deputed for the settling of differences — not indeed from the senatorial order and terrestrial officials, but selected from the royal court from among the secretaries of His Sacred Royal Majesty, or such other

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-136">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-136">p. 136</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

præcipuas & idoneas, Partes utrasque; a Sacra Regia Majestate impetrave rint, adhibito eis nihilominus Succamerario, vel Camerario, Terræ vel Palatinatûs ejusdem, vel vacante ejus in eodem Palatinatu, ubi Bona consistunt, alio, etiam remotiori.

Qui quidem Revisores, tantæ sunt Authoritatis, ut Commissarij in decidendis fundi Controversijs, & Tenutarum, unius ab altera per Granicies Distinctione: Hoc excepto quod non habent absolutam Facultatem Decisionis finalis faciendæ, per Motionem seu Appellationem, quæ est Partibus a sententia eorum Definitiva, ad Sacram Regiam Majestatem post curiam libera decætero, ad instar Actûs Commissorialis, Commissionum inter Bona Regalia & Terrestria, fieri consueti. Requiruntur ad Actum Revisionis, Collaterales, ac eorum agnitio, si suis Parietibus ad Angulares Scopulos Bonorum Regalium attingunt: Tum senum subditorum ex utraque; Tenuta Statuitio & Inquisitio.

Maxime verò eo in Actu valet Pacifica ac diuturna Fundorum in alterutrum pacifice & continué tentorum Possessio. Quæ a senibus Juramento obligatis inquiritur, & Inquisitione probatur. Nam cum Bona sint ab utrinque; Juris Regij, nihil eis decedit, tametsi Parti alteri acciderit, nec Detrimentum pati possunt. Præterquam villani, ac sui Tenutarij a quorum scilicet Parte, Pars fundi avulsa, alteri Parti adjudicata

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

principal and suitable persons as both parties shall have obtained from His Sacred Royal Majesty; with the Sub-Chamberlain or Boundary Chamberlain of that land or palatinate, or in his absence in that palatinate where the estates are situated, another, even from a more distant one, added to them.

Which reviewers have authority equal to that of commissioners in deciding plot disputes and the distinction of leaseholdings one from the other by boundaries — except that they do not have the absolute faculty of making a final decision, there being open to the parties hereafter from their final sentence, to His Sacred Royal Majesty after the court, a free appeal — after the manner of the commissarial act, customarily made for commissions between royal and terrestrial estates. Collateral heirs are required at the review act, and their acknowledgement, if their boundary walls meet the corner mounds of the royal estates; and likewise the production and sworn inquiry of elderly subjects from both leaseholdings.

Most of all in that act, the peaceful and long-standing possession of plots held by the one side peaceably and continuously against the other prevails. Which is inquired of by elderly witnesses bound by oath and proved by sworn inquiry. For since the estates are on both sides of royal right, nothing falls away from them even if it has accrued to one party, nor can they suffer detriment — except for the villagers and their leaseholders from whose side a portion of the plot avulsed has been adjudged

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-137">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-137">p. 137</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

fuerit: Cujus rei cognitio, & Disjudicatio Judicio Revisoriali subest. Qui vero Revisores utrarumque; Partium Ductibus lustratis ac revisis, attenta agnitione Collateralium visis signis metallicis & Scopulis realibus, præcipuè autem facta; usus & Domini, continui, tum & senum testimonio, diligenti Investigatione eidem Parti quæ de possessione pacifica docuerit, signa Granicialia demonstraverit, agnitione Collateralium nititur, Actoratum adjudicabunt, & Scopulos Ductu eodem agnito tam Angulares, seu Aciales, tum Parietales, præcluso Juramento Tenutarijs ipsis, prout in talibus fieri solet, in ambiguis & dubijs utriusque; Partis Ductibus Rem & Negotium Limitum ex æquo & justo moderantur, sententia sua revisoriali ad id accedente: A qua si appellatum fuerit, ac in Termino ex Appellatione incidenti, Sacra Regia Majestas sententiam Revisorum approbaverit, Granicies illæ sunt validæ semper, & robur fortiuntur firmitatis perpetuæ: Ratione vero violentiarum, si quæ interfuerint, Forum in Judicio Sacræ Regiæ Majestatis post curiam tanquam Tenutarijs, de Tenuta Regali assignatur.

##### *Exempty Żołnierzom, y innym in Legationibus posłánym.*

^[*1638 Kon. Fol. 1638.*] Exempty Żołnierzom w Obozie, albo na Woinie y expedycyi, y ná posłudze Rzeczypospolitey

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

to the other party; the cognisance and adjudication of which matter falls to the revisory court. The reviewers, both parties' boundary paths having been examined and reviewed, with due regard to the acknowledgement of the collateral heirs, the metal markers and real mounds having been examined, but above all a careful investigation having been made of continuous use and lordship, and of the testimony of elderly witnesses — shall adjudge the right of path to that party which has proved peaceful possession, demonstrated boundary markers, and relies on the acknowledgement of the collateral heirs; and shall moderate the matter and business of the boundaries of the acknowledged boundary path — both corner and particular corner mounds, and wall mounds — the oath being barred to the leaseholders themselves, as is customary in such matters of ambiguous and doubtful boundary paths of both parties, by their revisory sentence. From which, if an appeal shall have been made, and at the session arising from the appeal His Sacred Royal Majesty shall have approved the reviewers' sentence, those boundaries are always valid and acquire the force of perpetual firmness. As regards acts of violence, if any occurred, the forum is assigned in the court of His Sacred Royal Majesty after the court as for leaseholders of the royal leasehold.

##### *Exemptions for soldiers and others sent on embassies.*

^[*Constitution 1638, fol. 1638.*] Exemptions for soldiers in camp, or on a war footing or expedition, or

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-138">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-138">p. 138</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

będącymi; aby w tych sprawách ktore dawne Konstytucye opisáły, y ocerklowały, wszelkie subsellia in personalibus tylko przyimowały za Atestacyą Hetmanow, albo Pułkownikow na mieyscu Hetmanow będących waruiemy. Także y tym ktorzy lubo od Hetmanow Násżych w spráwách Rzeczypospolitey, lub od Nas samych, w possudze także Nasżych y Rzeczypospolitey, ad exteros Principes, & ad externas Respublicas, wysyłáni in legatione będą.

##### *Exempty Żołnierskie*

^[*Kon. 1673. Fol. 105. Tomo 5to.*] Konstytucye przeszłe o Exempty w spráwách Żołnierskich reasumuiemy do ktorych Trybunały y insze subsellia w spráwách, w ktorych według Prawá iść powinny, záchować się máią według dawnego Práwá.

##### *Exempty*

^[*Kon. 1676. Fol. 363. Tomo 5to.*] Exempty Wielmożnych Hetmánow Násżych iáko są Prawem pozwolone dawnym, ták żeby w każdym Sądzie miały wagę swoię, in personalibus tylko Terminis: deklaruiemy: Ieżeliby záś pod tęráźnieysze Sądy Kápturowe gdziekolwiek Kondemnaty ná osobách Żołnierskich w Obozách zostáiących in Personalibus Terminis stánęły; tákże Kondemnaty, ktore przeciwko Práwu, Authoritate præsentis Conventus, znosiemy, negotio principali in integrum restituto.

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

in the service of the Commonwealth — We provide that all courts shall accept the same *in personalibus* only in those cases that the ancient constitutions have described and circumscribed, upon the attestation of the Hetmans, or the colonels acting in place of the Hetmans. And likewise for those who shall be sent on an embassy — whether by Our Hetmans in the affairs of the Commonwealth, or by Us ourselves, likewise in the service of Ours and the Commonwealth's, *ad exteros Principes & ad externas Respublicas*.

##### *Soldiers' exemptions.*

^[*Constitution 1673, fol. 105, vol. 5.*] We reassume the prior constitutions on exemptions in soldiers' cases, which the Tribunals and other courts are to observe in the cases in which they are required to proceed according to law, according to the ancient law.

##### *Exemptions.*

^[*Constitution 1676, fol. 363, vol. 5.*] The exemptions of Our Right Honourable Hetmans, as they are permitted by the ancient law — so that they may have their force in every court *in personalibus* only: We declare. If, however, condemnations should stand against soldiers' persons remaining in camp *in Personalibus Terminis* before any present cap-tribunal courts anywhere: such condemnations likewise, which are contrary to law, We annul by the authority of the present convention, the principal matter restored *in integrum*.

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-139">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-139">p. 139</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

Quandoquidem Militibus servitium Bellicum^[*Dilatio Exemptus.*] exercentibus, & in actuali obsequio Reipublicæ existentibus, circumscripta & concessa est tot Constitutionibus Anni dilatio exemptus in omni Judicio & subsellio Regni: proinde possunt uti, hoc beneficio Legis, in causa Granicierum, coram Judicio Succamerariali Campestri, tam Actor, quam citatus, (prævia tamen reproducenda Attestatione illo in Judicio) quoniam personalitas requiritur, ad lucrandum Ductum. Etiam & Collaterali debet concedi hæc Dilatio, quando non est visibilis Angularitas, vel acialitas, & quæstioni subest.

##### *De Succamerariorum Officio ac potestate.*

^[*Herburt Fol. 478.*] Primo autem omnium, Succamerarius in negotio Limitationis naturalem Limitem spectet; nempe rivulum aut fluvium interfuentem seu alveum novum & antiquum alias Rzeczylko Hereditates limitantem.

Crebra altercatione inter nostros subditos super Limitibus in Hereditatibus ipsorum distingvendis graves suscitantur quæstiones: ex quibus augentur expensæ, & labori nimio interdum supponuntur discordantes. Et quia fluvij & fluviorum alvei, ut plurimum ^[*Succamerarius naturale Limite spectet.*] inter hereditates, Possessiones & Villas fluunt & habentur. Unde si quæ Villæ aut Hereditates invicem se

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

Since to soldiers performing military service ^[*Postponement of soldier's exemption.*] and in actual service of the Commonwealth, the postponement of soldier's exemption has been circumscribed and granted by so many constitutions in every court and tribunal of the Kingdom — both the plaintiff and the respondent may therefore avail themselves of this benefit of the law in boundary cases before the field Sub-Chamberlain's court (with prior reproduction of the attestation before that court), since personal appearance is required for the winning of the boundary path. This postponement must also be granted to the collateral heir when the corner point or particular corner point is not visible and is subject to dispute.

##### *On the Office and Power of the Sub-Chamberlains.*

^[*Herburt, fol. 478.*] First of all, the Sub-Chamberlain in the matter of delimitation shall consider the natural boundary — namely a stream or river flowing between, or a new or ancient channel otherwise called *Rzeczyłko*, delimiting the estates.

Frequent disputes between Our subjects over boundaries in distinguishing their estates give rise to serious questions, from which expenses increase and those in disagreement are sometimes subjected to excessive labour. And since rivers and river channels for the most part ^[*The Sub-Chamberlain shall consider the natural boundary.*] flow and are held between estates, possessions, and villages — if any villages or estates meet one another

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-140">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-140">p. 140</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

conveniunt, aliquo fluvio vel rivo mediante, si inter easdem Hereditates non sunt Limites de Jure facti: tunc illa quælibet Hereditas suam teneat ripam illius fluminis seu rivi, ad illam Hereditatem protensam: & idem fluvius seu rivus pro limite semper sit habendus. Qui fluvius seu rivus, si etiam omisso primævo ^[*Antiquus meatus fluvij pro Limite assignatur.*] alveo per alia loca suum divertit meatum seu defluxum, sine ministerio hominis: sed propria virtute naturali, nihilominus ejusdem fluvij primævus alveus, ubi ante decursus aquæ fuit, verus limes Hereditatum illarum erit. In quo antiquo fluvio seu rivo alias antiqui fluvij alveo, homines utramque; villam seu Hereditatem illam inhabitantes, licite suas facient piscaturas, prout faciebant tunc, cum aqua primævum, seu antiquum cursum in eodem antiquo alveo habuit. Sed si fluvius seu rivus hujusmodi per hominis industriam aversus esset ab antiquo cursu & alveo, ut in alium novum influeret alveum; tunc ille novus alveus Hereditates limitabit: dabit quoque; sic ut antiquus ille alveus dare consuevit. Unam ripam uni hereditati, alteram alteri quam alluit. Quo casu dum per hominis industriam sit fluminis aversio, una hereditatum ipsarum illa videlicet, in qua remanet antiquus alveus, omnem & integram habeat proprietatem in alveo, & ripis antiqui fluminis seu rivi.

^[*Herburt Fol. 479.*] Præterea eum qui notabiliora signa Limitum ostendit, Succamerarius sub honore & conscientia,

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

by the mediation of any river or stream, and between those same estates there are no legally made boundaries: then every such estate shall hold its own bank of that river or stream, extended to that estate; and that same river or stream shall always be held as the boundary. Which river or stream, if even abandoning its original ^[*The ancient course of a river is assigned as the boundary.*] channel it diverts its course or flow through other places, without the agency of man but by its own natural force — nonetheless the original channel of that same river, where the flow of water previously was, shall be the true boundary of those estates. In which ancient river or stream, otherwise the channel of the ancient river, the people inhabiting either of those villages or estates may lawfully carry out their fishing, as they did when the water had its original or ancient course in that same ancient channel. But if a river or stream of this kind were diverted by the industry of man from its ancient course and channel, so as to flow into another new channel — then that new channel shall delimit the estates; and shall yield likewise as that ancient channel was accustomed to yield: one bank to one estate, the other to the other which it washes. In which case, when the diversion of the river is by the industry of man, one of those same estates — namely that in which the ancient channel remains — shall have full and entire ownership of the channel and banks of the ancient river or stream.

^[*Herburt, fol. 479.*] Moreover, the Sub-Chamberlain, under his honour and conscience,

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-141">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-141">p. 141</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

admittit ad probandum: deficientibus autem signis ex utraque; parte, Actori cum Testibus probatio deferatur.

De Limitibus autem nostrorum Nobilium, taliter ^[*Qui probabiliora signa ostenderit ad comprobationem admittatur.*] duximus providendum: quod si contigerit aliquem litigantem pro limitibus docere, vel ostendere Scopulos dictos kopce ad minus tres, aut alia signa notabilia in campis vulgariter Wiazdy: in Sylvis vero signa ad instar crucis facta, ut est moris ex tunc Succamerarius Noster, illum qui meliora & notabiliora signa seu Scopulos ostenderit, discernens, sub honore & conscientia debebit & tenebitur mittere ad probandum. Ubi autem utraque; pars signis notabilibus inter hereditates caret: ex tunc Actor ad probationem Limitum cum testibus prout de Jure est, admittatur.

##### *Succamerarius Terminum Partibus non transponat; nisi consentiant, aut Causa subsit.*

^[*Herburt Ibidem.*] Cum autem plerumque; Succamerarij, aut eorum Camerarij transponerent Limitationes Granicierum, aut favore quodam, aut partis unius necessitate accedente, altera nolente, exindeque; cum neglientia per Dilationes favorabiles in Limitatione Granicierum committeretur. Nos huic errori providentes, decrevimus, & decernimus perpetuo observandum: Quod à modo Succamerarij omnium Terrarum totius Regni, Terminos Graniciales per semetipsos pro suo velle, contra Juris Ordinem transponere

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

admits the party who shows more notable boundary markers to prove his case; but where the markers are lacking on both sides, proof shall be offered to the plaintiff with witnesses.

As regards the boundaries of Our nobility, We have thought fit to ^[*The party who shows more probable markers shall be admitted to confirmation.*] provide as follows: that if it should happen that any litigant for boundaries should show or produce the mounds called *kopce* — at least three — or other notable markers in the open country, commonly *wiązdy*; and in forests, markers in the manner of a cross, as is the custom — then Our Sub-Chamberlain, discerning the one who has shown better and more notable markers or mounds, shall under his honour and conscience be required and bound to send him to prove his case. Where, however, both parties lack notable markers between the estates — then the plaintiff shall be admitted to prove the boundaries with witnesses as the law requires.

##### *The Sub-Chamberlain shall not transpose the session of the parties, unless they consent or a cause exists.*

^[*Herburt, ibid.*] Since Sub-Chamberlains or their Boundary Chamberlains for the most part transposed the delimitations of boundaries, either out of favour or on account of the necessity of one party, the other being unwilling — and from this, through negligence, favourable delays were committed in the delimitation of boundaries — We, providing for this error, have decreed and decree to be perpetually observed: that from now on, the Sub-Chamberlains of all lands of the entire Kingdom shall not transpose boundary sessions of their own accord, against the order of the law,

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-142">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-142">p. 142</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

re non debeant, sub pæna 14 Marcarum in eo qui aliter fecerit per nos exigenda: excepto quod accederet ad id consensus Partium, aut causa interveniret legitima.

Condemnatus in Judicio Terrestri, in campo^[*Herburt Fol. 477.*] ad controvertendum non admittitur: quinimò non obstantibus quibusvis etiam Documentis Literatorijs vel signis evidentioribus, si quæ reus protunc ostenderit, Actoris tamen arbitrio ductioni ac Juramento cum Testibus standum est.

In quo quidem Termino Campestri non licebit citato ad quas controversia facere coram Succamerario vel ejus Officio super Actoratu: quem jam in Judicio Terrestri amisit: sed Actor ducet Officium juxta suam voluntatem, & suam conscientiam: facietque; Granicies Scopulos sipando, & alia signa metallia faciendo: non obstante quavis contradictione citati, nec quibusvis Documentis Literatorijs, vel signis evidentioribus, si quæ protunc ostenderit. Quibus ^[*Comprobet Actor met Septimus.*] finitis circa finalem & acialem Scopulum jurabit Actor cum sex Testibus sibi genere similibus super vera & justa Limitatione: ita videlicet, quod nihil Terræ & Hereditatis citato ademit, & ab eo alienavit.

Porro si Condemnatus etiam in Campo non paruerit, ^[*Herburt Fol. 478.*] idem sit, quin ubi Actor præstiterit Juramentum cum Testibus, Succamerarius decernit perpetuo duraturos.

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

under a penalty of fourteen marks to be exacted by Us from whoever shall do otherwise — except where the consent of the parties shall concur, or a legitimate cause shall intervene.

A party condemned in the land court ^[*Herburt, fol. 477.*] is not admitted to contest in the field; indeed, notwithstanding any documentary evidence whatsoever, or more apparent signs, if the defendant should produce any at that time, it is nonetheless to stand with the plaintiff's conduct and the oath with witnesses.

At that same field session, it shall not be permitted to the cited party to raise any contest before the Sub-Chamberlain or his office regarding the right of path — which he has already lost in the land court — but the plaintiff shall lead the office according to his own will and conscience; and shall make the boundaries by raising mounds and making other metal markers, notwithstanding any opposition whatsoever of the cited party, nor any documentary evidence or more apparent signs, if any he should produce at that time. ^[*The plaintiff shall confirm as seventh.*] When these things are done, beside the final and particular corner mound, the plaintiff shall swear with six witnesses of equal rank regarding the true and just delimitation: that he has taken nothing of the land and estate from the cited party, and alienated nothing from him.

Furthermore, if the condemned party should not comply even in the field, ^[*Herburt, fol. 478.*] the same applies: where the plaintiff shall have sworn the oath with witnesses, the Sub-Chamberlain decrees that the boundaries shall endure in perpetuity.

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-143">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-143">p. 143</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

Quod si in Termino Campestri Citatus non comparuerit, sed contumax exstiterit: nihilominus Succamerarius vel ejus Officium tenebitur Actorem sequi, & facere Granicies juxta Ductionem ipsius: Nihilominus circa ultimum & acialem Scopulum Actor Jurabit cum Testibus, non obstante absentia Citati, tantummodo in præsentia Officij Succamerariatus. Quas Granicies sicut præmissum est factas, & Juramento Actoris comprobatas, Succamerarius aut ejus Officium decernet perpetuò duraturas & tenendas.

#### MINORENNES.
##### *Si Coheredes citati essent Minorennes in Causa Limitum.*

^[*Herburt Fol. 283.*] Si verò plures essent participes, seu confortes, unus & ejusdem Hereditatis, Citati per quempiam alium, pro faciendis Graniciebus: & inter tales Citatos, essent aliqui Coheredes Minorennes æque citati:^[*Minorennes tenentur respondere in causa Granicierum.*] ex tunc, contra tales Judicium procederet, tam Terrestre, quam Officium Succamerarij: non obstante ipsorum Minorennitate. Ad quam quidem Limitationem Judicium & Officium Succamerarij procedet. Nihilominus: Succamerarius, vel ejus Officium, refervabit eis actionem, pro eorum interesse, cum Actore Granicierum; ^[*Salva reservata Actio Minorennii*] dum videlicet Annos Discretionis assequentur: tunc licebit eis Jure agere cum illis,

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

If at the field session the respondent should fail to appear and be in contumacy: the Sub-Chamberlain or his office shall nonetheless be required to follow the plaintiff and make the boundaries according to his leading. Nonetheless beside the final and particular corner mound, the plaintiff shall swear with witnesses, notwithstanding the absence of the cited party, in the presence only of the Sub-Chamberlain's office. Which boundaries, made as aforesaid and confirmed by the oath of the plaintiff, the Sub-Chamberlain or his office shall decree to endure and be kept in perpetuity.

#### MINORS.
##### *If Co-Heirs Cited in a Boundary Case Were Minors.*

^[*Herburt, fol. 283.*] If there were several participants or co-parties of one and the same estate, cited by some other person for the making of boundaries; and among those cited parties there were some co-heirs who were minors, likewise cited — ^[*Minors are required to respond in a boundary case.*] then proceedings shall be taken against such parties, both by the land court and by the Sub-Chamberlain's office, notwithstanding their minority. To which delimitation the court and Sub-Chamberlain's office shall proceed. Nevertheless the Sub-Chamberlain or his office shall reserve for them an action for their interest against the plaintiff of the boundaries; ^[*The reserved action of the minor is saved.*] namely when they shall attain the years of discretion — then it shall be lawful for them to proceed at law against those

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-144">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-144">p. 144</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

a quibus putarent sibi esse præjudicatum: tamen non nisi pro forte & interesse, ipsos concernente, agent.

##### *Minorennes cum Annos Discretionis attingent, pro Injurijs acceptis agant: Præscriptione illis non obstante.*

^[*Herburt Ibidem.*] Infantibus præsertim egenis, succurrere & providere volentes, Statuimus: Ut si aliquibus, Patre mortuo, per quempiam injuria fuerit illata: venientes ad Annos Discretionis, possent Juridicè agere contra Injuriantes, & exceptio Præscriptionis eis opponi non poterit: nisi tunc ætate in eis legitima completa. Et infra tempus debitum, si agere neglexerint, pro injurijs olim eis illatis, tunc obstat eis exceptio præscriptionis: propter negligentiam allegatam.

##### *Collaterales debent esse adcitati,*

^[*Herburt Fol. 475.*] Contra vicinos seu Collaterales, si sint simul cum Citato, necessario tam ad Terrestre, quam ad Campestre Judicium Citandis, sic est procedendum, ne in Campo Limitationem impediant.

SI verò essent alij Nobiles, habentes vicinas

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

by whom they should consider themselves to have been prejudiced; yet they shall proceed only for their own portion and interest.

##### *When Minors Reach the Years of Discretion, They May Proceed for Injuries Suffered; Prescription Not Obstructing Them.*

^[*Herburt, ibid.*] Wishing to succour and provide for children, especially those in need, We enact: that if an injury shall have been done to any persons by someone following the death of their father — on coming to the years of discretion, they may proceed at law against those who committed the injury, and the exception of prescription cannot be opposed to them, unless the lawful age be then fully completed in them. And if within the due time they shall have neglected to proceed for the injuries once done to them, then the exception of prescription stands against them on account of the negligence alleged.

##### *Collateral Heirs Must Be Summoned.*

^[*Herburt, fol. 475.*] Against neighbours or collateral heirs, who if together with the cited party must necessarily be cited both to the land court and to the field court, the following procedure must be followed, so that they may not obstruct the delimitation in the field.

If, however, there were other noblemen having neighbouring

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-145">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-145">p. 145</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

hereditates, quarum parietes adjacerent, & essent contigui hereditatibus, inter quas tales Granicies fieri deberent, putarentque; se habere interesse, ex vi contiguitatis, ad tales Granicies: tales etiam vicini Collaterales etiam per Actorem pro uno Termino, cum principali ad Judicium Terrestre adcitentur: & similiter prout cum principali, ita & cum eis per Actorem procedetur, ne in Termino Campestri Granicies possint impedire.

#### SCOPULUS.
##### *Disjectorum Scopulorum pæna & satisfactio.*

^[*Herburt Fol. 447.*] Si quis autem Scopulos erectos, vel alia signa metallia, vel in parte, vel in toto disjecerit, annihilaveritque; violenter; talis violator, a quolibet Scopulo Aciali, luet parti contra se Agenti, per tres Marcas pecuniæ usualis. Ab alijs vero Scopulis disjectis, & signis quibuscunque; metalibus destructis & Annihilatis, luet per sex Scotos. Et nihilominus idem violator teneatur Officium Succamerarij ad locum Granicierum violatarum, sumptibus suis educere: & eosdem Limites à se disjectos restaurare & de novo erigere, quod facere tenebitur, post Avisationem per Ministeriale & Nobiles, ex parte talem Injuriam passi infra octo Septimanas: sub pæna 14 Marcarum parti & totidem Judicio succumbenda: Qua pæna soluta in

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

estates, whose boundary walls were adjacent and contiguous to the estates between which such boundaries were to be made, and who considered themselves to have an interest by virtue of their contiguity in those same boundaries — such neighbouring collateral heirs shall likewise be summoned by the plaintiff for one session, together with the principal party, before the land court; and likewise as with the principal party, so too shall the plaintiff proceed with them, so that they may not obstruct the boundaries at the field session.

#### MOUNDS.
##### *The Penalty and Satisfaction for Destroyed Mounds.*

^[*Herburt, fol. 447.*] If anyone shall have destroyed or violently annihilated — in part or in whole — any mounds erected, or other metal markers: such a violator shall pay to the party proceeding against him three marks of current money for every particular corner mound. For other mounds destroyed and all metal markers destroyed and annihilated, he shall pay six *skots*. And the same violator shall nonetheless be required to bring the Sub-Chamberlain's office to the place of the violated boundaries at his own expense, and to restore and newly erect those same boundaries destroyed by him — which he shall be required to do, after notification by the court messenger and noblemen on behalf of the party that suffered such injury, within eight weeks: under a penalty of fourteen marks succumbing to the party and the same to the court. Which penalty having been paid, in

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-146">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-146">p. 146</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

alijs octo Septimanis, tenebitur Granicies ita ut præmissum est restaurare, & sic toties quoties ipsas Granicies restaurare renuerit, & noluerit: toties 14 Marcas præfatas parti & totidem Judicio ad solvendum succumbet, in quibuslibet octo Septimanis.

##### *Inofficiose erigens Scopulos, quam pænam luit, & quomodo satisfacit.*

^[*Herburt Ibidem.*] Si quis in alieno solo, sive aliena hereditate fine Succamerario & ejus Officio, signa Granicialia suapte, sponte, & temere erexerit, & fecerit: talis etiam pari modo a quolibet Scopulo aciali tres Marcas: & ab alijs per sex Scotos (prout supra) succumbet ad solvendum. Et nihilominus Scopulos, & signa omnia metallia, taliter inofficiose facta officiose per Officium Succamerarij destruet, dissipabit, & annihilabit: ita ut in posterum, nullum præjudicium, parti injuriam passæ, ex eorum vestigiis evenire possit, quod facere tenebitur, sub pæna 14 Marcarum parti, & Judicio totidem. Et similiter contra talem procedetur, in octo Septimanis: prout & contra violentum Destructorem Granicierum ut supra descriptum est.

##### *Quæ Dilationes ad Campum Citato admittuntur.*

^[*Herburt Fol. 478.*] POterit autem Citatus Terminum talem Campestrem

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

the next eight weeks he shall be required to restore the boundaries as aforesaid; and as often as he shall refuse and be unwilling to restore those boundaries — so many times shall he forfeit the aforesaid fourteen marks to the party and the same to the court, in each successive eight weeks.

##### *What Penalty Is Paid by One Who Erects Mounds Without Authority, and How He Gives Satisfaction.*

^[*Herburt, ibid.*] If anyone on another's soil or estate, without the Sub-Chamberlain and his office, shall of his own accord, voluntarily, and rashly erect and make boundary markers — such a person shall likewise pay three marks for every particular corner mound, and six *skots* for others (as above). And nonetheless all the mounds and metal markers so unlawfully made shall be formally destroyed, removed, and annihilated by the Sub-Chamberlain's office: so that in future no prejudice may come to the injured party from any trace of them — which shall be done under a penalty of fourteen marks to the party and the same to the court. And proceedings shall similarly be taken against such a person, in each eight weeks: as against the violent destroyer of boundaries as described above.

##### *What Postponements Are Admitted to the Respondent in the Field.*

^[*Herburt, fol. 478.*] The cited party may, however, defer such a field session

</div>

vera infirmitate differre, similiter & pro majori & non aliter.

##### *O Podkomorzym w Mázowßu.*

^[*Excepta Mázow. Kon. 1576. Fol. 947. Tomo 2do.*] Podkomorzy Ziemski, wedle starego zwyczáiu w Woiewodztwie Mázowieckim záchowałego, sam przez się y przez Komorniká swego widzenie, y iáko ie wypráwować będzie też spráwy około gránic, ma odnosić do Sądu Ziemskiego: A że kontrowersye ktore przed nim álbo przed jego Komornikiem strony czynić będą, áby w Sądzie tákże przy nim czynili y wywodzili, tákież też y munimentá ktore między sobą strony máią okázowáli. A Sąd wedle stárego zwyczáiu y zeznánia Iego, á z kontrowersyi stron y wywodow álbo munimentow ich, iáko będzie wedle Prawá pospolitego rozumiał, ták powodstwo iáko y dowod, skazáć władzą ma. A to dla tego zdawná záchowáło się, iż Sąd z kontrowersyi dekret právny uczyni, y Pisarz Ziemski kontrowersyą dostatecznie wypisze: tedy Aktow Podkomorzemu áni jego Komorniku Práwo Pospolite mieć nie pozwoliło. A wszákże stronie wolna będzie áppellacya wedle stárego zwyczáiu.

##### *O czynieniu Gránic.*

^[*Ibidem.*] Gdy ieden Szláchcic drugiemu Szláchcicowi zácznię Akcyą, áby między onym Dziedzictwem ich, sobie przyległym, kędy Gránice nie były iedno

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

by true illness, and likewise by a principal postponement, and not otherwise.

##### *On the Sub-Chamberlain in Masovia.*

^[*Masovian Exceptions, Constitution 1576, fol. 947, vol. 2.*] The land Sub-Chamberlain, according to the ancient custom observed in the Palatinate of Masovia, shall himself and through his Boundary Chamberlain conduct the site inspection and how it shall be conducted, and similarly the cases concerning boundaries, and shall bring them before the land court. And so that the contests which the parties shall raise before him or his Boundary Chamberlain may also be raised and presented before the court in his presence, and likewise the title deeds that the parties have between themselves be shown. And the court, according to the ancient custom and his testimony, and from the contests of the parties and their presentations or title deeds, as it shall understand according to the common law — shall have the power to adjudge both the right to proceed and the proof. And this has been preserved from of old, because the court shall make a legal decree from the contest, and the land notary shall write down the contest sufficiently; the common law therefore has not and could not permit the Sub-Chamberlain or his Boundary Chamberlain to have records. Appeal, however, shall be free to the party according to the ancient custom.

##### *On the Making of Boundaries.*

^[*Ibid.*] When one nobleman brings an action against another nobleman, so that between those estates of theirs adjacent to one another — where the boundaries were

</div>
</section>

<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-148">p. 148</a></h2>

używánie á possesye stron, sądownie zácząłby: tedy Sąd ma skazáć y posłáć Urzędniki Ziemskie do tego należące, á ktora stroná przed nimi lepsze y znácznieysze znáki ukaże, y przez starsze y swiadki wywiodłáby swoię spokoyną posesyą ná owym Imieniu, poki y przez ktore mieyíce trzymáłá dziedzictwo swoie, á Urzędnicy oni przyięli by to, tedy tam máią być czynione gránice y kopce nowe usypáne, przez zbijánia strony drugiey. A ieżliby z obu stron żadnych znákow gránicznych nie widzieli álbo nie przyięli, tedy pozwány będzie miał powodstwo, y przez ktore mieyícá poydzie z Swiádkámi, y przysięże, pierwszego swego uchodu álbo przeyścia nieprzestępuiąc, tedy kopce álbo znáki Grániczne máią być czynione, wedle dawnego obyczáiu Ziemie.

##### *O Ponowiániu Gránic.*

^[*Herburt Fol. 948.*] Gdy ieden drugiego, pozywa, áby z nim ponowił gránice, y kopce usypał przez stáre znáki grániczne między dobrami Ich, ktore znáki y ściánę stárą álbo kopce miánowałby ná pozwie, y one przysięgą potwierdzić podeymowałby się, táki z prawá będzie miał początek wywodu y probácyi przez stárce z tákich znákow, ktore ieżli Urząd Ziemski przyimie, á strona druga będzie mu negowáłá, powod z Swiádkámi ma probować przez przysięgę, iż te znáki stáre są, y prawdziwe grániczne między Dobrámi

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

not fixed but only use and possession of the parties — he should begin proceedings in court: then the court shall adjudge and send the appropriate land officials; and whichever party shows better and more notable markers before them, and through elderly witnesses and witnesses would prove its peaceful possession of that estate — for how long and through what places it held its estate — and those officials should accept this: then there boundaries shall be made and new mounds erected, overriding the objection of the other party. And if on both sides they should see or accept no boundary markers: then the respondent shall have the right to proceed, and through what places he shall go with witnesses and shall swear, not transgressing the first boundary of his entry or passage — then mounds or boundary markers shall be made according to the ancient custom of the land.

##### *On the Renewal of Boundaries.*

^[*Herburt, fol. 948.*] When one person summons another to renew boundaries with him and erect mounds through the old boundary markers between their estates — which markers and ancient boundary wall or mounds he would name in the summons and undertake to confirm by oath — such a person shall by law have the priority of presentation and proof through elderly witnesses from those same markers. Which, if the land official accepts them and the other party denies them: the plaintiff with witnesses shall prove by oath that those markers are old and true boundary ones between those estates,

temi, álbo stárą ściáną, álbo między grániczną y kopce stáre, wedle ktorych znákow Polesyą swoię y używánie wywieść ma, á ták zá temi znákami ktore przeydzie á przysięże, tam Gránice máią być czynione y kopce usypáne, ktore świádki powod w Sądzie Ziemskim ná bliskich Rokách po skáźni ma mienić, y popisáć sposobem z rotą przysięgi; á ná mieyíce rożnic z temi świádkámi przeyścia swego od węgłá do węgłá przez mieyícá y znáki grániczne, iáko się wyżey pomieniło, przysięgę ná roku stronom od Urzędu náznáczonym przy onymże Urzędzie powinien będzie uczynić, ktorą spráwę wszystkie Urząd przerzeczony, kopcow y znákow gránicznych uczynionych álbo okrążonych przed Sądem Ziemskim zeznáć powinien będzie, y w pisáć w Księgi Ziemskie. A gdzieby ktora Przywiley miáłá, álbo iákie listy, álbo iákie zapisy ná Gránice, álbo ná pewne mieyícá grániczne, to iest urociłká pewne: tá strona będzie miáłá początek do powodu probować y podeprzeć te gránice y mieyícá, wedle muniment swych y dawnego zachowania. A to ieżliby od tákich praw álbo muniment przez nowe gránice uczynione potym nieodstąpił, á od uczynienia Gránic, y usypánia álbo okrążenia kopcow, tedy Podkomorzemu puskopy, to iest trzydzieści groszy, á Stároście drugą trzydzieści groszy Polskiey monety, obiedwie stronie, pozwána y powodowa zápłácić máią, á Woźnemu sześć

or the ancient boundary wall, or between the boundary road and the old mounds; according to which markers he shall present his passage and use; and so following those markers through which he passes and swears, there boundaries shall be made and mounds erected. Which witnesses the plaintiff shall name and enter before the land court at the nearest sitting after the judgment, in the manner with an oath formula; and at the place of the differences with those witnesses of his passage from corner to corner through the places and boundary markers, as mentioned above — he shall be required to swear at the sitting appointed to the parties by the official before that same official. Which act in its entirety the aforesaid official shall be required to declare before the land court of the mounds and boundary markers made or circled, and to enter them in the land records. And where one party should have a privilege, or any letters, or any deeds concerning the boundaries or certain boundary places — namely certain boundary features — that party shall have priority in presenting and supporting those boundaries and places, according to its title deeds and ancient observance. And this — if from that time on it should not depart from such laws or title deeds through newly made boundaries, and from the making of boundaries and the erection or circling of mounds — then both parties, both the respondent and the plaintiff, shall pay the Sub-Chamberlain the fee of *puskopy*, that is thirty groschen, and the Starosta another thirty groschen of Polish currency; and to the court messenger six

groszy od tychże gránic płácić máią. A gdzie by między stronámi z dobrey woli były usypáne kopce álbo gránice uczynione: tedy przerzeczonemu Urzędowi obiedwie stronie iedno 30 groszy dáć będą powinny á nie więcey: á Woźnemu groszy trzy.

##### *O władzy Urzędnikow ná Wizyi w Mázowßu.*

^[*Exceptd Mázowieckie 1576. Fol. 937. Tomo 2do.*] Gdy Komornik álbo Woźny złoży stronom pewny dzień ku odpráwowániu widzenia, ná Imieniu wedle pozwu, y ktore złożenie dniá byłoby Aktámi Ziemskiemi álbo Grodzkiemi opisáne, ieżliby ktora strona tego dniá wypráwienia widzenia náznáczonego nie pilnowáłá, áni by przed onem Urzędem ná mnieyscu y ná Roku widzenia stánęłá: tákowa stroná z tákiego zniesienia Urzędu przerzeczonego, przed Sądem ná przyszłych Rokách uczynionego, w utrácie rzeczy swey o ktorą się on pozew wiodł, y widzenie być miáło, ma być skazána y sądzona. A gdyby sam Urząd ná ten dzień náznáczony y zápisány, ku wypráwowániu tego widzenia, intercedente legali impedimento nieprzyiechał, tedy inny dzień ku wykonániu tego widzenia, tenże Urząd Podkomorski in sequentibus Terminis Terrestribus złożyć będzie y nań z iácháć powinien, gdyż Urzędem nikt trácić nie ma áni może. A ieżliby iedná strona drugą groźbámi iáwnemi, álbo iákiemi gwałty od widzenia odegnáłá,

<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

groschen from those same boundaries they are required to pay. And where mounds have been erected or boundaries made between the parties by mutual will: then both parties shall be required to give that same office only thirty groschen and no more; and to the court messenger three groschen.

##### *On the Authority of Officials at a Site Inspection in Masovia.*

^[*Masovian Exceptions 1576, fol. 937, vol. 2.*] When a Boundary Chamberlain or court messenger fixes a certain day for the parties for the conduct of a site inspection at the estate in accordance with the summons, and the fixing of that day has been recorded in the land or municipal records — if any party should not attend at that appointed day of the named inspection, nor appear before that official at the place and session of the inspection: such a party shall, from such a removal of that aforesaid official made before the court at the next sitting, be condemned and adjudged to the loss of the matter concerning which that summons was brought and the inspection was to take place. And if the official himself should fail to come on that appointed and recorded day for the conduct of that inspection, *intercedente legali impedimento*: then another day for the conduct of that inspection, the same Sub-Chamberlain's office shall fix at the next land sittings and shall be required to come to it — since no one should or may lose through the office. And if one party should drive away the other by open threats or any violence from the inspection,

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-151">

álbo słowy iákiemi nieuczciwemi á zwádliwemi naygrawáłá: przeciw tákowey Urząd on ma zupełną moc penas in Statuto Regni descriptas skazáć, ktore skazánie ma do Sądu odnieść, á Sąd zá słusznym dowodem ma ie potwierdzić wedle zeznánia onego Urzędu: á on przekażcá álbo gwałtownik widzenia, odpowiedáć ná pozew wedle prawá ma być powinien, bez widzenia iuż inszego wszelákiego, bez dylácyi żadney: od ktorego widzenia ten kto wziął sobie ná widzenie nie ma dáć więcey Komorniku álbo Woźnemu, iedno trzy grosze obiema, á oni też nie máią więcey wyciągáć.

##### *Obieránie Sądu Ziemskiego y Podkomorskiego y przysięgá Podkomorska,*

^[*Kon. 1588. Fol. 1211. Tomo 2do.*] Elekcya ná Obieránie Urzędnikow Sądu Ziemskiego, post cessum vel decessum ktorego z nich, wedle stárego zwyczáiu, Woiewoda, & in defectu Kásztelan głowny onego Woiewodztwa álbo Ziemie, naydáley w sześć Niedziel, złożyć ma, ná mieyíce zwykłe. A gdyby Woiewodá onego Seymiku do sześci Niedziel nie złożył, tedy to będzie powinien Kásztelan uczynić. Tymże obyczáiem Podkomorzego Ziemskiego Elekcya ma dochodzić ktory też przysięgły być ma od tego czasu, exceptis modernis Possessoribus, do Daty, tey konstytucyi. A

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

or should mock it with any dishonourable or quarrelsome words: against such a party that office has full power to adjudge *penas in Statuto Regni descriptas*, which adjudication it shall bring before the court, and the court on sufficient proof shall confirm the same according to the testimony of that official; and that obstructor or violent hinderer of the inspection shall be required to respond to a summons according to law, without any further inspection, without any postponement. For which inspection the person who took a site inspection need give no more to the Boundary Chamberlain or court messenger than three groschen to both, and they too shall not demand more.

##### *The Election of the Land Court and the Sub-Chamberlain's Court, and the Sub-Chamberlain's Oath.*

^[*Constitution 1588, fol. 1211, vol. 2.*] The election for the election of officials of the land court — after the departure or death of any of them — according to the ancient custom, the Palatine, and in his absence the senior Castellan of that palatinate or land, shall fix at the latest within six weeks at the customary place. And if the Palatine should not fix that sitting within six weeks, then the Castellan shall be required to do so. In the same manner shall the election of the land Sub-Chamberlain proceed, who is also required to be sworn from that time — excepting the present holders, from the date of this constitution. And

</div>
</section>

<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-152">p. 152</a></h2>
<div class="lit-orig" lang="la" markdown="1">

tákowe Elekcye, opátruiemy eadem securitate, iáko y Trybunálskich Sędziow. A Podkomorzy ma ták przysięgáć: ^[*Rota Juramentu Wiel: Podkomorzego.*] Ja N. przysięgam Pánu BOGU, iż spráwiedliwie, według Páná BOGA, prawá pisanego, spráwiedliwości kontrowersyi stron sądzić, tychże kontrowersyi pilnie słucháć, y one przyimowáć: tákże znáki grániczne y podobieństwa ich uważáć, y wiernie opisowáć: ktemu Księgi swe, y Przodkow swych, wiernie chowáć, y w nie wpisowáć będę, ná ktorym Sądzie nie mam mieć względu, ná żadną Osobę, ták Przyiaciela, iáko y Nieprzyiacielá: bogátego y ubogiego: gościa y swoiego: nikomu w tym moim Sądzie, żadney przyiaźni y nieprzyiaźni nie pokázuiąc; y dáru ni od kogo względem tego Sądu nie bioráć. Ták mi Pánie Boże pomoż y tá S. Ewangelia.

##### *O relaxacyi y zniesienia Bánnicyi.*

^[*Eodem Anno Fol. 1220. Tomo 2do.*] Gdy by się kto będąc Bannitem, z stroną poiednał, á od niey sámey z tey Bannicyi kwit otrzymał: tedy tákowemu relaxatio Bannitionis z Kancellaryi Násżey ma być dána, ktorą naydáley do dwunástu Niedziel po kwicie ma sobie wypráwić, á w tym czasie u Prawa każdego będzie miał locum standi & inscriptiones, quasvis recognoscendi. A iż też Spráwy y ákcye wszystkie zwykli jure victi rozmáitemi sposoby trudnić: przeto opátruiemy, że ná każdey instancyi, etiam Bannitionis in ipso puncto będzie

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

such elections We safeguard with the same security as the Tribunal judges. And the Sub-Chamberlain shall swear as follows: ^[*The oath formula of the Right Honourable Sub-Chamberlain.*] I, [name], swear to the Lord GOD that I will judge justly, according to the Lord GOD, the written law, and the justice of the parties' contests, listen carefully to those contests and receive them; likewise consider the boundary markers and their probabilities, and faithfully describe them; and likewise keep my records and those of my predecessors faithfully, and enter matters in them; and at which court I shall have no regard for any person — neither friend nor enemy, rich nor poor, stranger nor kinsman — showing no favour or disfavour to anyone in that my court; and taking no gift from anyone with respect to that court. So help me Lord God, and this Holy Gospel.

##### *On the Relaxation and Annulment of Banishment.*

^[*Same year, fol. 1220, vol. 2.*] If anyone being under banishment should reconcile himself with the opposing party and receive from that party itself a release from that banishment: then such a person shall be given a relaxation of banishment from Our Chancery, which he shall obtain at the latest within twelve weeks after the release; and during that time he shall have standing before every court, and may register any recognitions. And since all cases and actions are customarily obstructed by the party defeated in law by various means: therefore We provide that at every instance — even *Bannitionis in ipso puncto* — the party defeated in law shall be

</div>
</section>

jure ^[*Male obtentum probować należy.*] victus powinien inferowáć male obtentum nie czynić o to osobney ákcyi. A w tákiey mierze ma być wolna appellacya ná Trybunał. Záczym gdy uzna Trybunał dobry on być Proces, y banniowałby jure victum, iuż takiemu sublacya dawána być nie ma: wszákże po bánnicyi tákiey, czasu wolnego dwánáście Niedziel będzie miał jure victus do kompozycyi cum jure vincente: y zapisy ku uspokoieniu oney spráwy móc będzie zeznawáć, dysponowáć, przedawáć Dobrá swoie, á kredytorom się iścić authoritate præsentis Constitutionis mediante, alias ieżli się nie godzi, iuż ná onym wywołánym Starostá exekucyą czynić będzie powinien. A ieżliby in contumaciam na tym bánnicya otrzymana byłá, tedy zá pozwániem Starosty onego y strony przeciwney, przez Bannita ná Trybunał, może táką Bannicyą znieść swym juramentem, choćby sublacyi jure victus nie miał. A gdyby też per definitivam sententiám ktoregokolwiek prawá Bannicya zniesiona z kogo byłá, tam iuż relaxacyi z Kancellaryi Násżey nie będzie trzebá. Tákże gdzieby komu w ktorymkolwiek práwie Bánnicya in Contumaciam otrzymána zadana byłá: tedy záraz super ignorantiam illius Bannitionis ^[*Super ignorantiam processus Evasio concessa.*] przysięgłszy, ma mieć locum standi in illo Judicio, potym onę Bánnicyą obyczáiem wyżey opisánym z siebie złożyć.

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

required to allege ^[*A wrongly obtained process is to be proved.*] *male obtentum* without bringing a separate action therefor. And in such a measure a free appeal to the Tribunal shall be permitted. And when the Tribunal shall recognise that process to be good and shall have imposed banishment on the party defeated in law: no annulment shall be given to such a person any longer. Nevertheless after such banishment, the party defeated in law shall have twelve weeks' free time for a settlement *cum jure vincente*; and may make deeds for the settlement of that case, may dispose of and sell its estates, and satisfy creditors *authoritate præsentis Constitutionis mediante* — otherwise if it is not willing, then the Starosta shall already be required to carry out execution against that person under banishment. And if the banishment was obtained *in contumaciam*: then at the summons of that Starosta and the opposing party by the person under banishment before the Tribunal, he may annul that banishment by his oath, even if he has no annulment *jure victus*. And where from anyone in any case banishment has been lifted by *per definitivam sententiam*: there no relaxation from Our Chancery shall be needed. Likewise where banishment has been imposed on anyone *in Contumaciam* in any case: immediately ^[*A discharge is granted on account of ignorance of the process.*] upon swearing *super ignorantiam illius Bannitionis*, he shall have standing *in illo Judicio*, and thereafter may remove that banishment from himself in the manner described above.

##### *O Surrogácyach Podkomorskich.*

Tomo 2do.*] Iż právem wárováno iest, áby Podkomorzowie Surrogátory w swych spráwách własnych ná swe mieyíce subdelegowáli: ci iednák Surrogátorowie ad hunc solum Actum muszą być; y potrzebá tego áby byli przysięgli, iáko y Podkomorzowie; záczym z trudnością ludzi dobrego rozsądku, y existymácyi uczciwey do tego przywodzić. Przetoż ták postánawiamy, áby káżdy Podkomorzy gdy spráwę swą mieć będzie, viciniorem Succamerarium vel ejus Camerarium, álbo Urzędnika Sądowego przysięgłego swey Ziemie, ábo Powiátu do tákowych Aktow zwieść był powinien: ktory iáko Urzędnik przysięgły, grániczny Akt odpráwić ma, podpisawszy innotescencyą swą, sub pæna de Commissarijs sancita ná czas złożony ziechać będzie powinien. ##### *On Sub-Chamberlain's Surrogacies.*

^[*Constitution 1601, fol. 1503, vol. 2.*] Since it is provided by law that Sub-Chamberlains shall sub-delegate surrogates to their places in their own cases — those surrogates, however, must be for that one act only; and it is necessary that they be sworn, as the Sub-Chamberlains are; and it is difficult to persuade persons of good judgment and honourable reputation to this. We therefore enact: that each Sub-Chamberlain, when he has his own case, shall be required to bring out the nearest Sub-Chamberlain or his Boundary Chamberlain, or a sworn judicial official of his land or district, for such acts; who as a sworn official shall conduct the boundary act, and having subscribed himself upon his summons letters, shall be required under the penalty enacted for commissioners to arrive at the appointed time. This is to be understood for future times.

##### *Ecclesiastical Persons in the Crown Holding Hereditary Estates.*

^[*Constitution 1607, fol. 1603, vol. 2.*] Since some ecclesiastical persons are accustomed to acquire terrestrial hereditary estates for themselves and their relatives and successors: We therefore declare that from such paternal estates and those serving them and their relatives and successors in whatever manner — and not given and founded for the Church — ecclesiastical persons shall be required to perform the military expedition, and shall be subject to all

</div>
</section>

wszystkim ciężarom iáko y Swiecki Stan podlegáć máią. Tákże też względem wszelákich krzywd, z gruntow, z Dobr pomienionych pochodzących; iákie w Ziemstwie, y w Grodzie, ukrzywdzonym odpowiádáć máią: tákże też gdy ex appellationibus, & remissionibus, na Trybunał Koronny tákowe spráwy przypadáć będą, sądzone być máią przez wszystkie Sędzie Swieckie, á przez sześć Osob Duchownych, do Spraw wszelákich od Stanu Duchownego wedle Konstytucyi wyłádzonych, z strony Dobr Dziedzicznych. Jednák ieżliby Osobá Duchowna inter litis comparticipes w spráwie tákiey naydowáłá się: tákowe spráwy sámo sæculare Judicium sądzić ma. Tákże y spráwy względem Dobr tákowych ktoreby się cum Personis sæculatibus sądzić záczęły, á potym lite pendente in Personas Spirituales, vel Successionis, vel assecuto Jure przypadły, sæculare Judicium sądzić ma sposobem niżey opisanym, ex regestro causarum sæcularium, fine rejectione: wyiąwszy y te dobrá, ktore per commutationem, ábo záprzedániem Dobr Kościelnych, do Kościołá przytączone są álbo będą, ktore dawnym prerogátywom Kościelnym podlegáią. A onera publica æquivalentia; ná Dobrách kommutowanych, y przedánych, zostáwáią. A insze spráwy ták Duchowne, iáko y Swieckie, in sæculari wedle Konstytucyi 78. odpráwowáć się máią. A in causis Granicierum, Plebani ktorzy nie máią Poddánych,

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

other burdens like the secular estate. Likewise, with respect to all injuries arising from the plots and estates mentioned: they shall be required to respond to the injured party as is appropriate in the land court and municipal court; and likewise when by way of appeals and remissions such cases shall fall to the Crown Tribunal, they shall be adjudicated by all the secular judges and by six ecclesiastical persons delegated to all cases by the ecclesiastical estate according to the constitution, from the side of the hereditary estates. However, if an ecclesiastical person should be found among the co-parties in such a case: such cases shall be adjudicated by the secular court alone. Likewise cases concerning such estates as began to be adjudicated *cum Personis sæcularibus* and thereafter *lite pendente* fell to ecclesiastical persons, whether by succession or upon the acquisition of a right — the secular court shall adjudicate in the manner described below, *ex regestro causarum sæcularium, fine rejectione*: excepting those estates which have been or shall be attached to the Church *per commutationem* or by the sale of ecclesiastical estates, which are subject to the ancient prerogatives of the Church. The *onera publica æquivalentia* remain upon the commuted and sold estates. Other cases — both ecclesiastical and secular — *in sæculari* shall be conducted according to the constitution of 1578. And in boundary cases, those parish priests who have no subjects

y folwárkow udzielonych, ále tylko z Dziesięcin, y z pensyi fundowáni są cum Heredibus przyjmieszywáni być nie máią, ále sámym Dziedzicom Spráwy tákowe należeć będą.

##### *Quæ Causæ Spiritualium, Juri Terrestri subsunt.*

^[*Herburt Fol. 452.*] Spirituales pro Bonis sive injurijs Bonorum, Juri communi subjectorum, Jure Terrestri experiantur, Injuriæ sunt super Limitibus Cmethonibus, Fugitivis, cæde & vulneribus, alias juxta Statuta Joannis Alberti Regis.

##### *Spráwy Sądowe gruntu się tyczące, personales y simplices y Dylácye veræ infirmitatis, & pro majori*

^[*Kon. 1611. Fol. 78. Tomo 3tio.*] W spráwách Sądowych ktore się Gruntu dotykáią, á zowią ie personales & simplices, żádne Dylácye być niemáią; okrom ieżliby przysięgá ^[*Dicatio concessa Infirmitatis.*] w tákowych y innszych Spráwách nástąpiłá, á ktoreykólwiek nákazána stronie byłá, tedy przez Posłańcá oney Ziemie osiádłego y umocowánego, Dylácya prawdziwey niemocy pozwolona być ma, ták Aktorowi, iáko pozwánemu do pierwszych Rokow: ná ktorych Rokách z záchowáłego Roku stroná, ktorey dylácya pozwolona będzie, ma sámotrzecia

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

and no separate farms, but are founded only on tithes and annuities, shall not be joined together with the heirs, but such cases shall belong to the heirs alone.

##### *Which Cases of Ecclesiastical Persons Fall Under the Terrestrial Law.*

^[*Herburt, fol. 452.*] Ecclesiastical persons, for estates or injuries to estates subject to the common law, shall proceed by the terrestrial law. The injuries are in respect of boundaries, peasants, fugitives, killing, and wounding — otherwise according to the statutes of King John Albert.

##### *Judicial Cases Touching upon Land, Personal and Simple, and Postponements of True Illness and for the Principal.*

^[*Constitution 1611, fol. 78, vol. 3.*] In judicial cases that concern land and are called personal and simple, no postponements shall be permitted — except that if ^[*A postponement of illness is granted.*] an oath in such and other cases falls upon someone and is ordered to a party, then through the court messenger of that land, a resident with full authority, a postponement of true illness shall be permitted, both to the plaintiff and to the respondent, until the next sittings. At which sittings, from the preserved session, the party to whom the postponement shall be granted shall

</div>
</section>

<section class="lit-passage" id="p-157">
<h2 class="lit-loc"><a href="#p-157">p. 157</a></h2>

przysiądz, iż chorá byłá prawdziwie; á po przysiądze do spráwy ma być przypuszczona.

Ma też Dylacya być dana pro Majori, tákże per^[*Ibidem Fol. 79.*] nuncium Possessionatum, cum Plenipotentia missum,^[*Dilatio pro Majori*] ieżli się Roki personales tráfią, w rożnych Sądziech: to iest, ieżliby komu przysięgá urosłá, tedy się odpráwić pierwey powinien, ktora o więkkszą Summę idzie, á tá ma być mnieysza prorogowána do Drugich Rokow: á potym ma tego dowieść ten co mu Dylacyą dano literis significatorijs onego Sądu, że tám prawdziwie miał Spráwę przysiężną, y postępek z ktorego tá Spráwá urosłá, áby dáremnie Aktor ludyfikowány nie był, y Damnifikowány sub amissione causæ.

Tego dokłádáiąc, ieżli w krzywdách y rożnicách ^[*Ibidem Fol. 79.*] gruntownych, ktore między Sąsiády zaydą, gránic ^[*Non cognita Causa Granicierum Causa facti decidi non potest.*] nie będzie uczynionych, á o gránice ieden drugiego, bądź krzywdy uchodząc, bądź pokoiu sobie żyeząc, zápozwie: tedy Spráwa Graniczna wprzod ma być rozsądzona, á potym krzywdy uznawane. Jednák bez zwłoki ma te Gránice kontynuowáć, alias ieżli Ich popierać nie będzie stroná, ákcye ktore o krzywdy y szkody będą instytuowane, máią mieć wolny swoy regres do prawá, bez wszelákiey kontrádykcyi y impedymentu práwnego.

##### *Suspensa Spraw, Senatorom, Komisarzom, y Posłom, currere powinna,*

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

swear as a third, that it was truly ill; and after the oath it shall be admitted to the case.

A postponement shall also be given for the principal ^[*Ibid., fol. 79.*] likewise through ^[*A postponement for the principal.*] a possessory deputy sent with full authority, if personal sessions fall in different courts — that is, if an oath has arisen for someone, he is required to discharge it first in the case that involves the greater sum, and that one shall be postponed as the lesser to the next sittings; and thereafter he shall prove this to the one to whom the postponement was given by the signatory letters of that court, that he truly had a case under oath there, and a proceeding from which that case arose — so that the plaintiff may not be mocked and damaged in vain *sub amissione causæ*.

Adding to this: if in injuries and land differences ^[*Ibid., fol. 79.*] that arise between neighbours ^[*A boundary case cannot be decided without cognisance of it.*] there are no boundaries made, and one summons another either to escape injuries or wishing himself peace — then the boundary case shall first be adjudicated, and then the injuries considered. However, without delay the boundaries are to be continued; otherwise, if the party shall not pursue them, the actions brought for injuries and losses shall have free recourse to the law, without any opposition or legal impediment.

##### *The suspension of cases is required to run for senators, commissioners, and deputies.*

</div>
</section>

##### *Ubespieczenienie Pánow Senatorow, y Posłow Ziemskich.*

^[*Kon. 1659. Fol. 591. Tomo 4to.*] Reasumuiąc in toto Konstytucye Seymow blisko przyszłych, o ubespieczeńiu PP. Senatorow, Urzędnikow Koronnych; Komisarzow, Posłow do Pánow Postronnych, y Posłow Ziemskich ac eorum pupillorum, authoritate præsentis Conventus deklaruiemy, iż wszelákie Dekreta, Kondemnaty ktorekolwiekby lubo per controversias, lubo in contumaciam od Seymiku obieránia Posłow ná ktorymkolwiek z Pánow Senatorow, y Posłow, Ziemskich, ná Seym obránych, in quocunque; Judicio majoris, & minoris subsellii stáneły, y do Seymikow Relationum stawáć będą. Tákowe wszystkie Dekreta, y Kondemnaty kasuiemy. Po Seymiku iednak relationis, in Duabus Septimanis, każdy z Pánow Senatorow, y Posłow, in quocunque; Judicio spráwować się ad instantiam cujusvis będzie powinien. A żeby in posterum żadney w tym wątpliwości nie było, authoritate præsentis Conventus to Práwo pro perpetua lege deklaruiemy. Co tákże Posłom do Postronnych Pánow, Rezydentom, ábo Komisarzom post expeditam functionem służyć ma.

##### *O Bespieczeństwie WW. Senatorow y Urodzonych Posłow Ziemskich, ták Korony; iáko y W. X. L.*

^[*Kon. 1661. Fol. 721. Tomo 4to.*] Przychyląiąc się do Konstytucyi Anni 1659. o ubespieczeńiu WW. Senatorow y Urodzonych

</div>
<div class="lit-en" lang="en" markdown="1">

##### *Security of the Lord Senators and Land Deputies.*

^[*Constitution 1659, fol. 591, vol. 4.*] Reassuming in full the constitutions of the most recent Sejms concerning the security of the lord senators, Crown officials, commissioners, deputies to foreign lords, and land deputies, and their wards — by the authority of the present convention We declare that all decrees and condemnations that shall have stood against any of the lord senators and land deputies elected to the Sejm, whether by contest or in contumacy, at whatever election sitting, in whatever court of the greater or lesser tribunal, and that shall be standing until the relation sittings — all such decrees and condemnations We annul. After the relation sitting, however, within two weeks each of the lord senators and deputies shall be required to conduct himself *in quocunque Judicio* at the instance of any person. And so that there may be no doubt about this in the future, by the authority of the present convention We declare this law to be a perpetual law. Which shall also serve for deputies to foreign lords, residents, or commissioners *post expeditam functionem*.

##### *On the Security of the Right Honourable Senators and Honourable Land Deputies, both of the Crown and of the Grand Duchy of Lithuania.*

^[*Constitution 1661, fol. 721, vol. 4.*] Inclining ourselves to the constitution of the year 1659 on the security of the Right Honourable Senators and Honourable

</div>
</section>
